# EU5 Knowledge Brief

> Last updated: 2026-10-24

## Project Identity

- Mod name: **Standard of Living (SOL)**
- Mod ID: `hades.sol` | Version: `261023` | Target: EU5 `1.4.*` beta
- Release versions use `YYMMDD`; `build.bat` stamps the selected target from the local date while `supported_game_version` remains independent
- Requires Community Mod Framework v2.*; SOL owns a unified Glorp UI-based location window rather than declaring Glorp UI or Construction Manager as metadata dependencies. The copied file retains optional `cm_*` references so Construction Manager can supply its definitions when enabled.
- Active deploy targets: `src/stable/`, `src/sol_standalone/`, `src/sol_pp_compatibility_submod/`, `src/sol_mnt_compatibility_submod/`, and `src/sol_jtg_compatibility_submod/`

## Branch Boundary (2026-08-09)

`main` is the reference for the shipped location-level income=expense system:
one monthly pass computes each location's raw coefficient, applies the legacy
country poverty factor, and writes the location modifier. It has no country
matrix or KKT solver. `beta` is a development branch for the not-yet-shipped
country-level system. Its country classifier, matrix aggregation, exact
diagnostic, prefilters, approximation candidates, gate, and final coefficient
application are beta-only work and must not be described as part of the stable
location-level algorithm. The four-way equal-capacity (`country_total / 4`)
classifier is retired; the beta classifier uses a 1% per-class anchor and
negative-pressure capacity allocation instead. See
`docs/technical/SOL_Country_Level_Strata_Demand_Model.md` for the module and
performance boundary.

## Core Features

**SOL situation visibility** - The situation is hidden from every country in both stable and standalone (`visible = { always = no }`). Its definition, panel assets, start/end conditions and monthly calculations remain intact; market caches, country display refreshes and annual rankings continue through the original situation lifecycle.

1. **Standard of Living (SOL) System** *(primary feature)* - Recalibrates baseline `demand_add` for 56 goods in `z_SOL_pop_goods.txt` and includes the 1.4 `camels` good in market accounting, disables the engine-wide development demand multiplier, fully negates all nine vanilla per-good development thresholds, and sets `NPop.POP_NEEDS_INCOME_SCALE = 1` so strata pay the nominal cost of purchased goods instead of vanilla's 33% surcharge. It applies a monthly country-closed `local_pop_demand` coefficient after raw location spending, class assignment, and matrix aggregation. The CMF Performance tab's Country-level Solver group independently controls the exact solve and the approximation solve for each of AI and human countries; all four default ON. A side whose two toggles are both off skips country-level aggregation entirely and uses a raw-only baseline that omits classification, ordering, matrix construction, approximation prefiltering, solver bookkeeping, and human solver logs while preserving raw location demand and modifier writes. Approximate candidates are accepted only when neither scaled metric worsens against raw; otherwise `raw_fallback` preserves raw factors. Locations are classified by nobles, clergy, burghers, or lower-strata relative over-representation, with lower = commoners + tribesmen; class matrices remain raw-weighted so within-class local variation is preserved. The CMF in-game log is deliberately minimal: one human-only row per country solve naming the adopted strategy (exact, strict no-worsen vertex sweep, or raw fallback), with no dynamic values and no per-class/matrix/candidate rows. The system feeds the Living Standard situation panel, map data, mapmode, and location GUI. Demand follows purchasing power only (liquid funds / baseline spending); living standard is a separate derived layer (feature 16).

**Income-funded goods demand** - Slaves have no income and no commodity demand in stable, standalone, PP, M&T, or JTG combinations. Generators cancel resolved native slave demand, including generic `all` demand and native slave multipliers; the seven income-bearing baskets and solver inputs remain separate. Native population food consumption is unchanged. `test_sol_slave_demand.py` resolves all goods across the five targets and checks income-bearing quantities.

**Purchasing-power basket calibration** - The baseline uses the approved rounded 1340 country-median calibration: nobles x1, clergy x2, commoners (laborers/peasants/soldiers) x4, and burghers approximately x0.6555 with one or two significant digits per good. Tiny masonry targets use the nearest emittable vanilla-net quantity. PP extra victuals and the rounded JTG extra-good targets follow the same rebase; M&T inherits the calibrated costs through its price conversion. This changes actual baseline goods demand and internal accounting, without adding a separate GUI normalization multiplier.

**Country-level nonnegative demand approximation** - A size prefilter in `sol_solve_country_pop_demand` routes any country with `num_locations < 5` straight to the raw-only path, skipping classification and the solver entirely; below four locations the pigeonhole principle caps the matrix at three non-empty columns, and measured gains are marginal (median scaled L1 for a single-location country moves 0.329 to 0.310, versus 0.371 to 0.222 from three locations up), so skipping them drops 70% of the exact-failed country count for 17% of the owned-location traversal. It is checked before anything else because `num_locations` is known up front while the non-empty class count only exists after the confidence-ordered classification pass has been paid for. Past that gate, stable and standalone enter the country solver body only when the matching CMF preparation toggle is enabled. Inside that dependency, exact runs first when enabled; exact success is adopted directly as strategy id 7. Exact failures run a single strict no-worsen vertex sweep (`sol_country_demand_run_vertex_selector`, strategy id 8) for both AI and human countries. The sweep minimises mean relative deviation (scaled L1 over the four stratum rows) with worst-stratum relative deviation (scaled L-infinity) as a 1% tie-break, under the hard constraint that neither metric may worsen against raw; the incumbent is seeded with raw itself so a fallback always exists. These are the only two live strategies: the four L2 variants, `minimax_ratio`, and AI `proportional_fast` are all retired. The L2 variants and minimax optimised neither declared objective and were beaten on scaled L1 in 91.1% of exact-failed countries (median +17.11pp, zero regressions); `proportional_fast` adopted a candidate for only 4.2% of eligible countries against the sweep's 90.7% on the same save, with median scaled L1 barely off raw (0.335 vs raw 0.343, sweep 0.114), so both sides were merged onto the single shared path. Vertex enumeration covers 209 (exact-row-order, free-column) combinations, which offline replay confirms matches partial pivoting exactly, and shrank the solver file by 87%. A raw-error prefilter (`sol_country_demand_gate_possible`) skips the sweep entirely when every raw row is already inside tolerance; when the sweep finds no admissible vertex, factors remain 1 and locations use `raw_fallback`. Runtime variables are limited to state needed by solving, strategy selection, and factor application; obsolete Log/offline-only best-candidate, failure-detail, final-error, candidate-count, and residual caches are not retained. Compatibility submods inherit this base effect and keep their local `REPLACE` overrides.

2. **Economic Balance & Anti-Snowballing** - Base tax efficiency defaults to -15% and is CMF-adjustable. Age escalation raises construction, RGO expansion, city/town upgrade, and food-consumption pressure across ages; by Age 6 it reaches -100% global building efficiency, +100% RGO expansion cost, +50% city/town upgrade cost, and +50% pop food consumption. Base RGO size is halved to 1, total-population RGO scaling is reduced, low-control locations receive a construction-efficiency penalty, and road prices are increased to gravel x2, paved x2, modern x3, railroad x5.

3. **Tax, Prices & Development** - Difficulty tax bonuses are halved for AI Hard/Very Hard and Player Easy/Very Easy. Diplomatic spending changes with used diplomatic capacity, cultural tradition reduces stability investment cost, and gold-transfer/action prices are rebalanced. GDP-to-development applies a yearly local development modifier scaled by local GDP, and AI countries can receive a small configurable devastation-recovery bonus.

4. **War & Combat (Harsher Wars)** - War exhaustion further reduces levy size, control, stability, legitimacy, and defensive capability. Total occupation adds +1.6 monthly war exhaustion on top of vanilla, and capital occupation adds +0.2 monthly war exhaustion on top of vanilla. The reduce-war-exhaustion cabinet action can be restricted to peacetime. Blockade, siege, occupation, hostile troops, looting, raiding, and razing modifiers are harsher for prosperity, control, construction, migration attraction, development, raw materials, and food.

5. **Food, Climate & Prosperity** - Normal winters and raised levies create harsher local food pressure, while Little Ice Age event penalties are softened to avoid runaway food-crisis spirals. Age escalation starts with mild prosperity scarcity and later returns prosperity in the late game.

6. **Colonial & Diplomatic Restrictions** - AI generally requires more than 500 tax base to create colonial charters; historical colonizers are exempt, and colonial nations can colonize only in their capital region. The player is not restricted by this rule. In the 1.4 beta reference the scripted create_colonial_charter action has disappeared; these restrictions still live in the old override and require migration/verification against the new creation path before they can be claimed to work. Asking another country to join a war costs 30 favors.

7. **CMF Settings & Migration Support** - Two CMF tabs. The Features tab registers SOL pop demand, map coloring, economic balance, base tax efficiency, age escalation, stability discount, diplomatic spending, difficulty tax nerf, GDP-to-development, AI prosperity recovery, and war acceleration sub-features. The Performance tab holds a Calculation Frequency group containing the default-on AI reduced-frequency toggle, followed by the four default-on exact/approximation toggles. Humans solve monthly. Reduced-frequency AI solves on its own yearly anniversary without monthly territory/market/goods snapshot polling or dirty-triggered solves; explicitly disabling the full mod's frequency toggle restores monthly AI solving. Standalone uses human-monthly/AI-yearly scheduling without CMF. Both targets still apply the one-month location modifier every month. New countries without a solve status initialize once; an acquired/settled location with no cached modifier size gets neutral size 0 inside the existing apply traversal and waits for the next scheduled solve, while existing coefficients are preserved. Start/load and explicit SOL/substitution setting callbacks retain their immediate refresh/cleanup behavior; ordinary AI economic and ownership changes deliberately wait for its anniversary. Built-in after-lobby migration logic notifies human players when loading older saves.

8. **Prosper or Perish Compact Compatibility Submod** - A separate final-loading target keeps full PP while narrowing full SOL to its income-based demand/UI, compact age construction and city/town costs, low-control construction penalty, base tax/diplomatic/stability rules, difficulty tax adjustments, configurable AI devastation recovery, non-conflicting prices, and selected war/expansion rules. It removes SOL residuals from PP-owned weather, Little Ice Age, base location, blockade, siege, occupation, and looting values; lets PP's later same-operation price file naturally replace SOL's repeated road-price fields; keeps SOL GDP-to-development disabled by default but exposes a compatibility-specific CMF opt-in; excludes non-compact age fields; preserves PP's zero lumber demand; and adds PP `victuals` to SOL calculations and both goods panels. Compatibility `demand_add` sets rounded Compact `victuals` net-demand targets of 0.05/0.03/0.023/0.0016/0.0008/0.0016 for nobles/clergy/burghers/laborers/peasants/soldiers, chosen from the PP-only spending-ratio comparison. Stable and standalone contain no PP-load detection or PP-only UI/value data.

9. **Just Trade Goods Compact Compatibility Submod** - A separate final-loading target applies SOL-style per-stratum scaling to the 26 direct-demand goods from Just Goods, including the clocks goods added by the merged release. The legacy seed multipliers are 1.05/0.45/1.75/0.02/0.10/0.025/0 for nobles/clergy/burghers/laborers/peasants/soldiers/tribesmen; scaled targets use clean 0.01/0.001/0.0001/0.00001 steps by magnitude, all 26 source wealth gates, all 15 source development gates, and all recorded continent/sub-continent demand modifiers are fully negated, and slave demand is fully cancelled because slaves have no income. It adds final JTG quantities to SOL market and country accounting and rebuilds both goods panels with all 82 demand goods using the JTG DDS icons. The merged Just Goods mod is required, and this target is mutually exclusive with the SOL-PP compatibility target because both replace the same two SOL effects.

10. **MEIOU and Taxes Compatibility Submod** - A separate final-loading target makes M&T authoritative for economic balance by default while retaining SOL demand, Living Standard UI/map, migration, and non-conflicting war/colonial rules. It rebuilds all 56 SOL goods from complete M&T objects where available and current vanilla fallback objects for new goods such as camels, inversely scales the seven SOL pop quantities against M&T prices, equalizes maize/millet/rice, keeps tools laborer demand at 0.002, sets M&T slave demand to zero, and removes wealth/development gates. Its marked EPBM replacement leaves M&T country charges unchanged while caching attributable domestic maintenance on SOL's five location variables; foreign, crown, dhimmi, and Gaelic-tribes attribution is excluded by policy. A compatibility-specific CMF economic master defaults off and can explicitly re-enable SOL's trigger-gated base-tax, age-escalation, stability, diplomatic-spending, difficulty, GDP-to-development, and AI-recovery settings; dynamic values and callbacks remain available for that opt-in, while M&T continues to own goods, prices, and economic static modifiers.

11. **Unified Glorp UI Location Window** - Full SOL uses the complete current Glorp UI location-window file as its merge base, preserves its 1.4 action-button and road-builder APIs (and migrates older snapshots when needed), preserves Glorp UI and optional Construction Manager references, and inserts one Living Standard tooltip button. SOL Standalone remains vanilla-based, and the M&T target migrates its own final window. Missing `cm_*` definitions with SOL alone are expected optional-dependency logs; enabling Construction Manager supplies them without changing the unified file.

12. **Unified SOL Economy UI** - The location tooltip and country panel use sheet C purchasing-power rows: a centred signed -100%..+200% bar uses separate linear scales on either side of the 0% baseline, alongside right-aligned unclamped raw alpha-minus-one percentages and a smaller gray funds / baseline pair with bilingual explanatory hover. One shared ruler above the estate rows shows equidistant -100%, -50%, 0%, +100%, and +200% labels; each row retains short ticks and a thicker fixed zero line. Zero funds reaches -100%; three times baseline reaches +200%. The total row has a faint gold background and outline with a thicker bar; five compact 42-unit estate rows use subdued backgrounds and smaller percentages; dials, population/income distribution charts, share tracks and ratios are temporarily removed by user decision. Solver beta is absent from SOL GUI; the location modifier retains the applied result. Goods retain F's required/daily/luxury tiers and all 67 memberships across 20 groups, with fixed cards, supplied/total counts, gold supply ratios, mutually exclusive full/dim goods images and a seven-level native substitution-icon row beneath each grouped good. PP/JTG regenerate the same shared layout with neutral extra goods; M&T inherits the base UI. Basket-cell hover retains country/owner lifecycle and location-price context. Actual goods tooltips use sheet C tier/group rows with every member's icon/name and seven price preferences; only market goods tooltips add the current market/base-price ratio and a gold highlight for the current tier. Ungrouped, unknown/default and empty goods keys hide the substitution section. All vanilla statistics are preserved.

13. **Country goods substitution** - Algorithm A with price-only preferences retains 20 groups, 56 core goods and 67 overlapping memberships. Explicit seven-stratum subscriptions in `pop_group_baskets.csv` require whole groups, with positive calibrated quantities for every subscribed member and no demand outside the subscribed union; generators fail on incomplete baskets. Baskets evaluate once per country market/pop type after aggregating owned population, and overlaps include only subscribed groups; seven country-adjusted market spending totals feed each location and the existing SOL solver. The 56 country auto modifier definitions only read guarded nonzero per-good deltas cached by the scheduled monthly/yearly solve; zero caches are removed after spending/summary reads, and bilingual AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_/DESC_ keys provide names and descriptions; basket and external-offset calculations never run in auto scales. The default-on CMF switch clears caches when disabled; standalone enables substitution without CMF. PP uniformly removes lumber from groups and keeps victuals neutral, M&T preserves positive member demand after price scaling, and the 26 JTG extras remain neutral. Source generation and offline regressions cover whole-group consumption and commoner condiments; EU5 demand refresh, fixed-point rounding and actual month timing still require in-game acceptance.

14. **Living Standard four-section left-panel HUD** - Stable and standalone ship a 570×36 row below the nine left-panel buttons, split into living standard/YoY, purchasing power, discounted goods/substitution, and world-ranking sections with independent contextual tooltips. Current SOL appears on the first solve; its annual change uses a new same-month history namespace that cannot read legacy income samples. National income aggregation is removed, and population weights share the SOL accumulator. The tier medallion and its tier-only title compare country living standard with the population-weighted world average (below 75% low, above 133% high), and the world ranking orders countries and estates by living standard (single-location countries are excluded from the ranking, world average and estate ranks but still get a tier; the HUD shows them as unranked); the ranking tooltip exposes generated per-estate world rank, percentile, count, and top-country caches, showing living-standard amounts. Section 1 and its four-column tooltip show SOL with one decimal; tooltip SOL amounts carry a dedicated house unit icon. Section 2 and its tooltip remain raw purchasing power. Section 3 shows up to six goods whose active country substitution multiplier is greater than 1 (at least the first positive weighting tier), in fixed goods order. The existing country substitution refresh selects and caches six localized icon flags; the persistent HUD only reads six slots and one status value, with no per-frame goods filtering or prefix counting. Cache rebuilds/cleanup remove stale slots, fewer matches show fewer icons, and inactive/no-match states stay empty; the tooltip retains goods groups without the unimplemented spending ranking. An independent Glorp UI shim shifts its own stats bar and collapse toggle down one row; offline rendering covers both languages, pending/ranking states, and Glorp UI, while in-game hover, click pass-through, scaling, and actual load order remain to be confirmed.

15. **SOL map modes and HUD navigation** - The four HUD sections preview their corresponding map on hover and select it on left click: local living standard (annual liquid funds per thousand with flat/percent SOL bonuses; red at 0, yellow at world average, green at 3× world average, fallback reference 6), local raw purchasing power, country goods substitution in each country's own color, and overall world ranking from red to green, with single-location (unranked) countries striped via `secondary_map_color`. Country map tooltips reuse sections three/four with a preserved hovered-country scope, including nested ranking peers; the two local maps are independent of the country solver's applied modifier. Both targets ship the map menu icons and bilingual labels. Offline scope tests and renders cover the bindings; engine hover, selection and country aggregation still need in-game acceptance.

16. **Living standard vs purchasing power** - Purchasing power (liquid funds / baseline spending) alone drives demand. SOL = flat bonuses + max(0, 1 + summed percentage bonuses) × annual liquid funds per thousand people, floored at zero per estate, population weighted and displayed as an amount. Two native location modifier types feed flat/percent hooks shared by all five estates; 17 building injections add healthcare/clean-water bonuses, pollution penalties and theater/opera/library percentages, scaled by native building level and goods access. Types, existing SOL icon mappings and bilingual formula descriptions are generated for stable/standalone and inherited by PP/M&T/JTG. The existing demand/solver passes cache and aggregate SOL without another traversal; zero-baseline populations still participate. SOL affects migration attraction, population growth, tiers, rankings and living-standard maps, while purchasing-power surfaces retain their values. War, disease, institutions and estate-specific native modifier types remain deferred. `test_sol_living_standard.py` checks formulas, floors, sources, population weighting and demand isolation across all five targets; native source tooltips and scaling await in-game acceptance. See `docs/technical/SOL_Living_Standard_Modifier_Sources_Design_20261023.md`.

17. **Local SOL gameplay feedback** - The existing monthly modifier pass reads cached population-weighted local SOL and grants migration attraction of `clamp(0.02 * SOL, 0, 0.5)`. Local population growth changes by -0.1 percentage points below 0.1 SOL or +0.1 percentage points above 10 SOL; both exact boundaries and the middle range are neutral. One-month static modifiers replace rather than stack, missing SOL clears feedback, and disabling SOL clears it through the base/PP/M&T callbacks and monthly cleanup. Human-monthly/AI-yearly SOL computation cadence is preserved without another location traversal; standalone and compatibility targets inherit the mechanic.

## Script Reference

| Script | When to run | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `update_workshop_tags.py` | SOL launcher for `.eu5-base/scripts/update_workshop_tags.py`; explicit `--item-id`, default stable metadata, optional `--metadata` override | Shared implementation owns Steam tag updates and verification; offline preview by default, `--check` reads Steam, `--apply` replaces tags; local usage in `docs/guides/Workshop_Tags.md` |
| `update_mod_version.py` | Automatically before every `build.bat` deployment; may also be run manually with `--target` and `--date` | Selected target metadata `version` fields in `YYMMDD` form |
| `gen_sol_chain.py` | Preferred one-shot SOL generation entry; `build.bat` runs it automatically for the selected target | Active SOL generated files for all five deploy targets |
| `build.bat` | Build/deploy selected targets; use `--validate-only` for generation plus full source validation | Uses the explicit EU5_PYTHON interpreter or resolves the eu5 environment, prints the interpreter and validator paths, and retains tmp/build_validate.log. Normal deployment validates changed files before BOM normalization and mirroring; --validate-only checks all sources and exits before those steps |
| `gen_sol_messages.py` | After SOL resolution message changes; called by gen_sol_chain.py; supports --check | Additive main_menu/gui/messagetypes/SOL_messages.txt for stable/standalone with only SOL's recalculation type; checks every vanilla message file for id collisions and rejects obsolete gui/messagetypes.txt; update mode removes only its own legacy generated file |
| `gen_sol_pp_compat.py` | After changing SOL balance/demand/effects/UI or updating the PP reference; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Generated compact feature cleanup plus lumber correction, fixed rounded victuals demand/accounting, effect/GUI overrides, and the default-off PP GDP-to-development CMF opt-in with localization; also verifies that PP's road-price file still wins by same-operation filename order |
| `gen_sol_mnt_compat.py` | After changing SOL demand/effects/UI or updating the M&T reference; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Generated M&T-based goods, unit-consumption values, economic authority, EPBM location caches, CMF/action overrides, merged location window, and the default-off SOL economic opt-in settings/value callbacks with localization in `src/sol_mnt_compatibility_submod/` |
| `gen_sol_jtg_compat.py` | After changing `jtg_pop_demand.csv`, SOL demand/effects/UI, or updating the Just Goods Workshop reference; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Generated JTG demand corrections, unit-consumption values, two effect replacements, and both 82-good GUI overrides in `src/sol_jtg_compatibility_submod/` |
| `sync_location_window.py` | After updating the Glorp UI reference; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` for `stable`/`all` | Unified Glorp UI-based stable location window with optional Construction Manager references, 1.4 button/road-builder migration, one SOL tooltip button, and migrated extracted vanilla type support |
| `gen_sol_economy_effects.py` | After changing SOL economy emitters, the country solver, building sources or demand data; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` for `stable`/`sol_standalone`/`all` | Emits economy/solver effects, annual SOL values, flat/percent modifier types, icons, 17 building injections, local migration/growth static modifiers and bilingual names for stable and standalone |
| `gen_sol_ls_gui.py` | After changing the SOL economy UI design, the shared kit, the estate/goods vocabulary, or `data/goods_weights.csv`; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Rebuilds the location tooltip and country situation panel for stable/standalone, plus `SOL_ls_ui_l_*.yml`; copies stable's `SOL_living_standard_kit.gui` to standalone and includes that copy in its generated-output check |
| `gen_sol_lshud.py` | After changing the four-section design, translations, SOL history/ranking/map sources or Glorp UI types; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Stable/standalone HUD, Glorp shim, four map modes, scoped tooltips, map icons, bilingual localization and ranking caches; `--check` verifies all outputs |
| `sol_lshud_income_source.py` / `sol_lshud_ranking_source.py` | Imported by the economy and HUD generators | SOL-only same-month annual histories and trend classes; population accumulation belongs to the shared SOL accumulator; annual country-only living-standard ranking with a population-weighted world average, tiers, top-ten and neighbour slots |
| `sol_living_standard_source.py` | Imported by `gen_sol_economy_effects.py`; edit to change SOL sources, modifier hooks or gameplay feedback | Annual population-weighted SOL, flat/percent hooks and native types, 17 building sources, icons, numeric display values, migration/growth feedback effects and bilingual names |
| `test_sol_living_standard.py` | After changing demand, SOL, feedback, ranking or map code | Executes annual SOL/population weighting and feedback thresholds, transitions and cleanup; checks all five targets for demand isolation, basket independence and numeric SOL display |
| `test_sol_lshud_income.py` / `test_sol_lshud_ranking.py` / `test_sol_lshud_ui.py` | After changing HUD data, sorting or presentation | Executes generated SOL history/ranking effects and renders the row/tooltip in both languages/targets plus Glorp UI bar/toggle composition; `SOL_LSHUD_PREVIEW_OUT` saves review images |
| `gen_sol_goods_tooltip.py` | After changing `data/goods_weights.csv` or vanilla goods_details/market_tooltips; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Generates sheet C group icon/name rows and seven static price preferences in goods_details, plus a full preserved market_tooltips override supplying the live market/base-price ratio and gold current-tier highlight only for SpecificGoodsMarket_tooltip. Emits bilingual membership/group/price localization and location-market snippets |
| `sol_goods_order.py` | Imported by the two scripts above and by both compatibility generators; not run directly | `ordered()` orders unique goods by primary group; `group_rows()` preserves every CSV membership in labelled rows, with neutral ungrouped compatibility goods last |
| `sol_goods_ui.py` | Imported by the three GUI generators; not run directly | Shared goods-block renderer (`render_goods_vbox` / `replace_goods_vbox`), group labels, mutually exclusive supply icons, country +/- markers, contextual hover and lifecycle summary. Base and compatibility generators rebuild the same block through it |
| `test_sol_slave_demand.py` | After goods, demand generator, or compatibility changes | Resolves all five targets to verify zero slave commodity demand, PP lumber stacking, JTG source audit and existing income-bearing quantities |
| `gen_pop_goods.py` | After editing `target_demand.csv` | `src/<target>/in_game/common/goods/z_SOL_pop_goods.txt` (calibrated demand_add baseline, zero slave demand and complete vanilla development-threshold negations; supports `--target stable\|sol_standalone\|all`) |
| `gen_whole_group_baskets.py` | `--check` runs before generation; `--fill` performs one-time migration | Owns subscription loading and whole-group/net-target validation; fills missing members within existing allocated group budgets and rounds connected groups together, preserving positive minima and emittable INJECT deltas |
| `gen_demand_csv.py` | After demand calibration | `data/demand_price_table.csv` |
| `gen_market_unit_consumption.py` | After `gen_demand_csv.py`, or after changing SOL market base-spending logic | `src/<target>/in_game/common/script_values/SOL_market_unit_consumption_values.txt` (supports `--target stable\|sol_standalone\|all`); `gen_sol_economy_effects.py` embeds the matching `sol_refresh_market_pop_demand_maps` block |
| `gen_sol_substitution.py` | After changing memberships, historical sizes, or country substitution runtime; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` | Stable/standalone country effects, guarded cache/display values, enable triggers, and 56 country auto modifiers; supports `--check` |
| `sol_substitution.py` | Imported by base and compatibility generators | Shared algorithm A oracle and emitters for market details, country aggregation/lifecycle and location spending |
| `test_sol_substitution.py` | After changing substitution, spending or lifecycle | Executes generated effects and guarded display values in an offline float scope fixture; checks tiers, overlaps, external offsets, cleanup, periodic cache refresh, UI lifecycle and PP fallback without emulating EU5 fixed-point or engine demand |
| `test_sol_spending_cache.py` | After changing country/market spending caches | Verifies seven cached totals, shared-market owner isolation, country/world refresh and cleanup, missing scope/maps and PP/JTG/M&T calibrated quantities |
| test_sol_economy_cadence.py | After changing scheduling or location application | Executes stable/standalone pulse and apply control flow with solver/native-write boundary stubs; verifies monthly humans, yearly AI, no snapshot polling, new-country initialization, missing-location fallback, control changes and explicit monthly-AI override |
| `test_sol_substitution_performance.py` | After changing substitution aggregation or modifier lifecycle | Compares multi-market aggregation to the independent oracle; verifies high-frequency cache-only auto evaluation without economic reads or writes, no static grants, periodic external updates, toggles and cleanup |
| `measure_sol_substitution_performance.py` | When auditing substitution operation costs | Compares a Git baseline with current generated runtime using shared-context float fixtures; writes script operation/read/write counts and explicit engine/fixed-point limitations |
| `report_sol_substitution_perf.py` | After recording one or two uncompressed debug saves with substitution enabled | Country-only mmap report of refresh coverage, current strategy-8 cache share, or counter deltas (including historical dirty causes); reads the current sol_sub_perf_human flag and top 20 grouped by AI/human/unknown control; separates reset/new/cleared records and supports JSON output |
| `test_report_sol_substitution_perf.py` | After changing the diagnostic save reader | Verifies country-only reads, signed/zero fixed point, coverage, ordered-save comparison and counter resets |
| `test_sol_substitution_ui.py` | After changing goods UI or contextual localization; requires installed game assets | Checks all memberships, supply pixels, markers, localized hover, missing-cache/lifecycle states and shipped stable/standalone/PP/JTG tooltip bounds with mock values. `SOL_UI_PREVIEW_OUT` optionally saves bilingual previews; does not test engine hover |
| `generate_sol_location_window.py` | After vanilla `location_window.gui` changes; called by `gen_sol_chain.py` for `sol_standalone`/`all` | `src/sol_standalone/in_game/gui/location_window.gui` |
| `gen_brief.py` | After editing `*.yaml` or `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` | `docs/knowledge/BRIEF.md` (also calls `gen_index.py` automatically) |
| `gen_index.py` | Called by `gen_brief.py`; or run manually after structural changes | `data/index/` symbol indexes (icons, triggers, effects, modifiers, loc keys) |
| `gen_scaffold.py` | When creating a new EU5 file (event, effect, trigger, modifier, etc.) | Syntactically valid skeleton file with TODO markers; location static-modifier scaffolds emit delta-only `TRY_INJECT` blocks |
| `sync_reference.py` | After EU5 game updates to a new version | Mirrors all vanilla game subdirectories except mod/, matching eu5-towards-victory: skips gfx/binary files and other locales, preserves non-language localization containers and text formats, applies 10 MB file / 30 MB directory caps; --source is the install root containing game/; --dry-run previews added/removed paths |
| `validate.py` | Before launching game (`--changed` checks changed files under `src/`); `--ai-report` for JSON output | Checks anti-patterns, enums, modifiers, SOL baseline and map writes. Comment masking and the shared parser distinguish direct invalid fields from nested script values and vanilla-goods overrides; custom IO icon checks inspect actual definitions and declared local dependency assets. Real errors fail, generated-output staging reminders remain nonfatal in both report modes |
| `test_validate.py` | After changing validation rules, reporting or build exit codes | Verifies text/JSON exit-code parity, comment and line handling, nested vs direct variable fields, vanilla vs custom goods colors, and missing vs available custom IO assets. Windows batch integration checks the selected interpreter, retained log and success/failure status without reaching deployment |
| `generate_sol_icon.py` | Legacy/optional (the current icon is hand-drawn, see `render_sol_icon.py`). After setting an Images API relay key: `--all-candidates` / `--candidate NAME` for preview PNGs only, or `--convert-existing-png` for a chosen source; add `--overwrite` to refresh existing DDS outputs | Vanilla-style SOL icon: generated on a flat magenta key with vanilla map-mode icons as style references, keyed to a transparent background and fitted with a ~6px margin via `icon_keying.py`. Candidate previews go to `data/generated_icons/candidates/`; DDS outputs are the four shared `stable` icon paths, plus PNG/metadata under `data/generated_icons/`; uses `dds_image_lib.py` |
| `render_sol_icon.py` | After editing `assets/icons/sol_living_standard.svg` (needs cairosvg + Pillow) | Renders the hand-drawn SOL icon (terracotta bowl of bread, cheese and apples) to the vanilla-style 128x128 transparent `assets/icons/sol_living_standard.png` (soft darker edge, 6px margin); also renders the 64x64 inline text icon `assets/icons/sol_living_standard_texticon.png` from the same SVG minus `class="detail"` elements (`gen_sol_lshud.py` encodes it to `sol_proto/sol_living_standard_texticon.dds` for `@sol_living_standard!`); install it with `compose_sol_mapmode_icons.py --base assets/icons/sol_living_standard.png --overwrite`. This is the current source of the SOL icon set |
| `compose_sol_mapmode_icons.py` | `--base <png> [--overwrite] [--preview-only]` after picking the base icon | Installs the base icon to all four shared paths and builds the `sol_purchasing_power` (coins), `sol_ranking` (up arrow) and `sol_substitution` (exchange arrows) map-mode variants from the vanilla-derived badges in `data/generated_icons/_vanilla_badges/`, for both `stable` and `sol_standalone`. `gen_sol_lshud.py` calls its `variant_dds()` so regenerating map modes keeps the badges |
| `icon_keying.py` | Imported helper | Pure-Python chroma key (alpha from key-ness, de-spill), subject trim/fit with area-average downscaling, and alpha compositing for icon pipelines |

The 1.3 SOL demand runtime no longer uses `gen_scarcity.py`, `gen_sol_ui.py`, `engel_export.py`, or `gen_goods_demand_overrides.py`; those tools are retained only as legacy/reference helpers unless explicitly revived.

## Tool Reference

| Tool | When to run | Output |
|---|---|---|
| `ai_gui_assessment/renderer/run.py situation\|location\|tooltip\|goods_tooltip\|event\|market\|government\|battle` | Before launching the game after changing SOL GUI, or when checking how a `.gui` layout will look | Reads the installed game's `.gui` files with SOL `src/stable` overrides, DDS textures, Noto fonts and localization, and renders a mock-data screen plus an element tree at 1080p. Parts of the SOL situation panel and location window match in-game screenshots; the SOL tooltip frame width matches its screenshot. `goods_tooltip [good]` renders the goods tooltip to check the substitute-group section. Screen-wide pixel accuracy and real save data are not verified. Rules and limits: `docs/guides/EU5_GUI_Modding_Guide.md` |
| `ai_gui_assessment/renderer/guidef.py <name>` | Before writing a `blockoverride` or using any vanilla `type`/`template` | Prints the full resolved definition from the game and SOL files |
| `ai_gui_assessment/renderer/compare.py` / `lib_batch.py` | When verifying the renderer against new in-game screenshots | Side-by-side/zoom crops; batch render of all 26 UI Library pages |
| `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools/perf_monitor/eu5_perf_log.ps1` | While a long observer run is going, to tell whether the bottleneck is single-core CPU, RAM capacity (pagefile thrashing), or disk | Every N seconds appends eu5.exe CPU in cores, busiest-core %, working set/private memory, system commit, pagefile use, hard page reads/s and disk busy to `data/perf_logs/eu5_perf_<stamp>.csv`, plus a hardware sidecar `.txt` (CPU, board, DIMMs, pagefile, disks). CIM-only so it works on localized Windows |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser` | On an uncompressed text save produced with EU5 debug mode when calibrating or auditing the location-class compensation model | Memory-maps the save and extracts only metadata, country tags and solver caches, population records, location/population links, and decoded `sol_location_*` caches; writes analysis-ready location and country CSVs, a diagnostic manifest, and an optional population-group CSV under `data/save_analysis/` by default |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.demand_analysis` | After exporting a debug save and when comparing the active location classifier with exact and gated approximate solves | Replays the current classifier or uses saved class ids, tries five total-constrained nonnegative approximation objectives plus `proportional_fast` as **offline comparison baselines** (the runtime itself ships only exact and the vertex sweep — these retired objectives are kept here solely to reproduce the measurements that justified retiring them), enforces the four-stratum gate by default (with explicit diagnostic bypasses for worsening and total-drift candidates), and writes country diagnostics including maximum worsening and total drift, one raw/approximate/exact error row per country and stratum, four-row stratum summaries, a strategy comparison, and metadata without collapsing nobles, clergy, burghers, and lower into one headline score |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.cone_feasibility` | **First step** when a country reports `negative_factor` or a class-wide `-100%` `local_pop_demand` | Decides whether the exact solve can be feasible for *any* 4-way classification by testing whether the target lies in the cone of all owned-location spending vectors; also reports the minimum-norm ideal factor field, how many locations would need a negative factor, and which stratum subsets conflict. A residual above ~0.01 rules out every classifier-side fix |
| `python tools/eu5_save_parser/solver_outcome_survey.py` | When reporting what the solver actually achieves, or when re-checking whether the size prefilter still earns its keep | Rebuilds each country's 4xK matrix from the exported location caches and replays the full pipeline (size gate, exact 4x4, 209-vertex sweep with the hard no-worsen rule), then reports the outcome split and median improvement per save. Also reports monthly variable-operation cost derived from that same replay, which supersedes any estimate keyed off cone feasibility — the cone bound understates sweep counts because it overstates how often the exact solve succeeds. Measured 2026-08-13 over three timepoints (1337.10, 1386.03, 1743.04): **the exact solve is rare, 1.0%-3.3% of countries** — it is an optimistic fast path, not the workhorse. The approximation carries 28.2%-40.1%, cutting median per-country scaled L1 by 43%-67% and scaled L-infinity by 13%-52% (improvement is taken per country then medianed, not median-raw over median-solved). Genuinely unsolvable is only 3.8%-4.4% except in 1743, where it reaches 13.9% and L-infinity improvement collapses to 13.4%. Cost: full solve is 16.6M-22.5M varops per save-month with sweeps at 87%-94% of it, dropping to 2.5M-2.7M under AI yearly frequency. One sweep (45,562) equals **426 locations** of per-location work while the mean country holds only 9-33, so the governing cost variable is the count of countries reaching a sweep, not map size. Corrects an earlier cone-bound estimate: the size gate sacrifices **almost no exact solves** (0-2 countries per save, since the pigeonhole rule already precludes them below four locations) but does give up the approximation for 43%-62% of gated countries, while the remaining 38%-57% were unsolvable anyway. Run by path, not `-m` |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.market_spending` | When per-stratum base spending shifts and the cause is unclear | Reads the persisted `sol_market_unit_spending_*` maps for one market across several saves, showing that unit spending is frozen between yearly refreshes and exactly when it stepped; `--goods` lists the engine's own consumed-goods filter from the `sol_market_consumes_*` maps |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.autosave_archiver` | While a long observer/test game runs (EU5 keeps only a few rotating autosaves) | Stdlib-only local watcher of the EU5 save folder: waits for finished `.eu5` files, reads the in-game date and playthrough from save metadata (zip member or file-name fallback) and copies the first save of every 10-game-year bucket to `data/saves/observer_archive/<playthrough_id>/`, with `.part` copies, a free-space reserve and a resumable `archive_state.json`; `--interval/--anchor/--out/--once/--dry-run` |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.world_export --archive <archive> --out <worlds>` | To prepare observer worlds for obsession experiments | Resumable `/2` world snapshots and hashed per-playthrough timelines, with original save SHA/size/mtime, resolved runtime culture/religion identities, official country faith and declared religious-head status, actual populations/ownership/markets/resident merchant power and roads, plus hash-bound active marriage/alliance supplements. Reads archived saves only; excludes commodity trade quantities and guessed transport routes |
| `python -m tools.eu5_save_parser.relationships_export --archive <archive> --worlds <worlds>` | To add or resume diplomatic inputs on already-exported observer worlds | Extracts current royal marriages and actual alliances from diplomacy-manager records without reading population objects; excludes peace offers and opinions, verifies the original save SHA, preserves world-snapshot bytes and manifest roles, and binds each supplement into the world hash |
| `docs/knowledge/SOL_Save_Analysis_20260808.md` | Session handoff and reproducibility reference for the save-demand analyzer | Records the three-save 1337/1386/1743 benchmark, definitions of valid locations and solver failure statuses, size-binned gate rates, and the measured cost/benefit of relaxed total preservation |
| `docs/knowledge/SOL_Economy_UI_Handoff_20261013.md` | Session handoff for the SOL economy front-end redesign | Records the organising principle behind the three surfaces (estate-per-row ledger, composition ring, goods grouped by demand group), the file map and commands, the two deliberate omissions (no country-side power share; no country multiplier or market tier on the generic goods tooltip), the open items in priority order, and the layout gotchas that cost time |
| `docs/knowledge/cone_survey_20260811.md` | Feasibility survey across all saves and countries | Proves that 87.3% of normal country-save states are mathematically infeasible for an exact solve even under the finest possible model (one independent nonnegative coefficient per location), so classifier improvements cannot raise the 12.7% feasible rate. Shows the rate is stable across 406 years and increases with country size (10% for 1–9 locations, 63% for 200+). Tool: `cone_survey.py`. Note that this is a **cone upper bound, not the shipped solver's behaviour**: replaying the real 4-class pipeline (`solver_outcome_survey.py`) puts exact solves at only 0.4%-3.3%. Also note this document records the 1536 save as "likely a data issue" — it is not; that save carries no SOL runtime state at all (see `empty_sol_caches_mistaken_for_parser_failure`) |
| `python tools/eu5_save_parser/heterogeneity_survey.py` | When checking whether location heterogeneity is large enough for classification to work, or when a save lacks SOL caches and cannot be used for solver analysis | Measures within-country stratum-share dispersion using only engine-native population columns, so it works on SOL-less exports too. Measured 2026-08-13 over 1337.10, 1386.03 and 1743.04: median noble-share max/min ratio rises from 3.8x to 5.8x across the campaign, and rises with country size from 2.7x (5-9 locations) to 7.2x-13.2x (200+). Four-way class coverage — every stratum having some location where it is the most over-represented — holds for 39.6%-59.5% of countries. Confirms from independent data that the premise behind the whole country-level approach holds, and explains why large countries solve better — their classes are not merely more numerous but more sharply differentiated |
| `python -m tools.obsession_sim import-eu5 --game <installation> --out <graph.json>` / `run --graph <graph.json> --scenario <scenario.json> --html <report.html>` | When calibrating the planned obsession (痴迷) diffusion, which has no gameplay code yet; design lives in `docs/design/obsession/` | Extracts full-resolution real EU5 land adjacency, explicit straits, region hierarchy, template attributes and verified 1337 ownership/date; supports complete save-CSV snapshots or explicitly requested partial overlays. Runs the one-x/temporary-queue/country-clock reference with sparse scheduled directed transport, shortest-land-path corridors, strait weights, finite origin inputs, conditioned weak contacts, existing customs, hundred-year propagation windows and clock renewal that preserves effective x. Real market/population data can construct bounded-degree hub rings and directed volume flows without market cliques. Reports separate first-positive and x≥1/20/45/70/100 arrivals, equal-location or actual-population-weighted milestone errors, negative constraints, front profiles and reproducible scenario defaults. HTML reports embed real-map geometry and historical playback. Legacy synthetic regressions remain exact; dynamic ownership/trade, independent snapshots, regional historical acceptance and actual engine cost remain unverified |
| `python -m tools.obsession_sim.historical --graph <graph.json> --budget 50000 --out <result.json>` / `python -m tools.obsession_sim.study --graph <graph.json> --out <study.json> --html-dir <folder> --mc 30` | When validating the coffee/tea/tobacco first batch and controlling peak abstract U | Applies one shared parameter set to all three goods, frozen city targets and an explicitly permitted static historical port/caravan approximation. All goods share a calendar and budget: clocks/inputs are charged first, then node admission rotates fairly; unavoidable maintenance overruns remain failures. Studies retain failed low-budget cases, transport/corridor ablations, arrival/connectivity/entry audits, input sensitivity and self-contained HTML plus explicitly named .playback.json/.scenario.json files; the replay importer accepts JSON or embedded HTML and identifies configuration/report mixups. Conservative pricing adds 10+d coefficient evaluation per service. City-proxy success is kept separate from full population-weighted L1/L2/L3: row-level source verification, real colonial export eligibility, joint wine/coffee constraints, tobacco bans and blind independent-snapshot validation remain open |
| `python -m tools.obsession_sim.round3_workflow {mechanics,protocol,fit,select,diagnostics}` / `python docs/design/obsession/experiments/build_round3_report.py` | For handoff 26 fit-only selection and diagnostics | The mechanics-only mode never loads targets or constructs scorers. One committed 64-candidate six-dimensional Latin batch uses seed 2026102201; six finalists receive seeds 0–4 and committed selection precedes diagnostics. R has 30 seeds per S/H mode; both real-endpoint O worlds have 10 per S/G mode, plus five rule ablations, four single-mechanism rollbacks and doubled-budget seeds 0–9. Reports include all candidates, 24 fit A/B targets, 84 order pairs, 43 new resident-fact trigger counts, K rule/case tables, clock/wave histories, earlier national/L clock-anchor postprocessing bound to immutable raw records, report-only historical channel cases and masked fit-only playback; full/holdout scoring remains with the auditor |
| `python -m tools.obsession_sim.experiment {run,scan,stages,matrix,freeze-check}` / `python -m tools.obsession_sim.round2 audit-batch` | For the current dynamic three-layer obsession reference | Static opening tea/tobacco customs are immutable, reject inputs, have filtered outgoing influence and are excluded from open country/market aggregates; full aggregates still provide resident/ownership sources and preemption. Structural country clocks use deadline D, a 1200-month first-touch cap plus 240-month hold, national-adoption shortening, and retreat-only wave renewal. Wine is a live-religion static-region background with no simulator/U item; R residents use measured q0.9, while O keeps actual nonterritorial shares. The workbench exposes country clocks, immutable-zone counters, reachability ceilings, sparse-K diagnostics, rollback/channel/budget contrasts and both observer worlds. core reference outputs remain exact; no gameplay code is deployed |
| `python -m tools.obsession_sim serve --graph <graph.json> --scenario <scenario.json> --port 8765` | When tuning obsession diffusion speed against history in the browser | Calibration workbench over the imported EU5 map or a 13,572-node hex grid, both run by the same Python core. Click locations to set or append seeds, pick observation scopes (country/province/area/region/subcontinent/culture/religion) with adoption-share curves, and save location or scope milestones. Every run enters an in-page history with parameter diffs; a chosen reference adds overlay curves, arrival-difference colouring and milestone deltas. One-parameter sweeps (`POST /api/sweep`, up to 24 values) report summaries and milestone error/tolerance and pick the best value. Live frames during computation, form-vs-replay change marks, a p·x₀ vs δ startup check, timeline seeking and keyboard control; exported replays still work offline |

## Data Files

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `data/target_demand.csv` | Source of truth for calibrated baseline demand_add and complete vanilla development-threshold negations |
| `data/demand_price_table.csv` | Computed demand matrix used to hardcode SOL unit-pop consumption quantities |
| `data/jtg_pop_demand.csv` | Reproducible Workshop ID, prices, native pop demand, wealth/development gates, and regional demand modifiers for the 26 Just Goods goods scaled by the SOL-JTG generator |
| `data/goods_weights.csv` | Authoritative 20-group membership mapping for substitution, demand-price generation and UI; preserves all 67 memberships |
| `data/pop_group_baskets.csv` | Authoritative binary subscriptions for all 20 groups and seven strata; core demand equals the subscribed member union, with PP's target-wide lumber removal |
| `data/substitute_groups.json` | Algorithm A, V=1, epsilon=0, historical group sizes independent of eligible counts, and frozen compatibility members |
| `data/alpha_bracket_table.csv` | Legacy Engel curve data; not active in EU5 1.3 SOL demand |
| `data/alpha_generator_settings.json` | Legacy Engel generator settings |
| `scripts/sol_economy_effects_source.py` | Python emitters for location raw demand, classification, country matrix aggregation, solver logging, panels, and monthly application; target selection controls stable-only gold transfer and the standalone panel-stat variant |
| `scripts/sol_country_demand_solver_source.py` | Renderer for the separate country-level exact 4x4 solve and the 209-case strict no-worsen vertex sweep; it keeps the fixed-dimensional Gaussian/reduced blocks out of the location aggregation file |

## SOL System Architecture

- **Calibrated demand baseline** - `data/target_demand.csv` drives `src/stable/in_game/common/goods/z_SOL_pop_goods.txt` and `src/sol_standalone/in_game/common/goods/z_SOL_pop_goods.txt`. These `demand_add` values remain the baseline that the SOL multiplier scales. The generator rejects unnegated vanilla wealth-threshold keys (including the 1.4 wine gate) and requires exact negation of every vanilla `development_threshold`, while both targets set `NPop.DEVELOPMENT_SCALE_ON_DEMAND = 0` and `NPop.POP_NEEDS_INCOME_SCALE = 1`; the latter removes vanilla's 33% purchase-side surcharge so nominal goods cost matches SOL's strata-income accounting.
- **Hardcoded unit consumption constants** - `data/demand_price_table.csv` is converted into each target's `in_game/common/script_values/SOL_market_unit_consumption_values.txt`, storing net demand quantity for each pop type and good. Duplicate goods that appear in multiple old demand groups are counted once.
- **PP compatibility calculation** - The compatibility target uses fixed rounded `victuals` net-demand targets of 0.05/0.03/0.023/0.0016/0.0008/0.0016 for nobles/clergy/burghers/laborers/peasants/soldiers, selected after comparing each stratum's PP-only default-price spending ratio against six staple goods. It injects the positive or negative `demand_add` delta needed to reach those targets, and the same final quantities feed SOL accounting. It negates PP's `victuals` development threshold, forces all lumber quantities to zero, and explicitly `REPLACE`s the two SOL effects that need PP-aware market refresh and country goods aggregation. Existing SOL values/effects remain owned by the full SOL mod instead of being duplicated under later filenames. The compatibility-specific `sol__pp_gdp_dev` CMF setting defaults off, has no no-CMF fallback, and can explicitly restore SOL's yearly GDP-to-development pulse.
- **M&T compatibility calculation** - The M&T target uses complete M&T goods bodies where available and current vanilla fallback for new goods such as camels; it replaces population-demand fields and rebases lumber location_potential because its old vanilla trigger was removed. Valid M&T inverse_control military penalties remain intact even though vanilla dropped them from that object. Seven SOL quantities are inversely scaled by the vanilla/SOL to M&T default-price ratio and rounded to five decimals; maize, millet, and rice are equalized from combined SOL spending, tools laborer demand resolves to 0.002, M&T slave quantities are zero, and all wealth/development gates are removed. Matching generated constants and market refresh logic keep SOL accounting aligned with the final goods database. The compatibility-specific `sol__mnt_as_on` CMF master defaults off and gates the restored SOL economic subsettings; without CMF the trigger stays false, while an explicit opt-in reactivates only trigger-controlled SOL systems and leaves M&T's unconditional goods, price, and static-modifier authority intact.
- **JTG compatibility calculation** - `data/jtg_pop_demand.csv` records the 26 direct-demand Just Goods goods plus their source wealth/development gates and continent/sub-continent demand modifiers. The compatibility generator applies the legacy per-stratum multipliers and clean magnitude rounding, then rebases those targets by x1/x2/~0.6555/x4 with one or two significant digits for burghers, injects the exact five-decimal demand deltas, fully negates every recorded threshold/modifier, and emits matching unit-consumption constants. It replaces only `sol_refresh_market_pop_demand_maps` and `gls_accumulate_panel_stats`, expands both goods grids from 56 to 82 entries, leaves six demand-free Just Goods goods untouched and cancels every JTG slave demand, and audits the CSV against the installed `3785316314` Workshop content root so new gates cannot be omitted silently.
- **Unified Glorp UI merge** - `sync_location_window.py` validates Workshop `3812518640` (`glorp.ui.rio`, version `04.10.26`) as the current source snapshot, uses the complete Glorp UI location window as the merge base, accepts its already-migrated 1.4 GUI API, preserves the integrated optional `cm_*` references, and inserts one SOL Living Standard tooltip button. It also keeps the extracted location types synchronized; older pre-1.4 snapshots remain supported by the shared migration helper. The M&T generator applies the same migration to its own reference snapshot.
- **Generated economy effects** - `gen_sol_economy_effects.py` makes both base-target `A_SOL_economy_effects.txt` files and matching `B_SOL_country_demand_solver.txt` files. A owns location raw demand, classification, matrix aggregation, strategy logging, and modifier application; B owns the exact 4x4 solve, Gaussian elimination primitives, the 209-case vertex sweep with its scaled-L1/L-infinity metrics, the raw-error prefilter, and strategy selection. The split changes file ownership and loading size only; effect names and cross-file calls remain global and compatible submods continue to inherit the base effect.
- **Exact-solve feasibility is cone containment, not linear independence** - `M x = t` with `x >= 0` is solvable exactly when `t` lies in the nonnegative cone spanned by the four class columns. `det(M) != 0` only gives uniqueness, and a healthy condition number does not imply feasibility. Because every class column is a sum of nonnegative location vectors, `cone(any 4-way partition)` is contained in `cone(all owned locations)`, so when the target is outside the all-location cone **no classifier change, class count, capacity rule, or conditioning fix can make the exact solve feasible** - only relaxing the target definition or accepting an approximation can. Many normal save states are genuinely infeasible in this sense; measured HUN residuals were 0.0465-0.1057 with 8-43 locations requiring negative factors. A zero coefficient returned by a nonnegative approximation is a legitimate boundary optimum rather than a clamped artifact, which is why a whole class can legitimately land on `-100%`. Run `tools/eu5_save_parser/cone_feasibility.py` before diagnosing any such report; see `docs/technical/SOL_Country_Level_Strata_Demand_Model.md` section 3.9 for the derivation, the subset conflict table, and the four attributions the data ruled out.

- **Country solver runtime cost** - Measured in variable operations (see `tools/eu5_save_parser/solver_outcome_survey.py`): the raw pass is 70 per location, classification plus matrix aggregation is 37 per location, solver setup is 120 per country, the exact 4x4 solve is 112, and one vertex case is 218 so the full 209-case sweep is 45,562. Total cost is best read as `107 * total_locations + 45,562 * countries_reaching_a_sweep`, and the second term dominates: one sweep equals 426 locations of per-location work while the mean country holds only 9-33, making sweeps 87%-94% of all solver cost. So the only optimisations that matter are ones that reduce how many **countries** reach a sweep — never disabling the exact solve, which is 0.2% of cost and saves a whole sweep on every success. Countries with fewer than five owned locations pay none of the solver cost: the size prefilter routes them to raw-only before classification runs. For the rest, with `n` owned locations, classification still contributes one confidence sort `O(n log n)` plus linear scans and matrix aggregation `O(n)`. After raw-error and nonempty-column prefilters, exact-enabled countries stop at their validated 4x4 result; exact-disabled countries skip that matrix solve but still prepare approximation inputs. Approximation cost is a fixed 209-case vertex sweep, gated by the raw-error prefilter and by the side's CMF approximation toggle. Only two strategy ids are live: `7` for exact-direct and `8` for the vertex sweep, with `0` marking `raw_fallback`; the single CMF log row names the adopted one, and no separate numeric Log/offline-analysis cache is persisted.
- **Yearly market spending maps** - `sol_refresh_market_pop_demand_maps` scans `every_market_in_world`, writes consumed-good counters keyed by market scope, and caches one unit-spending `global_variable_map` per pop type. For each consumed good, spending is computed as hardcoded consumption quantity times `min(market_price(goods), default_price(goods))`, so cheaper goods lower base spending while expensive goods do not inflate it above vanilla base price.
- **Yearly SOL maintenance caches** - Normal SOL targets call `sol_update_estate_building_maintenance` to scan every owned land location, cache the five SOL estate building counts, and subtract 1 gold per cached estate building from matching stratum income. In the M&T target that scanner is disabled: M&T's own player-monthly/AI-yearly EPBM calculation writes charge-factor- and estate-power-adjusted domestic location costs into the same five variables without changing M&T country deductions.
- **Monthly local demand modifier** - `monthly_country_pulse` calls `sol_update_local_pop_demand_modifiers`, which computes each owned land location's income-closed raw coefficient, then (for countries with at least five locations) scores its four-stratum shares relative to nationwide shares and classifies it with a confidence-ordered greedy pass. Classification capacity is measured as `raw * base`: every class receives 1% of nationwide raw spending as a deliberately tiny anchor, and the remaining 96% is divided among strata with `raw baseline - target > 0` in proportion to that downward pressure. The matrix is built raw-weighted; exact is attempted first, then exact failures run the shared 209-case strict no-worsen vertex sweep for both AI and human countries. A candidate is admissible only when neither its scaled L1 nor its scaled L-infinity exceeds the raw value. A successful factor multiplies every member location's raw coefficient before the one-month modifier is applied; factor 1 preserves raw, 0 to 1 reduces it without crossing zero, and factors above 1 are uncapped. Missing classes leave empty columns; singular, negative, numerical-residual, or gate failures use `raw_fallback`. The first post-lobby refresh primes the estate-building cache once; yearly pulse keeps it current afterward.
- **Income-closed location scale** - Each location sums five gross stratum incomes, subtracts cached estate building maintenance to derive total net stratum income, applies the country's unified savings adjustment `(total savings / total savings target - 1) * 0.25` capped at `0.5` (+50%), and divides liquid funds directly by market-keyed base spending. Commoner base spending remains exact by internally using laborer, peasant, and soldier unit-spending maps. Development does not scale or gate demand. The raw result remains cached for diagnostics and fallback; successful class coefficients use dedicated caches and become final for classified locations, while negligible-base class-0 locations and failed country solves retain raw. No country-level poverty compensation factor is applied.
- **Country-level aggregation** - Location income, savings, base spending, liquid funds, solved final coefficient, and consumed market goods feed the situation panel and map overlay.
- **Map display** - The automatic situation map uses vanilla's `situation_data` path via `is_data_map = yes` plus the situation's own `tooltip`, `map_color`, and `legend_key` blocks. The situation data map and the separate selectable `sol_living_standard` economy mapmode both color by local living standard (`sol_map_living_standard_value` = annual funds per thousand with flat/percent SOL bonuses): red at 0, yellow at world average, green at 3× world average, with tooltip bands at 0.5×, 1× and 2×. A positive 6-unit fallback covers missing or zero world averages; no consumption baseline is required. The SOL situation panel creates a zero-size child whose `trigger_on_create` state calls `PdxGuiWidget.PushMapModeOverride('sol_living_standard')`; the widget-scoped override is removed when the situation-specific panel is destroyed, restoring the prior mapmode. EU5 reference syntax still does not expose a verified situation script field that points to a custom mapmode tag.
- **Country substitution** - The scheduled C_SOL_substitution.txt solve subtracts the active cached auto delta from modifier:global_<good>_pop_demand, computes size=(1+e)(M-1), and publishes both sol_sub_size_<good> and sol_good_effective_<good>. Country population is aggregated by market/pop type before basket evaluation. Explicit subscriptions choose whole groups; current and neutral contributions use the same supplied, positive-external members, preserving overlapping memberships. The 56 country auto modifier definitions read only guarded nonzero size caches; zero caches are removed after spending and summary reads, and names/descriptions use bilingual AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_/DESC_ keys; no market, population, price or aggregate-modifier reads occur in their scales. Seven market-keyed country-adjusted unit costs retain owner isolation for location spending.
- **Substitution lifecycle** - Humans and normal-cadence AI recompute substitution and SOL monthly; reduced-frequency AI solves yearly on its own anniversary. Start/load and explicit settings/manual callbacks refresh immediately. Auto modifiers keep reading the cached result between solves, including when external modifiers change. Missing/disabled caches yield zero, and empty/master-off cleanup removes state once; substitution-off refresh retains external spending only. The enabled-refresh counter and sol_sub_perf_human flag remain for save diagnostics. Static grants, apply/dirty-check effects, per-location snapshots and live/request readbacks are removed with no old-implementation migration. Missing market detail initializes the shared cache; world/full/load refresh invalidates unit-cost owners. Native refresh timing and fixed-point rounding remain unverified. See docs/technical/SOL_Substitution_Auto_Modifiers_20261023.md.
- **Retired 1.2 chain** - Per-stratum Engel curves, religious/cultural preferences, market-hub scarcity compensation, development cancellation and `sol_era_coeff` remain retired; the restored price-only substitute response does not revive their scales.

## Known Anti-Patterns

| Category | Scope | Bad Form | Correct Form | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| gui | any | datacontext = "[InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer]" inside action_button_diamond in an organization panel | Remove datacontext entirely. Use Player.MakeScope.GetVariable(...).IsSet in visible expressions instead of Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...).IsSet | Adding datacontext to action_button_diamond overrides the widget's internal binding, causing tooltip generation to fail silently (empty tooltip, button stays grayed regardless of action potential). Vanilla IO panels never use datacontext on action_button_diamond. Use Player (global GUI binding) instead of Country (datacontext-dependent) to access player-country variables. |
| modifier | country | Using add_country_modifier with a modifier id defined only in common/auto_modifiers | Keep Country Auto modifiers in common/auto_modifiers for actual automatic effects. For tooltip previews or scripted effect lists, emit real non-modifier effect lines or omit the modifier-list preview. Do not pass auto modifier ids to add_country_modifier, and do not restore finalization-time static grants/removals for building-gated wonder effects. | Country Auto modifiers and static country modifiers are separate databases. A Country Auto modifier may have category = country, but add_country_modifier still cannot use its id. |
| script | country | add_treasury = X to add or subtract gold from a country | Use add_gold = X (simple literal: add_gold = -200) or add_gold = { value = script_value multiply = N } (computed form). Trigger side: gold >= 200 (literal integer) is valid. | add_treasury does not exist in EU5. The effect is add_gold; it accepts a bare literal integer (positive or negative) or a block with value/multiply. Error: 'Unknown effect add_treasury'. |
| modifier | any | INJECT:advance_name = { allow = { ... } } when the vanilla advance already has an allow block | Use REPLACE:advance_name = { <full vanilla block> allow = { OR = { <mod condition> <original condition> } } } to merge conditions. Use INJECT only for advances with no existing allow block. | EU5 advance definitions allow only ONE allow block. INJECT appends properties; a second allow block via INJECT is invalid. Must copy the full vanilla advance definition into a REPLACE block and merge the OR condition manually. |
| gui | character | Arts leader select_trigger using column.data = character_info | Use column.data = character_artist for artist-only character selectors so the list uses the artist ability view. | The character_artist attribute column swaps the character list's ability area and tooltip to artist skill. Generic character_info shows ruler-style ADM/DIP/MIL information and is the wrong view for selecting a Chief Artist. |
| modifier | character | value = character.artist_skill  multiply = 0.002 (assuming artist_skill is an integer 0-100 scale) | Use multiply = 0.1 (or other factor scaled to the 0-1 range). artist_skill in EU5 is a normalized float 0.0-1.0, not a raw integer. A skill-5 artist returns ~0.5, not 5. | artist_skill is a level-based attribute normalized to [0,1] by the engine when accessed as a scripted value. Design formulas written assuming 0-100 scale must divide the intended multiplier by 100. |
| modifier | character | static_modifier or character_modifier using artist_skill_modifier = X | Use artist_skill_level_gain = X instead (already_percent = yes, category = character) | artist_skill_modifier does not exist. The correct modifier type for boosting artist skill development is artist_skill_level_gain (declared as already_percent in modifier_type_definitions). |
| localization | modifier | Emitting a common/auto_modifiers id without matching AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> localization | Generate AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> for every emitted auto modifier, including hidden hide_effects companions such as *_unscaled. | Hidden Country Auto modifiers still load as modifier database entries and the localization system requests their AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME keys during startup. |
| gui | any | Filling a `-1 <fixed_height>` (expanding-width, fixed-height) `background = { texture = ... }` widget with a wide non-tileable illustration (e.g. the 2048x1152 victory reward-tree banners) -- e.g. gen_tv_victory_situation_gui.py's first draft on append_tree_background used `spriteType = stretched`, then a second draft added `fittype = centercrop`, and both still visibly distorted/misbehaved because the widget's runtime width is not fixed and does not match the texture's aspect ratio. | For a non-tileable illustration with a specific aspect ratio, do not let the container's width float (`-1`/expanding) while only the height is pinned -- any stretch or crop fit mode still has to guess how to reconcile an arbitrary runtime width against the fixed texture aspect ratio. Instead give the widget an explicit fixed size on both axes computed from the source image's real aspect ratio (e.g. `width = 508`, `height = round(508 * source_height / source_width)`), with `layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` and `layoutpolicy_vertical = fixed`, so `spriteType = Stretched` maps the texture 1:1 onto matching proportions with no distortion and no cropping. | Found 2026-07-12 wiring the six victory-path reward-tree background DDS assets into tv_victory_situation.gui. First fix attempt (fittype = centercrop) was rejected by the user as both an unwanted crop and non-functional; the working fix is a hard pixel size (508 wide, proportional height) instead of any fit-mode reliance on a floating container width. |
| gui | any | Appending a `parentanchor`/percentage-size widget as a direct child of a `blockoverride "<name>" { ... }` body, on the assumption that because the widget appears as a plain sibling in the mod's own .gui file (not literally nested inside a local `vbox = { }`/`hbox = { }`), the hbox/vbox restriction does not apply. `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay(lines, 2)` was emitted as a direct sibling inside `blockoverride "situation_panel_main_content" { ... }` with `parentanchor = top|left` and `size = { 100% 100% }` directly on it. | A `blockoverride "X"` substitutes content at the exact point where the BASE type declares `block "X" {}` — read that declaration in `reference_game_files` before assuming the substitution point's parent container. `situation_panel_main_content` is declared inside `scrollwidget = { vbox = { ... block "situation_panel_main_content" {} ... } }` in `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/panels/situation/common.gui`, so every direct child emitted inside the local `blockoverride` block is actually a vbox child at runtime, even though the mod's own file shows it as a sibling of other top-level widgets. Do NOT fix this by wrapping the ENTIRE blockoverride body (including the real scrollable content) in one outer `widget` — a plain `widget` does not propagate its children's natural/intrinsic height to its own parent the way a `vbox` does, so wrapping the tabs+pages vbox inside that outer widget hides its true content height from the enclosing `scrollarea`'s `autoresizeviewport` and disables scrolling entirely (confirmed by the user in-game). Only wrap the OFFENDING widget itself, and keep the real scrollable content vbox as a direct, unwrapped sibling in the blockoverride exactly as before. For a widget that must also default to zero layout footprint while hidden (e.g. a conditional overlay), the wrapper must be a `vbox`/`hbox` with `ignoreinvisible = yes` set on itself — not a plain `widget`, which does not support `ignoreinvisible` at all (see rule `gui_ignoreinvisible_on_plain_widget`) — then nest a plain `widget` inside that ignoreinvisible-capable vbox, and only put `parentanchor`/percentage `size` on a THIRD widget nested one level deeper still (a child of that plain widget, not of the vbox). | Found 2026-07-13 from docs/error_log/error.log: 'Unsupported property: parentanchor on widgets that are a child of a hbox/vbox' / 'size with a percentage ... consider using layout policies' at tv_victory_situation.gui:20148-20149, origin 20145 — the confirm-overlay widget added right after the tabs vbox closed. The existing lint validator `check_gui_parentanchor_under_hbox_vbox` (see [[parentanchor_under_hbox_vbox]]) only scans the LOCAL file's brace structure and cannot see that a `blockoverride` substitution point is itself inside a vbox in a different (vanilla) file, so this class of violation is not currently lint-detectable — verify blockoverride parentage against the base template (see gui.md risk card point 5) whenever adding parentanchor/percent-size widgets as direct blockoverride children. First fix attempt wrapped the WHOLE blockoverride body (tabs/pages vbox + overlay) in one outer `widget = { layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding  layoutpolicy_vertical = expanding }`; this passed `validate.py` and cleared the log errors, but the user reported it broke the situation panel's scrollarea entirely. Re-fixed by reverting `append_panel` to its original unwrapped vbox, and instead restructuring only `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` into `vbox { ignoreinvisible = yes ... } > widget { datacontext ... } > widget { parentanchor = top|left  size = { 100% 100% } ... }` — three levels, not the two originally used, because the outer level needs to be an `ignoreinvisible`-capable vbox (see `gui_ignoreinvisible_on_plain_widget`) to keep contributing zero height to the scrollarea while the popup is hidden (the default and by far most common state). SUPERSEDED 2026-07-13 (same day): `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` itself was deleted once `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold = yes` (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`) made the whole overlay unnecessary. The structural fact about `situation_panel_main_content` being nested in vbox in vanilla `common.gui`, and the wrap-only-the-offender-not-the-whole-block fix pattern, remain generically true for ANY future content injected into that same blockoverride — kept for that reason, not because the deleted overlay still exists. |
| gui | any | text_single widget inside blockoverride "common_header_text" | blockoverride overrides a 'text' property value, not a widget container; do not add child widgets | Guessing block structure causes silent failures; always read cards.gui before using blockoverride |
