| modifier | location | Boolean location modifier can_recruit_regiment_in_this_location assigned numeric 0/1 | Use can_recruit_regiment_in_this_location = yes or no. The modifier type is declared boolean=yes, and vanilla building modifiers use yes. | EU5 logs Malformed token near the numeric literal when a boolean modifier value is emitted as 1. Generated YAML data must preserve bools and render them as yes/no, not Python True/False or 1/0. |
| scope | location | Using direct `owner = <scope>` or `dominant_culture = <scope>` comparisons while iterating all locations or a geography that can include non-ownable, water, or ownerless locations | Filter the iterator with `is_ownable = yes`, then use optional scope links: `dominant_culture ?= <culture>` and `NOT = { owner ?= <country> }`. Optional owner comparison safely treats an ownerless ownable location as not controlled; optional dominant-culture comparison ignores locations with no culture object. | Saving the country owner scope fixed the earlier invalid-root path but did not make each iterated location's nullable links valid. The task generator still scanned sea/non-ownable locations with direct `dominant_culture` and direct `owner` links, so Governor's House creation and its hover-pre-evaluation logged invalid-object errors repeatedly. The safe pattern is emitted for all culture and geography unification checks, including the culture-group display trigger. |
| script | any | Using `building_efficiency:` in any colon-chained form (`<scope>.building_efficiency:<X>`, `<scope>.building_efficiency:<type>:<X>`, or bare `building_efficiency:<X>`) as a numeric value source. | For numeric building-efficiency reads, use the quoted function-call-style event target link: `value = "<location_scope>.building_efficiency(building_type:<id>)"`, or `value = "location.building_efficiency(scope:<saved_building_type>)"` when the current scope is a building and the building type was saved as a scope. For trigger checks, the block form remains valid: `building_efficiency = { building_type = building_type:<id> value >= <N> }`. Do not use the colon-chain form; it is parsed as an event-target link and either fails to resolve the building id or rejects the extra colon. Do not use `building_efficiency` as input-goods access. | 2026-08-02: tv_wonder_labor_camp_input_goods_access originally used `value = var:tv_wonder_site.building_efficiency:tv_wonder_labor_camp`, treating building_efficiency as a colon-chained numeric data function. Two in-game failures ruled out the colon chain: the bare argument form failed to resolve `tv_wonder_labor_camp`, and the scope-prefixed argument form failed earlier with `More than one colon in event target link`. The user's discovery in reference mod 3735059838 (`set_local_variable = { name = epbm_bldg_eff value = "location.building_efficiency(scope:epbm_bt)" }`) shows `building_efficiency` can be read numerically, but only through the quoted parenthesized event-target-link syntax. This quoted+parenthesized style is not a reference-mod oddity: vanilla uses the same script-value/parser form for `estate_power(...)`, `market_price(...)`, `default_price(...)`, and `border_distance_to(...)`. Later same-day in-game validation showed `building_efficiency` matches the labor/staffing satisfaction represented by `total_effective_building_levels`, not the input-goods green bar. See `building_efficiency_not_input_goods_access`. |
| script | any | Using `building_efficiency` as the material/input-goods satisfaction green bar. | For labor/staffing, use `total_effective_building_levels:<id>` versus nominal `location_building_level(building_type:<id>)`, or the quoted `building_efficiency(...)` read when a building-efficiency value is specifically needed. The green input-goods bar is GUI `Building.GetInputGoodsAccess`; no script numeric event target with that value was found in official defines. In script, use building-scope `is_lacking_goods` as the authoritative boolean gate for whether an input shortage affects the building at all. When a numeric material-side approximation is needed, use the site's market-scope `goods_supply_in_market(goods:<input>) / goods_demand_in_market(goods:<input>)` for each required input only after that boolean gate is true; average the per-input satisfaction ratios across the building's fixed input list rather than taking the bottleneck. For a two-input recipe, one input short by x% produces an x/2% material penalty. Do not use `building_goods_input` as the green bar. | For `tv_wonder_labor_camp`, user in-game validation showed `building_efficiency` and `total_effective_building_levels` both track labor/staffing satisfaction. The vanilla green input-goods indicator is `Building.GetInputGoodsAccess`, but official docs expose that only as a GUI data function, not a script numeric accessor. `building_goods_input(goods:X)` is a building-scope required-input value; the 3735059838 reference mod comments that it is scaled by market access and building efficiency, which means it is entangled with the labor value the Wonder breakdown is already displaying separately. The Wonder construction cache therefore checks the active worksite building's `is_lacking_goods` first, then uses the selected site's market supply/demand ratios for the Labor Camp's two fixed inputs, `tv_wonder_materials` and `tools`, averaging the two satisfaction ratios. This matches the observed two-input behavior: if only one input is short by x%, the building's material/input penalty is x/2%, while small market imbalances that do not trip `is_lacking_goods` apply no penalty. This is not a general alias for `GetInputGoodsAccess`; it is a local approximation chosen because the exact green bar is GUI-only. |
| modifier | location | Direct numeric price in EU5 building definition, e.g. price = 200 (EU4 carry-over) | Use price = <named_constant> referencing a definition in common/prices/ (e.g. price = tv_academy_1_price with gold = 600). Capital buildings that scale with age use expensive = yes instead. | EU5 building gold cost uses named price constants from common/prices/ (e.g. price = expensive_estate_building at line 416 of estate_buildings.txt). Direct numeric literals like price = 200 are silently ignored. Named constants ARE valid. |
| script | building | Using building levels multiplied by nominal employment size to determine how many workers a building actually employs | For actual employment, scope into the building object with ordered_buildings_in_location/random_buildings_in_location and read building_employed_amount. If downstream variables are stored as people rather than pop-size units, multiply the value by 1000. `location_building_level(building_type:X)` remains valid when the intended value is nominal completed building levels, such as the Engineering Department's current worksite scale. | location_building_level reports completed levels/capacity, not current staffing. For labor-sensitive mechanics such as Engineering Department construction, use completed levels only for nominal scale, then compare against total_effective_building_levels or building_employed_amount for labor-sensitive effective staffing. |
| script | building | Using building on_built as the only completion signal for a mechanic that also upgrades existing building levels | Use on_construction_ended to directly mark the active auxiliary-building ritual finished and run the normal ritual completion effect. Do not depend on on_built for upgrades, and do not require location_building_level to have updated inside the construction-ended hook. | Engineering Department style-2 ritual annex completion relied on on_built. Fresh annex construction completed, but expansion reused the same annex building as a higher level, so the ritual needed construction-ended direct completion. |
| script | any | change_variable = { name = X add = prev.var:some_var } — reading a named variable from prev scope in a numeric value position | Use set_local_variable / change_local_variable to capture the numeric value before the scope switch, then use add = local_var:name inside the new scope. Example: set_local_variable = { name = tmp value = var:some_var }  then in the other scope: change_variable = { name = X add = local_var:tmp }. | prev.var:X is valid only as a scope reference (country, religion, etc.), not as a numeric value source. Using it in an add = position causes 'Variable not of the value scope type. Type: empty'. Built-in scripted values like prev.num_locations are valid inline; only prev.var:X is broken. |
| script | any | change_variable { divide = N } expecting float result when input < N (e.g. score ÷ large_threshold to get [0,1]) | Multiply by 100 (or desired precision) BEFORE dividing, then compare/cap at 100 instead of 1. For thresholds divisible by 100, use divide = (threshold÷100) in one step. | EU5 change_variable { divide } performs FLOOR integer division. E.g. 160÷2000=0, not 0.1. Use integer scale [0,100] with max=100 on GUI progressbar. Pattern left empty (no auto-detect) — all divide uses cannot be classified as bad without context. |
| script | any | change_variable = { name = X value = 1 } | Use a named arithmetic operator with change_variable, e.g. change_variable = { name = X add = 1 }. If you want to overwrite the variable, use set_variable = { name = X value = 1 }. | change_variable does not accept a value field. The engine logs 'Failed to read value for change_variable' when value is used there. value belongs to set_variable; change_variable uses add/subtract/multiply/divide. False-positive edge case (confirmed live 2026-07 in scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/bank_of_saint_george.py): the single-line regex also fires on a legitimate nested scriptvalue block, e.g. `change_variable = { name = X add = { value = var:X multiply = var:Y } }` (compounding interest). That inline `add = { value = ... multiply = ... }` idiom is valid vanilla syntax -- confirmed at reference_game_files/game/in_game/events/disaster/turmoil_in_brandenburg.txt:1598 (`add_gold = { value = var:bra_owes_gold multiply = -1 }`) -- because the pattern's negated-character-class scan does not track brace depth and cannot tell the nested block's own `value =` from a top-level one on the same physical line. Do not silence this by weakening the regex (it still correctly catches the real mistake). Prefer rewriting the nested arithmetic as a named scratch variable instead: `set_variable = { name = tmp value = var:X }`, `change_variable = { name = tmp multiply = var:Y }`, `change_variable = { name = X add = var:tmp }`, `remove_variable = tmp` -- this also matches the codebase's existing scratch-variable convention (e.g. `*_ritual_total_favorable`, `*_combined_score`) better than a one-line nested scriptvalue. |
| scripted_values | any | change_variable = { name = X  value = Y } — value is not a valid operation for change_variable | Use change_variable = { name = X  add = Y } (or subtract / multiply / divide). Negative literals are fine for add: e.g. add = -0.00020. value = is reserved for set_variable. | set_variable accepts 'value = X' (absolute assignment); change_variable does NOT — it requires an operation keyword (add/subtract/multiply/divide). Mixing them up emits a runtime PostValidate error and the change is silently dropped. Often introduced by codegen scripts that mirror set_variable templates without adapting the op keyword. Pattern uses [^{}]* (not [^}]*) so it does NOT cross nested-block boundaries — this prevents false positives on the legal form 'change_variable = { name = X  add = { value = Y  multiply = Z } }' where value = is the inner-block scalar literal of add's value-with-operations form. |
| scope | character | A targeted character interaction uses scope:recipient in allow or effect without a character select_trigger that sets target_flag = recipient | Add select_trigger = { looking_for_a = character source = actor target_flag = recipient ... }. Character-interaction registration does not automatically pass the viewed/menu character as recipient; target eligibility belongs in the selector visible/enabled blocks, and recipient-dependent effects remain guarded with exists = scope:recipient. | Without the selector, the engine rejects the action at load with `Targetted action <id> doesn't specify a recipient for the action`, even when its allow/effect body refers to scope:recipient. |
| modifier | character | Putting country_cabinet_efficiency inside a character-scoped modifier to lower one cabinet member's efficiency | Use character_cabinet_efficiency for character modifiers. Reserve country_cabinet_efficiency for country modifiers. | country_cabinet_efficiency is a country modifier type and has no effect on a character modifier; the character-scoped equivalent is character_cabinet_efficiency. |
| modifier | character | Static modifier used with add_character_modifier but defined without game_data = { category = character }, e.g. my_modifier = { } | All static modifiers intended for use with add_character_modifier / has_character_modifier MUST include game_data = { category = character }. Example: my_modifier = { game_data = { category = character } [effects...] } | Without category = character, the engine does not recognize the modifier as character-scoped. add_character_modifier runs (tooltip shows the grant) but the modifier is not stored on the character — has_character_modifier returns false and no title prefix fires. Confirmed at runtime: tv_chief_artist_role_modifier = {} appeared in appoint tooltip but never on the character panel. |
| script | any | Placing character trait definitions in common/character_traits/ (e.g. src/in_game/common/character_traits/my_traits.txt) | Place trait files in common/traits/ — the correct EU5 directory: src/in_game/common/traits/my_traits.txt | EU5 loads character traits from common/traits/ not common/character_traits/. Files in the wrong directory are silently ignored and all has_trait checks against those trait IDs produce 'Invalid database object' errors at PostValidate. |
| framework | any | Packing many unrelated diagnostics or more than four dynamic values into one CMF log entry | Keep each CMF entry to one diagnostic concept and at most four dynamic values. Prefer compact labels; split an essential wide vector into short rows, or omit secondary fields rather than emitting an unreadable line. | CMF renders the actor and localized action text in one log row. Dense profile/equation entries become difficult to read or copy even when the total remains below the 200-entry cap. |
| workflow | any | Treating a `jomini_effect.cpp` 'Variable X is set but is never used' / 'used but is never set' warning as an unfixable false positive and leaving it undocumented once the read/write site is confirmed to be a GUI-only `GetVariable` read, a `variable_map`/`global_variable_map` name that is only ever touched by `add_to_*_variable_map`/`clear_variable_map` (never a literal `set_variable`), or a vanilla-consumed variable name (e.g. `parliament_seat`) | Once source-reading confirms the variable genuinely has a legitimate consumer the engine's static usage tracker cannot see, register it in `data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml` (a literal name in `extra_suppressions`, or a new structured family function in `scripts/in_game/common/on_action/gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py` for anything with more than a couple of instances, e.g. per-advance or per-slot families read straight from the owning data YAML) and regenerate `tv_cmf_suppressions.txt`. Do not hand-add `cmf_suppress` lines to that generated file. |  |
| effect | any | Assuming an existing `cmf_suppress = { v = X }` entry (via `data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml` + `gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py`) means variable `X` is a handled/accepted case and its 'used but never set' or 'set but never used' line in `error.log` can be safely ignored without checking whether `X` actually has a real setter/reader anywhere. | `cmf_suppress`'s own definition (`reference_mods/3692202776/in_game/common/scripted_effects/cmf_utility_effects.txt`) is an `always = no`-guarded dead branch that references `$v$` in every set/get form (`set_variable`, `exists = var:$v$`, `has_variable_map`, etc.), explicitly to make the engine's static usage scanner see both a 'set' and a 'used' occurrence — per its own comment this targets exactly the two `jomini_effect.cpp` warning strings this project cares about, not a separate CMF-only display. Whether it reliably works for every reference shape (plain scope variable vs. a bare `global_variable_map` name) through this project's own extra outer `always = no` wrapper (`gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py`'s `tv_cmf_warning_suppressions_monthly_pulse`) is NOT independently confirmed — do not present 'cmf_suppress does/doesn't silence the native log' as a settled fact either way without an actual in-game error.log re-check after the suppression entry is added and the mod redeployed. What IS certain regardless of that mechanism question: a suppression entry is never a substitute for checking the real reference graph. Grep the variable across both mod roots for every `set_variable`/`change_variable`/`add_to_variable_map` (a real setter) and every `var:`/GUI `GetVariable`/`GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap` read (a real consumer) before deciding whether to keep code, delete it, or just suppress the log line. If there is truly zero setter anywhere, delete the dead `remove_variable` calls and the code they clean up rather than leaving them for a suppression entry to paper over. If a `global_variable_map` genuinely has zero readers anywhere including GUI and isn't planned future scaffolding, delete it instead of suppressing it. | 2026-07-15: triaged 9 error.log lines after the Engineering Department mod split. tv_wonder_{foundation,body,function,decoration}_progress and tv_wonder_construction_paused had zero setters anywhere (superseded by tv_wonder_construction_progress + tv_wonder_active_part) despite being cmf_suppress-listed since this suppression file's first commit — the suppression entry masked genuinely dead code rather than a real false positive; deleted the dead remove_variable calls and their suppression entries. tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id (gen_tv_wonder_index_effects.py's FINAL_BUILDING_DISPLAY_ID_MAP) turned out to be a dead duplicate of the already-consumed tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_wonder_id (same generator loop, same wonder_id value, zero readers) rather than a genuine dynamic-reference case — deleted rather than kept suppressed. tv_wonder_suitability_weight (read only via GUI GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap) and tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_never_set (an intentional trade-off documented on tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger, trading one variable warning for avoiding dozens of unused-flag warnings) are both genuine cases where the code itself is correct and only the log line is cosmetic noise — added both to data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml for consistency with every other GUI-only-read/deliberate-workaround entry in that file, on the working assumption (unverified in this session, no way to launch the game) that cmf_suppress's mechanism is sound for the ~2200 other entries already relying on it and these two are not structurally different from those. |
| workflow | any | Re-deriving from scratch whether a variable-map name, GUI-only-read variable, or other dynamic-reference identifier flagged in error.log as used-but-never-set/set-but-never-used is a false positive, instead of checking data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml first. | This project has a pre-existing, already-adopted mechanism (data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml + scripts/in_game/common/on_action/gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py) for the whole class of variables that are read/written only through generation-time-static dynamic references (variable_map names, GUI GetVariable/GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap-only reads) the native scanner cannot trace. Check this file before spending time re-deriving why a variable 'looks unused'. But a suppression entry existing is not proof the variable is genuinely a false positive — see [[cmf_suppress_status_is_not_proof_a_variable_reference_is_real]] for the follow-up correction after one suppressed entry (tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id) turned out to be a real duplicate/dead map, not a dynamic-reference case, and five more (the wonder progress-tracking variables) turned out to have zero setters at all. | 2026-07-09: error.log flagged tv_advance_<id>_researched (31 advances, GUI-only read), tv_academy_debate_node_<1-5>_year/index/type (15 vars, GUI-only read), tv_research_open_age_ceiling (GUI-only read), tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id and tv_trade_good_global_consumption/production (global_variable_map names, assumed never literally set_variable'd), tv_trade_chain_demand_slot_1..20 (variable_map names, same reason), and parliament_seat (vanilla-consumed IO parliament-seat location, confirmed against reference_game_files) as used-but-never-set/set-but-never-used. All were added to data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml via new add_locked_advance_researched_suppressions/add_academy_debate_suppressions family functions plus TRADE_EXTRA_VARIABLES/trade-chain-slot-loop extensions and two extra_suppressions singletons, then tv_cmf_suppressions.txt was regenerated, without individually verifying each had a real reader. 2026-07-15: deeper investigation found tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id was actually a dead duplicate of tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_wonder_id (same generator loop, same value, zero readers anywhere) and was deleted rather than re-suppressed — this entry was previously (and incorrectly) recorded as a 'confirmed legitimate dynamic-reference case.' This note previously overwrote mechanic_similarity_audit_short_triggers_file_dominates_combined_ratio's own notes via a duplicate YAML key in that entry (YAML mappings silently keep only the last value for a repeated key) — split into its own entry 2026-07-15. |
| framework | country | Reusing a formerly enabled or hidden CMM setting ID for a new compatibility opt-in and expecting default_value = 0 to reset upgraded saves | Register a compatibility-specific setting ID with default_value = 0, sync it to the gameplay alias, and omit the legacy setting ID. Gate the gameplay trigger through that alias and keep the feature off when CMM is absent. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). CMM applies a setting's default only when its variable-map key is missing. A saved legacy key keeps its old value, so a fresh compatibility ID is the safe way to preserve default-off behavior without directly rewriting CMM internal maps. |
| framework | global | Initializing a default-OFF CMM bool alias in the mod's on_game_start defaults effect, e.g. set_global_variable = { name = sol_x_enabled value = no } | Omit default-OFF aliases from the init effect entirely. cmm_sync_bool_alias sets the global only when the toggle is ON and calls remove_global_variable when OFF, so has_global_variable must stay false until CMM enables it. Also invert the CMM-absent fallback in the matching sol_*_is_on trigger: default-ON toggles use OR = { NOT = { has_global_variable_map = cmm } ... }, while default-OFF toggles must NOT include that escape or they read as enabled without CMM. | Documentation-only (no regex lint) because a legitimate default-ON init line is textually identical apart from the value. set_global_variable with value = no still creates the key, so has_global_variable returns true and a default-OFF feature runs from turn one. Any setting registered with default_value = 0 must appear in exactly four places: cmm_register_global_bool_setting, cmm_sync_bool_alias, the cmf_on_callback switch, and a <mod_id>__<setting_id>_on_changed scripted GUI when cmm_add_scripted_gui is called — but never in the init defaults. |
| framework | country | Leaving a disabled feature in the mod's CMM registration effect, or directly mutating CMM internal maps to hide a stale setting from old saves | Replace the mod registration effect and omit disabled settings and callbacks. CMM's _cmm_register_group clears each group's setting-key list during every registration cycle, so omitted settings disappear from both new games and upgraded saves. Hard-disable the gameplay trigger separately for defense in depth. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Localization and unused scripted GUI definitions do not create a visible setting; CMM panel visibility is driven by the rebuilt per-group registration list. |
| scope | country | Using any_ally/every_ally/random_ally/ordered_ally as if vanilla registered an active ally scripted list | Iterate known countries and filter the alliance relation explicitly: effects use every_known_country = { limit = { is_allied_with = { target = scope:saved_owner } } ... }; triggers use any_known_country = { is_allied_with = { target = root } ... }. | A commented example in common/scripted_lists is documentation or disabled content, not a registered runtime iterator. Emitting every_ally logged Unknown effect; emitting any_ally logged Unknown trigger type. |
| script | any | local_var:X ?= var:Y, local_var:X > var:Y, or similar dynamic numeric comparisons inside an effect/key iterator when var:Y is owned by the outer scope | Capture the outer-scope value before entering the iterator with set_local_variable, then compare local_var to local_var inside the iterator. For equality, prefer NOT < and NOT > against the captured local instead of ?= var:Y. | During Engineering Department priority-candidate unregister, a variable-map key iterator compared a map-captured local candidate id against the country-scoped current candidate id with local_var:X ?= var:Y. EU5 logged 'Invalid right side during comparison var'. Capturing the country value into a second local before the iterator and comparing locals avoids the RHS parser path. |
| script | any | var:X ?= { this >= prev.var:some_numeric_var } or var:X > prev.var:some_numeric_var inside a trigger/effect limit | Use root.var:some_numeric_var when root is verified to be the original numeric owner, or capture the value before an effect iterator with set_local_variable and compare/use local_var:name inside the switched scope. | Using prev.var:X as the RHS of a numeric comparison can log 'Invalid right side during comparison 'var'' and make the condition fail every tick. In Engineering Department construction this caused the monthly material check to fail and kept the UI stuck on the materials-insufficient pause state. |
| modifier | any | Copying a downstream compatibility mod's complete goods or static-modifier object without comparing its fields against the current vanilla object | Compare current vanilla objects, modifier_type_definitions, and scripted-trigger definitions separately. Preserve valid downstream balance fields: removal from one vanilla object does not mean removal from the engine schema. Replace references to removed triggers with verified current conditions and use vanilla fallback objects for new SOL goods absent from the downstream reference. | In 1.4 inverse_control loses three military penalties but all three modifier types remain valid, so M&T's explicit balance stays intact. Lumber's old scripted trigger disappears and needs replacement. New camels demand needs accounting coverage; wine's new wealth threshold requires explicit negation and a generator guard. Also audit removal of whole action objects: create_colonial_charter disappears from generic_actions while the GUI calls ColonialCharterItem.CreateColonialCharter, so old TRY_REPLACE hooks cannot be assumed to run. The official script diff confirms ai_country_should_colonize is a valid added engine trigger despite its missing scripted-trigger file. |
| scope | pop | Looking for an add_estate effect, government reform, or starting privilege to explain how a population-backed conditional estate such as dhimmi_estate is enabled | Trace the pop type's ordered estate mappings and then the referenced pop-scoped trigger. Vanilla regular pop types map qualifying pops to dhimmi_estate with 'dhimmi_estate = { is_dhimmi = yes }'; is_dhimmi requires a protected non-Muslim religion group, a Muslim owner, and no link to a foreign building. Starting Muslim templates merely grant initial Dhimmi privileges. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Estate type definition, conditional pop membership, country_has_estate, and granted privileges are separate layers. The Dhimmi mapping covers nobles, clergy, burghers, laborers, soldiers, peasants, and slaves, but not tribesmen. |
| scope | location | Relying on debug_log with GuiScope.SetRoot(...).ScriptValue(...) to read numbers during in-game tests | Run effects from the console as `effect c:TAG = { ... }`. Record numbers in the CMF Action Log instead: cache the values as location variables, save the location as scope:cmf_log_loc, and call cmf_log_with_location_values = { action = <loc flag> arg1 = <label flag> value = scope:cmf_log_loc.var:a value2 = ... value3 = ... }. The pane shows two decimals, so pre-scale small values. Market goods totals are readable as `value = "market.goods_demand_in_market(goods:wheat)"` from a location. | User-reported: debug_log output is unreliable for test readings. CMF stores a snapshot per row (cmf_log_num/num2/num3 maps, max 200 rows; clear with cmf_clear_log). action and arg1 are flags rendered via Localize, so each needs a localization key. goods_demand_in_market is the market-wide total from all sources; isolate one country by differencing against a baseline. |
| script | location | construct_building = { ... cost_multiplier = N ... } without cost_multiplier_reason | When construct_building uses cost_multiplier, also provide cost_multiplier_reason = <loc key>. For event-funded free construction, vanilla commonly uses cost_multiplier_reason = "game_concept_event". | EU5 logs a script system error when construct_building has a cost multiplier but no reason key: 'No reason given for the cost multiplier in construct_building effect'. 2026-08-02: upgraded from advisory (documented but unchecked) to lint after this exact mistake recurred — the CMM skip_ceremony ritual-annex construction added 54 new construct_building calls (`cost_multiplier = 0 instant = yes`, one per generic wonder) in tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt without the reason key, even though the identical cost_multiplier = 0 free-construction pattern already existed correctly (with cost_multiplier_reason = "game_concept_event") in the adjacent tv_wonder_ceremony_effects.txt file in the SAME codebase. Added check_construct_building_cost_multiplier_reason, using the existing _find_matching_brace helper to extract each construct_building = { ... } block (handles both the single-line and multi-line forms both used in this codebase) and flag any block containing cost_multiplier but not cost_multiplier_reason. Unit-tested directly against synthetic single-line and multi-line bad/good snippets before trusting it, then verified 0 false positives against the full 288-file repo. |
| modifier | any | EU5 <=1.2 cost modifier name for construction, trade embark/disembark, or stability investment | Use the EU5 1.3 efficiency modifier name: global_build_buildings_efficiency, local_build_buildings_efficiency, local_build_new_buildings_efficiency, local_trade_embark_disembark_efficiency, or stability_cost_efficiency. Flip the sign when preserving old cost penalties/discounts. | EU5 1.3 renamed these cost-style modifiers to positive-good efficiency modifiers. Positive efficiency reduces cost, so old positive cost penalties become negative efficiency and old negative cost discounts become positive efficiency. |
| scope | country | Country-scoped pulse, iterator, scripted trigger, or effect `limit` uses a direct `capital = { ... }` link, or puts sibling `exists = capital` before it expecting trigger short-circuiting | Use the optional link form `capital ?= { ... }` for trigger/limit checks. Trigger blocks do not reliably short-circuit sibling lines, so `exists = capital` beside `capital = { ... }` still allows the direct link to be evaluated and can log `Event target link 'capital' returned an invalid object`. |
| scope | country | Reading `scope:recipient` or an unset actor variable directly inside country interaction accept scoring or a target-mutating effect before the select_trigger has chosen a country | Guard recipient-dependent reads and mutations with `exists = scope:recipient`. Direct reads then report wrong-type `none`, unset variables, or unset `var` scopes while the player is only looking at the country picker. |
| scope | country | any_international_organizations_member_of directly under country_interaction potential | Wrap country-scoped checks in scope:actor = { ... } inside country_interaction potential. | country_interaction potential does not provide a country root for this iterator; direct usage logs inconsistent trigger scopes (invalid vs. country). | scope | country | Reusable country milestone trigger uses root inside a nested market iterator | Use prev inside nested iterators so the target remains the currently checked country when the trigger is called from situation->every_country. | GUI ShowTriggerConditions(PlayerScope.Self) can pass because root is the player country, while the monthly situation checker calls the same trigger from a situation root. | script | location | Event-created artwork uses create_art with only artist/quality and no explicit type/key, expecting a reliable tooltip and artwork name | For event-created named artworks, provide an explicit work_of_art_type and key with matching main_menu localization. Vanilla named artworks pair create_art with both type = work_of_art_type:<type> and key = <loc_key>. Visible if/else_if branches can render the real create_art tooltip while still selecting the appropriate art type by artist_type. |
| script | any | add_country_to_international_organization = prev or set_leader_country = prev placed as direct (visible) children of create_international_organization = { ... } | Wrap add_country_to_international_organization and set_leader_country inside hidden_effect = { } within the create_international_organization block: create_international_organization = { type = ... hidden_effect = { add_country_to_international_organization = prev  set_leader_country = prev } } | The vanilla tooltip strings THIRD_ADD_TO_INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION_EFFECT and SET_LEADER_EFFECT use INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION.GetName / .GetLeaderTitle. When these effects are visible children of create_international_organization, the engine evaluates the tooltip before the IO is fully instantiated, causing 'Promote INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION returned nullptr' errors. Wrapping in hidden_effect suppresses tooltip generation and eliminates the errors. The outer create_international_organization tooltip (CREATE_INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION_EFFECT) still shows correctly. |
| script | building | Custom generic-action building buttons rely only on can_build_building and do not repeat the building max-level / in-construction cap check | Add an explicit trigger shared by the action allow, select_trigger visible/enabled, and final effect guard. For one-per-location selectors, also filter existing buildings and any_buildings_in_location with building_levels_under_construction >= 1. | Building max_levels can stop normal construction but a custom IO/generic-action button can still spend its scripted price unless its action and effect repeat the cap logic. | gui | scripted_triggers | custom_description { text = KEY  has_variable = X  var:X >= N  has_variable = Y  var:Y >= M } — multiple independent conditions collapsed into one block | Use one custom_tooltip block per condition group at the top level of the scripted_trigger body (implicit AND). | localization | any | Calling a Customizable Localization block directly off a bare `ROOT` in event/effect loc text: `[ROOT.Custom('some_block')]`. | `docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md`'s own documented Customizable Localization example already shows the correct form: `[ROOT.GetCountry.Custom('ruler_residence')]` — `Custom` must be chained off a properly-typed scope link (`GetCountry`, `GetCharacter`, etc. matching the block's own `type = country`/`character`/...), not directly off the bare `ROOT` keyword, even in an already-country-scoped event/effect. The engine cannot resolve `Custom` as a function on the untyped root object: `pdx_data_factory.cpp: Could not find data system function 'Custom' in 'ROOT.Custom(...)'`, followed by `pdx_data_localize.cpp: Data error in loc string '<key>'`. Insert the matching `Get<Type>` link between `ROOT` and `.Custom(...)`. | 2026-07-15: user reported the Unique Wonder Ceremony's 8-stage pay/decline option text (tv_wonder_ceremony_events.txt, event tv_engineering_department.9300+ options .a/.b) errored on every stage; error.log confirmed 'Could not find data system function Custom in ROOT.Custom(...)'. The KB section this exact pattern was designed from (see risk_cards/wonders.md rule 26, added when the 8-stage ceremony's per-wonder pay/decline text was migrated to Customizable Localization) already documents the correct `ROOT.GetCountry.Custom(...)` form via its own worked example — the ceremony generator (gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_l_english.py/gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_l_simp_chinese.py) simply didn't chain the `.GetCountry` link when it was written. Grepping the whole repo for the same bare `[ROOT.Custom(` pattern found the identical bug already latent (never yet triggered in this playthrough) in scripts/philosophy_debate_codegen.py's tooltip_change_text, feeding the Academy Philosophy Debate's seated/vacated custom_description text (tv_academy_debate_group_seated_text and 7 sibling keys in tv_academy_philosophy_debate_l_english.yml/_simp_chinese.yml) — fixed both generators and regenerated all four affected localization files. Promoted to detectability: lint since the bare-ROOT pattern is a simple, unambiguous regex with no legitimate use (Custom always requires a typed link). |
| modifier | any | Defining a custom modifier type without a matching common/modifier_icons entry | For every mod-defined modifier type, add a same-key entry in main_menu/common/modifier_icons with at least positive = "gfx/interface/icons/modifier_types/<vanilla_or_custom_icon>.dds". Reuse a close vanilla icon when no custom asset is needed. | EU5 loads modifier icon mappings separately from modifier_type_definitions. A valid custom modifier type can still log 'Missing Icon for Modifier : <name>' at startup until common/modifier_icons maps the key to a DDS. 2026-08-02: upgraded from advisory to lint after scripts/validate.py's check_modifier_type_definition_loc_and_icon_coverage was added (WARNING severity, since a missing icon does not block loading, unlike missing MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME/DESC localization which is an ERROR in the same check) — see missing_localization_for_new_modifier_price_auto_modifier_definitions. |
| gui | any | Removing custom_tooltip from event options (wrong fix for dotted key confusion) | Keep custom_tooltip; verify key format via Step 2/3 — dotted suffix IS valid in event options | AI wrongly removed custom_tooltip thinking dotted key format was invalid. Never remove; only verify format. |
| gui | any | A raw comparison against a conditionally-set variable (e.g. `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level` where the RHS is only ever `set_variable`'d under one specific branch) inside a `custom_tooltip` block, when that trigger is also used as an effect's own `if.limit` (not just a generic_action's `allow`). | Follow the Milestone Trigger Tooltip Pattern already documented in CLAUDE.md: guard every raw variable comparison inside a `custom_tooltip` with `has_variable = X` in the same AND block before comparing `var:X`, exactly like the sibling branch in the same trigger already did (`var:tv_wonder_level ?= { this >= 1 }` is the safe form; the broken branch instead used a bare `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level`). This is not just cosmetic: when the same trigger is reused as an effect's `if.limit` (not only a generic_action's `allow`), the tooltip-preview pass that renders the button's effect chain walks the whole body in order — if it errors out evaluating this `limit`'s custom_tooltip mid-pass, it can corrupt or blank rendering for a completely unrelated LATER effect in the same preview (observed: `destroy_building_forcefully = "building(building_type:tv_wonder_labor_camp|scope:tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner)"`, several lines further down in the same `if` body, showed a garbled/placeholder building name instead of the Labor Camp). Fix the root unguarded comparison rather than chasing the seemingly unrelated downstream symptom. | 2026-07-15: user reported the 'must have increased at least one level since starting/resuming construction' gate on the Finish Construction button was both ineffective and caused continuous pre-evaluation error spam while sitting in the construction stage, plus the button's own effect tooltip showed a corrupted 'X will be destroyed' line implying a missing building reference. Root cause: tv_wonder_project_base_level is only set via tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect when var:tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2 at site-selection time, so for any wonder not in that specific resume/upgrade mode the variable never exists at all, yet the trigger's first AND-branch compared against it unconditionally. Fixed by adding `has_variable = tv_wonder_project_base_level` to that branch, matching the safe idiom the trigger's own second branch already used one line below. The elsewhere-unguarded twin `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level` inside tv_wonder_set_current_project_priority_candidate_mode_effect (same file, ~line 2164) was left alone: it is a plain effect `if.limit`, not a custom_tooltip, so ordinary AND short-circuiting protects it at runtime and no player-facing tooltip walks it. |
| localization | character | Defining a second character_title_prefix block in a separate customizable_localization file | Copy/generate the full vanilla character_title.txt and insert custom title text entries into the original character_title_prefix block. | Top-level customizable localization keys are unique database entries, not additive merge blocks. A duplicate block is ignored, so custom character title prefixes never load. |
| compatibility | top-level database object | Using a later filename, a zz_ prefix, or a lower playlist position as a universal way to make any INJECT/REPLACE database edit win | Separate exact-path file overwrites from database object edits. For object edits, compare operation type first (INJECT_OR_CREATE -> REPLACE_OR_CREATE -> TRY_INJECT -> TRY_REPLACE -> INJECT -> REPLACE), then use filename order only when the operation types are identical. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Database entries are batched by operation type before filename ordering. Exact-path collisions use a separate whole-file rule: mod over base/DLC, then the lower mod in the playlist. INJECT is top-level append rather than nested deep merge, and scripted effects/triggers treat INJECT like replacement. |
| events | any | Deferring an option/button effect's reward to a `trigger_event_silently = { id = X days = N }` call, when the deferred event's `immediate` effect is not otherwise proven too heavy/unsafe to run inline, purely to 'match the async pattern used elsewhere.' | EU5's generic_action/event-option tooltip pre-evaluation only walks the option's own `effect =` body — it does not follow a `trigger_event_silently`/`trigger_event_non_silently` call into the target event's `immediate` block. So any reward effect placed only inside that deferred event is executed correctly at runtime but never rendered in the button's tooltip preview. The Unique Wonder Ceremony's shared 8-stage framework (docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 20) had this exact asymmetry: stages 2/4 called their reward dispatch effects inline in `tv_wonder_ceremony_advance_to_stage_{2,4}_effect`, rendering both cost and reward in the tooltip, while stage 8 only scheduled a hidden completion event (`tv_engineering_department.9308`) whose `immediate` contained the real payoff — so stage 8's button showed only the cost. Before accepting a deferral like this as necessary (per rule 9/31's guidance for genuinely heavy/unsafe chains — `destroy_building`, `create_international_organization`, character-title promotion, or other operations documented to break tooltip preview), verify the deferred effect body actually contains one of those unsafe operations or a proven pre-evaluation error. In this case it did not: the completion chain was `add_country_modifier` plus variable bookkeeping and one `every_international_organizations_member_of` read-only loop — lighter than stage 4's own inline 66-branch `construct_building` switch, and the identical chain (`tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect`) already ran inline from several `on_action` hidden_effects for every ritual in progress. Fixed by calling `tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect` inline in stage 8's advance effect (matching stages 2/4) and deleting the now-unnecessary hidden event and `COMPLETION_EVENT_ID` constant. | 2026-07-16: user reported that in the 8-step Unique Wonder Ceremony (applies to 121 unique wonders), steps 2 and 4's options rendered both cost and reward in the tooltip, but step 8 only rendered cost. Traced to `append_advance_stage_effects()` in gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_effects.py: steps 2/4 call their reward effects inline, step 8 instead only called `trigger_event_silently = { id = tv_engineering_department.9308 days = 1 }`, whose immediate block held the real completion reward. When the user asked 'why is this heavy, isn't it just applying a country modifier,' traced the full completion chain (`tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect` -> `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect` -> completion/static-modifier effects + `tv_wonder_finalize_effect`) and confirmed it really is just that (plus bookkeeping), with no `construct_building`/location loops/world-news calls, and confirmed via git history that the deferral had no stage-8-specific incident behind it — it was introduced for consistency with the async pattern used for stage 4's later building-finalization event and for Pharos/Hagia's bespoke rituals. Removed the deferral, deleted event 9308 and the unused `COMPLETION_EVENT_ID` constant, and updated `test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py`'s assertions accordingly. |
| trigger | market | Using demands_goods_by_pops = goods:<good> as a generic consumed-good or market pop-demand test | For a known market, scope to the good and use is_demanded_in_market_by_pops = scope:<market>. Reserve demands_goods_by_pops for checks where pop demand exceeds supply. | The trigger localization for demands_goods_by_pops describes a shortage condition, not mere consumption. The goods-scope trigger is_demanded_in_market_by_pops accepts a market scope target. |
| effect | any | Restoring/backfilling a set of source counters (e.g. tv_wonder_foundation_units/body_units/function_units/decoration_units, read back from location_building_level for an existing pre-built building) without also re-running the effect that recomputes the derived cache variable (tv_wonder_level) computed from those counters, then immediately snapshotting that stale/still-unset derived variable elsewhere (tv_wonder_project_base_level = var:tv_wonder_level). | Whenever an effect restores/backfills the SOURCE counters a derived variable is computed from, immediately re-run the dedicated recompute effect (here, tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect) before anything downstream reads the derived variable. tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect called tv_wonder_restore_locked_wonder_final_building_state_effect and tv_wonder_sync_units_from_buildings_effect (which correctly restore tv_wonder_*_units from the location's existing building levels) but never called tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect before capturing tv_wonder_project_base_level = var:tv_wonder_level a few lines later — so for a wonder resuming construction on a site with pre-existing progress (an existing-at-game-start unique wonder like Jerusalem's Dome of the Rock, initial_level > 0, or any wonder whose site already has partial levels), tv_wonder_level was still 0/unset at the exact moment of the snapshot, so the set_variable using that non-existent source silently produced no tv_wonder_project_base_level at all. Added the missing tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect call between the restore/sync calls and the snapshot. The debug/repair tool tv_wonder_reinitialize_building_state_core_effect already had the correct order (sync then update_wonder_level) — that established precedent is what confirmed the fix, and it was also missing the equivalent project_mode==2 + missing-base_level repair, which was added there too so players already stuck mid-playthrough (base_level already silently missing) can recover via the tv_reinitialize_mod debug action instead of needing a fresh project. | 2026-07-15: user reported the 'must gain a level since resuming' Finish Construction gate stayed permanently blocked while expanding the pre-existing Dome of the Rock at Jerusalem, after the has_variable guard fix (see [[custom_tooltip_unguarded_comparison_corrupts_later_effect_tooltip_in_same_pass]]) correctly stopped the pre-evaluation crash but exposed that tv_wonder_project_base_level was never actually being set for this exact scenario. Traced through tv_wonder_initialize_existing_unique_wonders_effect (seeds only the survey/final-building-level maps at game start, not the country's own _units/level variables) -> the priority-project/proposal pipeline (correctly sets tv_wonder_project_mode=2 and tv_wonder_resume_project=1) -> tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect (correctly restores _units from the site's actual building levels via two dedicated restore effects, but never recomputed tv_wonder_level from them before the snapshot line two calls later). Confirmed data/unique_wonders.yaml id 103's only final_buildings entry (style 1 = tv_wonder_unique_dome_of_the_rock_inaugurated, initial_level 1) is exactly what the restore effects look up, so the restore itself works fine — only the missing recompute call was the gap. |
| gui | any | Calling a scripted_effect that contains `destroy_building`/`destroy_building_forcefully` directly (even through nested scripted_effect calls) from inside a player-clickable generic_action's own `effect = { ... }` body — the block the button's hover/tooltip preview pre-evaluates — even after the target is resolved through a correct implicit link (`owner`, `root`, etc., see [[destroy_building_target_via_custom_saved_scope_breaks_tooltip_preview]]). | The implicit-link fix only cures the case where the destroy target itself was mis-resolved (a custom `save_scope_as`). It does not cure the separate case where the destroy call sits directly inside the button's own tooltip-pre-evaluated effect chain: `tv_wonder_finish_construction` (generic_actions/tv_engineering_department_actions.txt) has `effect = { scope:actor = { tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect = yes } }`, and `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` used to call `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` inline before scheduling `trigger_event_non_silently = { id = tv_engineering_department.202 days = 1 }`. Because the destroy call lived inside the directly-evaluated chain, the button's preview pass still tried (and failed) to promote TARGET_BUILDING for the THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT loc string, even with the owner-link fix already applied. Fixed by removing `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` from `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` entirely and adding it to event `tv_engineering_department.202`'s `option` effect instead — the event already fires asynchronously via the existing `trigger_event_non_silently ... days = 1` call, so the destroy now runs after the button's own preview pass has already rendered, from within an already-scoped event option rather than a hover-evaluated generic_action effect chain. This generalizes rule 9 in `docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md` (keep `hidden_effect` light, defer heavy chains to a triggered event's `immediate`) to also cover a generic_action's own non-hidden `effect =` body: any destroy_building call anywhere in that chain is pre-evaluated for the button tooltip, so it must be deferred to a separately-triggered event rather than run inline. | 2026-07-16: error.log kept showing the TARGET_BUILDING/THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT error after the owner-link fix (commit 431810e7) had already landed, proving the two bugs were distinct and the first fix alone was insufficient. Moved `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` out of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` and into `tv_engineering_department.202`'s option effect. All three callers of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (the player button, an on_action auto-completion path, and an events.txt call) still schedule event 202 identically, so the demolition still happens on every completion path — it is now deferred until the event's option resolves instead of running synchronously inside the completion effect. |
| events | any | Deferring a `destroy_building`/`destroy_building_forcefully` call out of a generic_action's own `effect =` body into a separately-triggered event's `option`, and assuming that deferral alone (without also using `hidden_effect`) is enough to escape tooltip/effect-preview pre-evaluation. | Event options get the exact same hover/tooltip pre-evaluation walk that generic_action buttons do — the fix for [[destroy_building_forcefully_in_button_effect_chain_fails_tooltip_preview_even_with_owner_link]] moved `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect` out of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (a button's own effect body) into `tv_engineering_department.202`'s `option`, but the option still called it directly (not inside `hidden_effect`), so the same pre-evaluation error persisted unchanged. `hidden_effect` is the only block the pre-evaluation pass actually skips, regardless of how many scopes/events the real call is deferred through. Fixed by wrapping the call in `hidden_effect = { tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes }` and adding a sibling `custom_tooltip = <key>` line so the option still shows correct static tooltip text — this is vanilla's own pattern (e.g. `earthquake_events.txt`'s `earthquake_events_minor.a`/`.b` options pair `hidden_effect` with `custom_tooltip` the same way). | 2026-07-16: user reported the wonder-finish worksite demolition still errored even after it had already been moved into event tv_engineering_department.202's option (per the rule 31 fix). The generalization is: deferring a heavy/unsafe effect call to *any* visible effect body — button, event option, whatever — never escapes pre-evaluation by itself; only `hidden_effect` does. Wrapped the call in `hidden_effect` and added `custom_tooltip = tv_engineering_department.202.a.tt` (with matching English/Simplified Chinese loc) so the option keeps a correct static tooltip. validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report passed with 0 errors/warnings after the fix. |
| gui | any | destroy_building/destroy_building_forcefully targeting `building(building_type:X|scope:Y)` where `scope:Y` was captured earlier in the SAME effect via `save_scope_as = Y`, instead of an implicit link (`owner`, `root`, `scope:actor`, `prev`) that is naturally available without a prior save. | Every vanilla destroy_building/destroy_building_forcefully call resolves its `building(building_type:X|<link>)` second argument through an implicit, framework-provided link (`owner`, `root`, `prev`, `scope:actor`, `scope:target`, `scope:recipient`) — never through a custom name captured by a preceding `save_scope_as` inside the same effect (confirmed by grepping every vanilla usage in reference_game_files, zero counter-examples). The engine's runtime executor happily resolves a custom `scope:Y` reference and destroys the correct building, so this pattern looks correct in testing — but the effect-tooltip PREVIEW pass (used to render "here's what will happen" for a player-clickable generic_action) does not replay `save_scope_as` as a real side effect before trying to resolve the destroy target for display, so it fails to promote the engine's internal TARGET_BUILDING placeholder and the tooltip renders garbled/placeholder text instead of the real building name — while the actual click-through destruction still works perfectly. If the destroy call already executes from inside the target location's own scope (e.g. `var:tv_wonder_site ?= { ... }`), just use the location's own `owner` link directly and delete the now-unnecessary `save_scope_as`. | 2026-07-15: user reported that after the finish-construction gate fix landed, the button's effect tooltip still showed a garbled "X will be destroyed" line for the Labor Camp, but confirmed the actual click-through correctly destroyed the building every time — the split between broken preview and correct runtime execution was the key clue that ruled out a logic bug and pointed at a preview-only scope resolution gap. tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect did `save_scope_as = tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner` at country scope, then entered `var:tv_wonder_site ?= { ... }` (the location) and referenced `scope:tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner` in the destroy_building_forcefully target — replaced with the location's own `owner` link (matching vanilla's catholic.txt seat_of_cardinal precedent exactly, called from the same location-scoped position) and deleted the save_scope_as entirely. NOT yet fixed: the same save_scope_as-then-scope:X_owner pattern is used ~926 times across tv_wonder_module_effects.txt (tv_wonder_module_owner) for the generic/module wonder building-consolidation system — that whole family is only ever called from tv_wonder_reinitialize_building_state_core_effect (the tv_reinitialize_mod debug action) and every_owned_location, not from a normal player-facing construction button, so the same tooltip-preview corruption is plausible but unconfirmed there and was left alone rather than blindly refactoring 926 call sites without a reported symptom — check this file first if a similar garbled destroy-building tooltip is ever reported near the reinitialize debug action or module-building consolidation. 2026-07-16 follow-up: the `owner`-link fix alone did NOT clear the error — `docs/error_log/error.log` kept logging `Promote 'TARGET_BUILDING' returned nullptr` / `Data error in loc string 'THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT'` because `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect` was still called directly from `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect`, which is itself the generic_action `tv_wonder_finish_construction`'s own `effect =` body — the exact chain the button's hover/tooltip preview pre-evaluates. See `[[destroy_building_forcefully_in_button_effect_chain_fails_tooltip_preview_even_with_owner_link]]` for the real fix (move the call out of the button's effect chain entirely). |

The real EU5/Jomini equivalent is a bare global variable: `set_global_variable = X` (shorthand for `set_global_variable = { name = X value = yes }`) and `has_global_variable = X`, both `Supported Scopes: none` (usable from anywhere). Confirmed extensively in vanilla (e.g. `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/on_action/_hardcoded.txt:564` `set_global_variable = byzantine_empire_fallen`) and in community mods (`reference_mods/3613232232/in_game/common/on_action/pp_game_start.txt`'s `pp_mod_welcome_situation_completed`/`pp_mod_welcome_situation_pending` pattern, which this mod's own standalone-mod-bootstrap event copied). | Found 2026-07 while implementing the standalone Engineering Department mod's game-start/game-load bootstrap (data/pulse_registry.yaml + src_engineering_department/in_game/common/on_action/tv_engineering_department_standalone_on_action.txt), which needed a save-wide 'has this mod's intro event already run' marker not tied to any one country. The doc entries for set_global_variable/has_global_variable themselves quote the generic set_variable/has_variable usage examples verbatim (a documentation copy-paste artifact, not a syntax difference) — trust the real vanilla usage sites over the doc's literal example text. |

The engine shows its own native confirmation dialog before running the action's `effect`, reusing the SAME `title`/`description` loc keys the action's `action_button` already sets for its tooltip — no extra loc keys, no pending variable, no custom overlay widget tree needed. `main_menu/gui/confirm_window.gui`'s `ConfirmWindow` singleton (the thing the original search was looking for and correctly ruled out as not parametrizable) is unrelated to this — `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold` is a different, per-generic_action-level field, not a GUI window. | Implemented 2026-07-13 for the Victory Path Tree's click-to-unlock flow, then corrected same-day. First pass searched reference_game_files for a reusable confirmation window, found only the hardcoded `ConfirmWindow` singleton (no mod-facing hook for a custom on-confirm effect), and WRONGLY concluded no native mechanism existed at all — missing the separate, un-searched-for `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold` field on `generic_action` itself. Built the pending-variable (`tv_tree_pending_id`) + shared overlay (`append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay`) workaround instead, which then needed two more anti_patterns entries (`gui_sibling_overlay_outside_datacontext_scope_loses_context`, `blockoverride_content_indirectly_inside_vbox_via_base_template`) just to debug the overlay's own GUI structure bugs. The user flagged that EU5 natively supports click-and-confirm and asked for the entire workaround to be deleted; verified via the generic_actions readme and two vanilla examples, then removed `PENDING_VAR`/`CONFIRM_ACTION`/`CANCEL_ACTION`/`node_uid`/`PATH_ORDER` from `victory_tree_node_codegen.py`, the whole `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` function and its call from `gen_tv_victory_situation_gui.py`, and the two now-dead action ids from `gen_messagetypes.py`. Lesson: when a reference search comes back negative for the FIRST mechanism you think of (a modal window), check the readme/field list of the RELEVANT script object (here, `generic_actions/readme.txt`) for a purpose-built field before concluding 'no native support' and reaching for a custom GUI workaround. |

Optionally also init via on_game_start as defense-in-depth, but the wrapper is the load-bearing fix. | EU5 evaluates script_values during file-load validation BEFORE any on_action (including on_game_start) fires. An unguarded global_var read produces 'Failed to fetch variable', 'Event target link global_var returned an unset scope', and 'Got value of type none' errors that propagate up to every caller script_value. on_game_start init alone cannot fix this — the validation pass runs earlier. Wrapping with has_global_variable + safe default is the only robust approach. |

Both quoted strings and block wrappers are broken; direct assignment is the only valid form. |

A bare `widget` with neither measures 0, and `minimumsize` on the parent does not rescue it because the parent is measured before that. Either use a `vbox`/`hbox` (which do size to their children) as the framed container, or put `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` on the child. | Cost a full prototype round: the SOL tooltip hero rendered as `widget 400x0` with all its content scrolled over the section below, and probes isolated it (a sized child measures; the same child inside a bare widget measures 0). Same rule applies to art placed as a bare `background` on a childless element - emit it as a sized child widget instead (2026-10-13). |
| gui | any | Declaring one block in a type and instantiating the type five times, expecting five different values - e.g. five table cells each with its own `blockoverride "cell_value"` | A `blockoverride` replaces a block *entirely*, and an instance's own override beats one inherited from outside, so sibling instances of the same type each keep their own default. Getting it wrong silently duplicates elements rather than erroring: emitting five cells *alongside* a block that already held five defaults produced ten per row (2026-10-13). |
| gui | any | Using GUI And(...) or Or(...) with three or more operands | Use And3(...) / Or3(...) for exactly three operands, or nest binary And(...) / Or(...) calls for larger expressions. | EU5 GUI And() and Or() are binary helpers, not variadic. And(a,b,c) fails conversion with 'Function And expected 2 arguments, got 3'. Pattern left empty because commas inside typed comparison calls make static regex detection noisy. |

| gui | generic_actions | Passing an IO member-row country action parameter as [Country.Self] | Pass country targets that generic actions read as scope:target through [Country.MakeScope], and place the parameter on the action button so both tooltip/allow evaluation and the click action receive the same scope. | gui | any | Emitting one static, near-duplicate GUI `widget` block per data-table row (e.g. one `visible = mechanic_id == N` branch per wonder) purely to display a (label, value) pair that is otherwise homogeneous across rows -- e.g. the pre-2026-07-13 Engineering Department 'suitability location conditions' block, 54 near-identical widgets (~4000 generated lines) differing only in loc key and a flat bonus number per wonder. | GUI expressions can build a dynamic composite key at runtime with `MakeScopeFlag(Concatenate('<system_prefix>_', Concatenate(<id_string>, '_<slot>')))` and read a value baked into a global variable map with `GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap('<map_name>', <composite_key>).GetValue`, matching a `flag:<system_prefix>_<id>_<slot>` key written at generation time via `add_to_global_variable_map`. Give the flag a named prefix (e.g. `tv_wonder_suitability_`), not a bare `<id>_<slot>` number pair -- a bare-number flag key is indistinguishable from a concatenation bug when it shows up in error.log's benign 'flag is set but never used' notice (see the sibling advisory note on this same entry). Combine with `text = "[Localize(Concatenate('PREFIX_', ToString_int32(FixedPointToInt(<id_value>))))]"` to resolve a dynamic loc key from a small numeric enum id. This collapses N per-row static branches into one shared widget plus two small generation-time-static lookup tables (condition/label id, numeric weight), keyed `flag:<system_prefix>_<entity_id>_<slot>`. Only applies when the *display* is homogeneous (same widget shape, different data); do not use this to flatten genuinely heterogeneous per-target logic (e.g. per-wonder survey trigger scripts checking different location conditions), which the Structural Fidelity Rule still requires to stay as explicit branches. If the user wants the per-key 'flag is set but never used' log noise eliminated rather than accepted: `has_flag` is NOT a valid EU5 trigger at all -- Jomini has no EU4-style boolean flag system, and referencing `has_flag = <key>` anywhere logs 'Unknown trigger type: has_flag' on load (this was a bug in the original fix attempt, not a working scanner-satisfier). Since a `flag:<name>` variable-map key is a string-typed map key, not a scope flag, the real reference is `is_key_in_global_variable_map = { name = <map_name> target = flag:<key> }` against the map that keys off it (e.g. `tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type`), placed inside a scripted trigger/effect that is itself reachable from a real on_action/event/decision entry point (e.g. called once from an existing on_game_start/on_game_load hook) -- an unreachable/never-called scripted trigger or effect does NOT satisfy the scanner even though it textually contains the reference; the engine's own message says so explicitly ('Use in unused scripted triggers and effects also does not count'). See tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger for the working pattern: one generated trigger ORs `is_key_in_global_variable_map` across every baked key (always true since every key is baked at init, harmless), called once via a no-op `if` (`remove_variable` on a name that's never set) from tv_wonder_initialize_index_on_game_start/_on_game_load. | Implemented 2026-07-13 for the Engineering Department suitability panel: two new global variable maps (tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type, tv_wonder_suitability_weight) keyed flag:tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot 1..5>, baked once from suitability_knowledge_for_wonder() at generation time, replacing the 54-wonder GUI loop with one wonder-agnostic widget. Net effect: tv_engineering_department.gui dropped from 9041 to 5168 lines. validate.py cannot execute Concatenate/MakeScopeFlag/GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap (no Clausewitz GUI expression interpreter in the pipeline), so any future change to this pattern still needs manual in-game verification per wonder/reveal-tier combination. In-game confirmed 2026-07-13: on load, error.log/jomini_effect.cpp:1162 logs one 'Flag "<key>" is set but is never used' line per baked flag key (270 = 54 mechanics x 5 slots). This is EXPECTED and benign -- the engine's flag-usage scanner only tracks references from scripted triggers/effects, and its own message already documents that localization use doesn't count; GUI-file reads (GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap in .gui) are the same kind of blind spot. A first attempt to silence it by adding a `has_flag` reference inside a scripted trigger that was never called from anywhere still logged all 270 warnings, confirming the engine message's own caveat that unused/unreachable scripted triggers and effects also don't count. The user then asked for the warnings to actually be eliminated rather than accepted. Fix (same day): added `tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger` (generated, one `OR` of `has_flag = tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot>` per baked key) and called it once from the existing, already-reachable `tv_wonder_initialize_index_on_game_start`/`_on_game_load` hidden_effect via a no-op `if` (`remove_variable` on a variable name that is never set elsewhere, so the branch has zero side effects whether or not the always-false OR matches). Making the *containing* trigger reachable, not just the flag literal present in a text file, is what satisfies the scanner. `SUITABILITY_KNOWLEDGE_ROW_SLOTS` and the `tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot>` key format were centralized into `scripts/wonder_mechanics/suitability_conditions.py` (`suitability_row_key()`) so both generators stay in sync. Do not add a per-flag repair anywhere else, and do not move this reference into a hot/monthly path -- it only needs to run once per game start/load.
Correction (2026-07-13, same-day follow-up): the `has_flag` reference itself was invalid EU5 syntax -- error.log/pdx_persistent_reader.cpp:289 logged 'Unknown trigger type: has_flag' once per line (270 occurrences) on parse, before the flag-usage scanner even ran. `has_flag` does not exist in Jomini; there is no EU4-style boolean flag system. Since `flag:<key>` here is a string-typed `global_variable_map` key namespace, not a scope flag, the reference must instead be `is_key_in_global_variable_map = { name = tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type target = flag:tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot> }` (vanilla precedent: reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/country_interactions/hre.txt:897). `append_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger` in the generator was updated to emit this instead; the OR now evaluates true for every entry (all keys are baked at init) rather than always-false, which remains harmless since this trigger is a static reference, not real gameplay logic. |

| gui | any | Putting ignoreinvisible on a plain widget/uberwidget | Use ignoreinvisible on layout containers such as hbox/vbox/scrollwidget content, not on a plain image or wrapper widget. To hide an image area, control the parent widget's visible expression and let the actual image children use their own visible expressions. | A plain widget logs Property 'ignoreinvisible' not handled and Error setting properties for the uberwidget. The attribute is accepted on layout containers elsewhere in the same panel, but not on the generic image wrapper. |
| gui | international_organization | Checking an IO member-row country with IsIOLeaderCountry(Country.Self), or comparing Country.Self directly to GetLeaderCountry without .Self | Compare matching GUI objects, e.g. Not(ObjectsEqual(Country.Self, InternationalOrganizationsView.GetInternationalOrganization.GetLeaderCountry.Self)). | IsIOLeaderCountry(Country.Self) produced FetchData failed in the IO member row context. The original ObjectsEqual without .Self did not hide the leader because it compared different object shapes. |
| gui | international_organization | Filtering InternationalOrganizationsView.GetMemberItems by comparing MemberTypeItem.GetName to a special-status display helper | Use real MemberTypeItem predicates such as MemberTypeItem.IsAllMembers for the outer group, then apply country-row filters inside MemberTypeItem.GetCountries. | The display-name comparison against ShowSpecialStatusName('tv_alliance_member_status') hid every Diplomatic Alliance member row. MemberTypeItem.GetName is not a reliable selector for custom special-status groups in this panel. |
| gui | any | Copying a 1.3 location-window action button into EU5 1.4 with header_action_button_left and actor/parameter/left_*_action fields | Use the verified 1.4 header_action_button_left_uber type, PdxGuiWidget.IsUberButtonVisible/Enabled, and a scripted_action_tooltip block. Keep the conversion in the generator and assert the expected number of migrated buttons. | The 1.4 type is not a rename-only compatibility alias: the old action properties were removed with the type. Stable SOL and M&T location-window generators now apply the migration to their third-party snapshots. |
| gui | country | Trying to bind a GUI row/list visible= expression to a scripted_trigger's result (e.g. an age-gate or has_advance check) so the display can filter a candidate list | GUI expressions cannot evaluate scripted triggers at all (see docs/knowledge/risk_cards/gui.md rule 2, which covers has_variable specifically — this generalizes to any scripted_trigger). Mirror the trigger's result into a country variable via a scripted_effect, and refresh that effect from a real lifecycle point (join/founding effect for immediate correctness, plus the feature's existing monthly_country_pulse hook for ongoing correctness) — never from a GUI/tooltip read path. Bind the GUI expression to the mirrored variable instead. | Discovered building the Academy of Sciences 'Potential Projects' card: the age-frontier eligibility trigger and has_advance are only readable in script scope, so the card needed a tv_research_open_age_ceiling variable (contiguous-prefix simplification of 4 age-gate triggers into one int) and per-advance tv_advance_<id>_researched variables, refreshed from the join effect and the existing tv_academy_of_sciences_monthly_pulse. Once mirrored, register the mirror variable with [[cmf_suppress_for_confirmed_dynamic_reference_variables]] — the GUI read does not count as a script-side use, so error.log still flags it as 'set but never used'. |
| gui | location | Using location.owner inside a scripted effect rendered by ShowScriptedEffectForScope with LocationView.GetLocation.MakeScope.Self | When the GUI passes the location itself as the effect scope, enter the owning country with owner ?= { ... }. Reserve location.owner for contexts that expose a separate location event target, such as building on_built. | In location-window tooltips the scripted effect root is already the location. A dotted location.owner chain is parsed as an event-target link named location, which is invalid from a location root and can also leave COUNTRY.GetName unavailable for add_country_modifier tooltip localization. |
| gui | any | Calling .GetMarket.GetLocation in GUI expressions to access a market's hub location | Use .GetMarket.GetCenterLocation only when the UI truly needs the trade-center Location object. For market-keyed caches, prefer global_variable_map keyed by Market.MakeScope instead of center-location variables. | Exhaustive search of reference_game_files for .GetMarket.GetLocation returned zero matches; .GetMarket.GetCenterLocation is the canonical accessor for navigation to the center location. It is not a recommended cache storage path for SOL market data. |
| gui | any | Requiring every upstream GUI snapshot to contain the legacy 1.3 action-button count, so a current snapshot that is already migrated to 1.4 fails before the SOL integration button is inserted | Make the shared GUI migration idempotent: migrate legacy markers when present, accept a fully migrated 1.4 snapshot after checking that removed APIs are absent, and reject mixed snapshots that still contain removed calls. | The current Glorp UI `glorp.ui.rio` snapshot already uses `header_action_button_left_uber`, `scripted_action_tooltip`, and `ShowRoadBuilder`; the same generator still needs exact-count migration for older M&T or historical snapshots. |
| gui | texture | Putting modify_texture directly in a layout/type block such as a card_bg override that expands into a vbox | Put modify_texture inside a rendered background, icon, or other image-layer block. The ceremony-card generator emitted it beside using = bg_paper_card_situations in a card_bg block that the local card type expands into a vbox, causing 16 Property 'modify_texture' not handled messages and a failed ubervbox property setup. Context-sensitive GUI parsing is required to lint this safely. |
| gui | any | GetVariable('tv_governed_area').GetArea.GetName — navigating from a scope variable that stores an Area to call .GetName directly via .GetArea | GetVariable(...).GetArea is not a valid EU5 GUI accessor.
