Location.GetArea.GetName IS valid when the Location type is a proper GUI-layer typed object (e.g. from GetLocation on a typed view). | The accessor .GetArea exists on the Location GUI type but not on scope variable wrappers returned by GetVariable(). Chain: GetVariable('loc_var').GetLocation.GetArea.GetNameWithNoTooltip works because GetLocation returns a typed Location object. Pattern rule narrowed to only flag GetVariable(...).GetArea. to allow Location.GetArea. chains. |
| gui | any | GetGoods('key'), GetGoodsFromKey, or MakeGoods('key') in GUI expressions to construct a Goods object from a string literal | Goods datacontext must come from a data model accessor chain (e.g. GoodsMarketEntry.GetGoods, Trade.GetGoods, GoodsItem.GetGoods in a foreach); there is no string-to-Goods lookup function in EU5 GUI | Exhaustive search of all reference_game_files *.gui files found zero occurrences of any string-to-Goods constructor; all goods access is typed accessor chains only |
| gui | any | Trying to draw a prerequisite-to-node connector line between two arbitrary widget positions using engine GUI widgets/properties (e.g. a rotated stretched sprite, a 'line' widget type, or a dynamically-angled background). | EU5 .gui has no rotation/line-drawing primitive outside coat-of-arms rendering (verified: grepping reference_game_files for `rotation\s*=` only hits coat_of_arms files). When node positions are fixed at generation time (e.g. an authored skill-tree layout), pre-render all connector curves as one transparent full-canvas PNG/DDS overlay in Python instead, then place it as a plain `background` widget layered under the node buttons. See scripts/gen_tv_victory_tree_connectors.py, which ports the Catmull-Rom curve math from towards_victory_editor_web/static/victory_tree.js (catmullRomPath/drawChain) into a pure-Python rasterizer (via scripts/dds_image_lib.py) so the in-game connectors match what was authored in the planner tool. | Discovered 2026-07-13 while designing the Victory Path Tree node-overlay UI (docs/design/Victory_Path_Tree_Variant_Design.md). Applies to any future node-graph/skill-tree-style panel with fixed node positions and prerequisite connector lines. |
| gui | any | Writing max = [1] as a data binding | Use a numeric property literal such as max = 1. Constants inside function arguments use explicit typed literals. | The renderer previously accepted the invalid numeric binding; it now reports INVALID BINDING. |
| gui | any | Passing a formatted text expression such as ScriptValue(...)|+0 to a pieslice value or other numeric GUI property | Use a numeric binding, e.g. FixedPointToFloat(Scope.ScriptValue(...)), for slice value. The renderer now rejects the suffix in data bindings while retaining formatted text and quoted pipe literals. |
| gui | any | Copying a pre-1.4 location-window road button with ShowRoadbuilder into a 1.4 GUI | Call ShowRoadBuilder with a capital B, and make the source generator migrate the third-party snapshot with an exact replacement-count check. | The official 1.4 data-type diff explicitly removes ShowRoadbuilder and adds ShowRoadBuilder. The old call survived the earlier header-button migration in both stable Glorp and M&T location windows; standalone already used the current spelling. |
| gui | global | Writing global_variable_map values only for GUI display, with GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap(...) as the sole read | Keep global_variable_map for keyed caches when appropriate, but also read each map name in a live script path with global_variable_map(name|scope:key). A local-variable count or no-op script-side read is enough when the value is only displayed. | Jomini's unused-variable validation also ignores GUI-only GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap reads. Use in unused scripted triggers/effects does not count; the read must sit in an effect/trigger that actually runs. |
| gui | any | Using set_variable only to feed GUI/localization display, with no live script-side read | For GUI-only display caches, ensure the scope variable is also read in a live script path. Scope variables written only for panel display can flood error.log with 'Variable X is set but is never used'. |
| gui | any | Putting blockoverride sol_good_tooltip on a bare widget when no inherited block has that name | Print the resolved type and verify the slot. For a widget tooltip, assign tooltip directly; use blockoverride only for an existing inherited block. | Source-level finding on 2026-10-13. No sol_good_tooltip block existed in the shared kit; layout previews did not exercise hover behavior. | gui | any | Referencing a named colour in a `pieslice` colour field, or pre-dividing slice values into percentages | A `pieslice` `color` takes a literal `{ r g b a }` list - a named colour cannot be used there. Slice drawing verified in the project renderer by pixel ratio on a 30/70 test (2026-10-13). |
| gui | any | Expecting the |+N number suffix to print a '+' sign, or writing '+[value|+N]' expecting a plain-coloured number | |+N only colours by sign (positive green, negative red, zero uncoloured) and prints no '+'. |=N / |+=N additionally print '+' on positive values. A hand-written '+' before [x|+N] stays in the surrounding colour. | Confirmed in game on the SOL situation panel and location tooltip (2026-10-11). |<1%, |W, |R, |G, |-=, |=+ remain unconfirmed. |
| gui | any | Putting margin_top directly on a progressbar widget | Wrap the progressbar in a fixed-size widget/layout container, put vertical placement such as parentanchor/widgetanchor on the progressbar inside that wrapper, and leave margin_top off the progressbar itself. | EU5 progressbar does not handle margin_top. Text and container widgets may use margins, but progressbar vertical offset should be expressed through a parent wrapper. |
| gui | any | Providing only progresstexture and expecting a parent background to supply the empty region | Set noprogresstexture explicitly. The progressbar shader samples Texture0 for the filled region and Texture1 for the empty region. A separate background does not bind Texture1. | The supplied screenshot showed solid lime bars while the renderer drew red/gold textures. Exact missing-sampler colour is engine-dependent. Evidence: screenshot symptom and source for the correction (2026-10-13). |
| gui | any | Writing `progressbar = { size = { W H } using = bar_gold min = 0 max = ... value = ... }` on the assumption that `using` names a texture | `using = X` inlines the properties of a `type X`; it is not a texture reference. If no `X` exists the progressbar resolves no texture and draws nothing at all - silently, while still occupying its layout slot and reporting a normal size. A progressbar needs an explicit `progresstexture` (and optionally `noprogresstexture`), plus `texture_density` / `spriteType` / `spriteborder` on the element. | Every bar in the SOL prototype tooltips measured correctly and drew nothing (2026-10-13). The failure is invisible to both the renderer's measure pass and a size check - only the pixels reveal it. |
| gui | any | A catch-all prototype mock returning zero before the scenario's supplied script values | Register only explicitly provided script-value/map keys before scenario mocks. Unspecified keys fall through to the scenario, and missing script values remain Unknown. | Tool regression (2026-10-13): explicit overrides, scenario fallback and missing-key reporting are checked separately. A zero unknown-expression count must not come from zero defaults. |
| gui | any | Emitting literal backslash-n or backslash-t into a quoted GUI string | GUI lexing allows only escaped quotes/backslashes. Insert [Localize('NEWLINE_BREAK')] for tooltip line breaks; newline escapes belong in localization YAML. Do not double the slash merely to suppress the lexer error. | SOL goods-cell generator emitted invalid escapes on every hover. The shared generator now uses vanilla NEWLINE_BREAK and renderer pdx tokenizer rejects invalid escapes; bilingual location/country hover regression checks real line breaks. |
| gui | any | Putting $LOCALIZATION_KEY$ substitutions inside raw_text GUI fields | Use a localized text = "KEY" field and put inline icons or static text in the localization value, or split dynamic raw text and localized text into separate widgets. | raw_text renders the string literally; $KEY$ substitutions are not expanded, so the UI displays the key. |

SOL's copied zoom-button support must omit it even when the location-window source is current. |
| gui | any | Resolving every widget expression from global mock values while ignoring a parent widget's datacontext | Keep ancestor datacontexts on a stack while resolving descendants; in the battle view, Combat.GetDefender supplies the CombatSide object used by child CombatSide.GetLeft. | gui | any | RequirementsList with an empty blockoverride "block_title" { } to hide its title | Keep the nested title block and hide it explicitly: blockoverride "block_title" { block "block_title" { visible = no } } | An empty override removes RequirementsList's safe title replacement and exposes the underlying TooltipTableHeader textbox. | gui | building | Passing a building key string to SelectLocationToBuildDefault / SelectLocationToBuildWithLocation in GUI | Pass a BuildingType object from GUI data context, such as BuildingItem.GetBuildingType, LocationBuildingItem.GetBuilding.GetType, or BuildingType.Self. If no BuildingType object is available in the panel, route the button through a generic action with a location select_trigger. | The vanilla build-location GUI calls SelectLocationToBuildDefault with a typed BuildingType object. A literal string key can leave the click appearing successful only because a following PlayAudioEffect runs, while no build-location interface opens. |
| gui | modifier | Calling ShowModifierEffect with wonder display modifier ids built by GUI Concatenate(...) without generated static modifier definitions and unreachable script references | Generate matching static display modifiers plus an unreachable always = no script block that references every possible display id through add_country_modifier/add_location_modifier. Use those mirrors for GUI ShowModifierEffect display only; do not route completed wonder grants or scripted-effect tooltip previews through them. | Building modifier blocks and GUI static mirrors carry display-only country/local effects, while country auto modifiers provide the actual national effects from owned final buildings. Scripted effect previews are a separate parser path: add_country_modifier validates against static/country modifiers, not common/auto_modifiers; the current wonder flow avoids modifier-list previews for auto-driven country effects. |
| gui | trigger | Calling ShowTriggerConditionsForScope with a trigger name built by GUI Concatenate(...) from a numeric display id, but only defining the underlying scripted_trigger under its string wonder key; or aliasing straight to a real gameplay trigger that carries extra guard conditions the display context already guarantees | Generate a thin alias scripted_trigger named with the numeric display id (e.g. tv_wonder_display_<id>_base_site_rules_trigger) so the Concatenate-built name resolves. Unlike ShowModifierEffect, scripted_trigger/scripted_effect names are not a separate database lookup, so no unreachable always = no reference block is needed for these aliases. Do not simply delegate the alias to the real gameplay trigger (tv_wonder_location_meets_<key>_base_site_rules_trigger = yes) if that trigger carries a guard condition (such as unique wonders' this = location:<site>) that is always true in the display's own context — a location tooltip already IS that location, so showing that condition as a row is redundant noise. Instead, re-embed just the site-rule condition list (the same trigger_conditions(...)/site_trigger_lines_for_wonder(...) call the real trigger uses) directly in the display alias, omitting the always-true guard. | tv_wonder_location_meets_<key>_base_site_rules_trigger is already the location-scoped (unwrapped) form of the Engineering Department's any_owned_location-wrapped site rule check, so a GUI block that already runs in LocationView.GetLocation.MakeScope.Self can reuse its condition list directly via ShowTriggerConditionsForScope(Concatenate('tv_wonder_display_', Concatenate(idString, '_base_site_rules_trigger')), LOCATION_SCOPE) without any_owned_location. For unique wonders specifically, keep the this = location:<site> guard only on the real gameplay trigger (still needed so the wonder can only be queued/built at its designated site); the display alias should call trigger_conditions(wonder, mechanics, 1) directly rather than delegating, so the redundant 'is <site>' row does not render. |
| gui | country | Appending a shared overlay `widget` (e.g. a confirmation popup meant to float above all tab content) as a sibling AFTER a `vbox`/`hbox` closes, when that vbox is the one carrying `datacontext = "[...]"`, instead of nesting the overlay inside the vbox or giving the overlay its own datacontext. `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay()` was called after `emit(lines, 2, "}")` closed the datacontext-carrying vbox, so the overlay widget and all its descendants (visible checks, text_single/text_multi labels) had no Country data context at all. | `datacontext` set on a widget/vbox/hbox only applies to that block's own descendants; it is NOT inherited by later siblings once the block closes, even if the sibling is still inside the same enclosing `blockoverride`. Any `Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...)` (or other typed accessor) used inside a sibling overlay placed outside the datacontext-carrying container must either be moved inside that container, or receive its own `datacontext = "[SituationView.GetPlayer]"` (or equivalent) directly on its own outermost widget. The runtime does not error at load time; every `FetchData` call inside the affected subtree silently fails with `pdx_data_callstack.cpp: No context supplied (Use SetDataContext), wanted context of type 'Country'`, which reads as the whole overlay's dynamic content just not evaluating (visible conditions default false, values blank) rather than a crash. | Found 2026-07-13 debugging the Victory Path Tree confirm/cancel overlay (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`): `append_panel()` calls `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay(lines, 2)` right after closing the tab-content vbox that carries `datacontext = "[SituationView.GetPlayer]"`, making the overlay a sibling of that vbox inside `blockoverride "situation_panel_main_content"`, not a descendant of it. Every `visible`/`text` expression inside the overlay referenced `Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...)` assuming it would inherit the parent's Country context; instead every one of them failed FetchData silently. Applies to any future shared overlay/popup appended as a top-level sibling after a datacontext-scoped container closes -- always give it its own explicit `datacontext` if it needs typed-scope accessors. SUPERSEDED 2026-07-13 (same day): the overlay this bug lived in was deleted entirely once `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold = yes` (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`) was found to make it unnecessary -- kept here only as a general datacontext-scoping lesson for any future shared overlay/popup, not as a reference to still-existing code. |

| gui | any | Giving a fixed-width column cell a `size = { 46 26 }` and expecting the column to be 46px wide | Set `autoresize = no` on every fixed-width text cell. `text_single` inherits `autoresize = yes` from `text_single_template`, and autoresize makes the declared size lose to the text's intrinsic width, so the cell collapses to its content and the columns run together. Keep the default only where shrink-to-fit is wanted. | Cost real debugging time on the SOL estate ledger: five declared columns (46/50/58/52/96) all rendered at content width and fused into one run of digits. The renderer reproduces the game's behaviour, so this was visible before launch. Rule also in the GUI guide section 2 (2026-10-13). |
| gui | texture | Using texture = "[Concatenate('gfx/...', key, '.dds')]" to dynamically select a raw DDS path | Route dynamic images through a GUI function that returns a texture handle, such as GetConceptTexture(...). Static texture paths are parsed by the GUI loader, while runtime texture expressions need a texture-like return value rather than a CString path. |

| gui | sizing | Using TooltipTextBlock for a fallback/empty-state line next to a sibling TooltipRequirementsList or text_single that has blockoverride row_size / field_text_format sizing, expecting both to render at the same height | TooltipTextBlock (vanilla type in main_menu_cooltip_types.gui) wraps its text in a fixed vbox { margin = { @tooltip_inner_margin @tooltip_inner_margin } } (10px each side) around a text_multi_template textbox, and exposes no blockoverride for font size or row height. blockoverride row_size / field_text_format on a sibling TooltipRequirementsList does not reach it. When a mutually-exclusive fallback line must match a sized row's height (e.g. an effect list vs. its own "no effect" line), replace TooltipTextBlock with a plain text_single using the same fontsize and an explicit size, not the templated tooltip type. | Observed in src/in_game/gui/location_window.gui: the ritual TooltipRequirementsList was resized to 13pt/22px rows to match the country/local modifier columns, but the mutually-exclusive ritual_no_effect_visible TooltipTextBlock stayed visibly taller until it was swapped for a text_single with fontsize = 13 and size = { -1 22 }. |

| gui | any | Using > >= < <= == operators directly in GUI visible expressions to compare variable values (e.g. visible = "[Country.MakeScope.GetVariable('x').GetValue >= 3]") | Use typed comparison functions: GreaterThanOrEqualTo_CFixedPoint(Country.MakeScope.GetVariable('name').GetValue, '(CFixedPoint)N.0') for integer milestone variables; EqualTo_float(FixedPointToFloat(...GetValue), '(float)N.0') for float comparisons | EU5 GUI expression language has no inline comparison operators; all comparisons require named typed functions (GreaterThanOrEqualTo_CFixedPoint, LessThan_float, EqualTo_int32, etc.) with explicit type-cast literals |

GetVariable('name').IsSet is the GUI-native null guard for optional variables stored on situation/country scopes. |
| gui | any | Gating a GUI visibility toggle on a proxy variable's mere existence (e.g. `tv_wonder_active_part`, 'which part is currently targeted') when the intended condition is a different, related-but-distinct fact (e.g. 'is real monthly progress > 0 actually accumulating right now'). | `tv_wonder_active_part` gets assigned by `tv_wonder_monthly_construction_effect` purely from `tv_wonder_organization_logistics_unlocked_trigger` (debate/lock/site-selected/not-complete) plus 'some part is still below max' — it says nothing about whether the labor camp is actually staffed (`tv_wonder_has_organized_labor_trigger` / `total_effective_building_levels:tv_wonder_labor_camp > 0`) or whether `tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress` is actually nonzero this month. A wonder can have `tv_wonder_active_part` set while producing zero real progress (e.g. an unstaffed Labor Camp), so a GUI toggle built on `active_part.IsSet` alone silently drifts from the intended 'is there a genuinely working site' condition. Add an explicit check on the real underlying variable (`GreaterThan_CFixedPoint(GetVariable('tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress').GetValue, '(CFixedPoint)0.0')`) alongside the proxy, rather than trusting the proxy to always track it. | 2026-07-15: user reported that the 'when monthly progress is 0, show no available work site instead of a remaining-time estimate' behavior had stopped working. Traced to the construction card's two visibility conditions (added 2026-07-13, commit 469ab549) having always been gated purely on `tv_wonder_active_part.IsSet` — a proxy that was never actually tied to real progress, since the monthly auto-advance branch that assigns `tv_wonder_active_part` does not check `tv_wonder_has_organized_labor_trigger` the way the four manual tv_wonder_begin_foundation/body/function/decoration actions already do. Added `GreaterThan_CFixedPoint(... tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress ..., 0)` to both visibility conditions (AND'd with the existing active_part check) so the GUI now reflects genuine 0-progress stalls (e.g. an unstaffed Labor Camp) instead of only 'no part selected at all'. Left tv_wonder_monthly_construction_effect's script-side auto-advance logic untouched since the underlying construction math already self-corrects (0 progress never crosses a completion threshold) — this was purely a display-layer gap. |
| gui | any | Placing a wrapping `text_multi`/`textbox` in a layout without a `max_width`, assuming the parent's width will constrain it | Give every wrapping text an explicit `max_width`. An hbox measures its children with no width hint, so an uncapped multiline text reports its full *unwrapped* width as a **minimum**, and a minimum defeats the contextual-tooltip width cap: the whole tooltip is dragged wider than its frame. `max_width` caps that minimum as well as the hint. | Missed twice in the SOL tooltip rebuild - first as a bare `text_multi`, then again after rebuilding it on TooltipListBase, because the cap only applies once `max_width` is set. Rule also in the GUI guide section 2 (2026-10-13). |

| gui | any | An hbox/vbox sets an explicit `size = { W H }` to bound a mixed fixed-column + expanding-column layout (e.g. a fixed icon widget plus an expanding text_multi) but does not itself declare `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` -- e.g. gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py's per-card `hbox = { size = {432 104} spacing = 8 widget {...64px icon...} text_multi {...} }`. | When a container combines an explicit `size` hint with expanding children, also set `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` (and `layoutpolicy_vertical` if relevant) on the container itself, matching this file's own established precedent (Pharos stage rows at tv_engineering_department.gui:8401-8402; the Hagia active-ritual row at :9372-9375). Without it, the declared `size` is not honored -- the container instead sizes itself to the sum of its children's intrinsic minimums, so a fixed-width icon column plus a text_multi with no minimumsize (0 intrinsic width until stretch is applied) collapses the whole row to roughly just the icon's width, leaving the text column effectively 0px wide and unselectable. | Reported by the user via in-game inspection: the per-card content hbox rendered at ~80px wide (icon column + spacing only) instead of its declared 432px, and the flavor text_multi was unselectable (effectively 0 width). A prior guess that the missing property was `ignoreinvisible = yes` on the same hbox was reverted by the user as not addressing the actual observed symptom; the real gap was the container's own layoutpolicy_horizontal. This rule is specific to hbox/vbox as multi-child flex containers -- for the DIFFERENT (but easy to conflate) failure where a vbox uses `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes`, see `set_parent_size_to_minimum_vbox_needs_leaf_minimumsize_for_width` instead; adding layoutpolicy_horizontal to the surrounding widget/vbox in that case has no effect and was reverted by the user as such. |

| localization | any | #YText#! / #Y{generated_text}#! — color tag immediately followed by normal text or generated text with no separator | Insert a separator after the color tag, e.g. #Y Text#!, #G Text#!, or #R Text#!. The formatter can treat the adjacent content as part of the formatting tag and drop or mangle the highlighted span. A visible space after the tag terminates it. |
| localization | any | #Y[variable_expression]#! — color tag immediately followed by [ with no separator | Either omit the color tag and write [variable_expression] as plain text, OR add a space: #Y [variable_expression]#! — the space terminates the tag name so the parser reads #Y (tag) then ' value' (content). Note the leading space will be visible in the rendered string. | In EU5 Jomini localization, the color tag name is read from # until the first non-alphanumeric. When a [var] resolves to a number (e.g. 64) and is placed directly after #Y, the parser reads #Y64 as the tag name (unknown). The leading space in '#Y [var]' terminates the tag name at Y. |
| localization | any | Only using #R/#G color tags (or inventing a custom GUI/widget solution) for localization text emphasis, tabular tooltip alignment, or numeric breakdowns, without checking whether an existing `#tag` already covers the need | Check the Text Format Tag Catalog in docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md section 6.3 before designing new emphasis/layout for event or tooltip text. Confirmed tags: colors #R #G #Y #W #V #L #P #N #X #D #F (plus lowercase #r #g #y); style #bold #italic #weak #high #T #subtle_name; tabular #col #col_t #col_m; and special #TOOLTIP:$BREAKDOWN_TAG$, #indent_newline:N, #trigger_pass/#trigger_fail. All must be closed with #! and (per [[loc_color_tag_adjacent_text]]) separated from adjacent content by a space. | There is no single canonical reference file for the full #tag list in this repo's mirror — the underlying textformatting.gui asset is gfx-adjacent and pruned by scripts/sync_reference.py. The catalog was reconstructed by frequency-counting #[A-Za-z_0-9]+ tokens across all main_menu English localization files and reading surrounding usage context to infer each tag's semantics. |

If a temporary value is needed inside a later scope, set_local_variable before entering that scope and read local_var:X inside it; that direction is valid. | The unreliable direction is dynamic-scope local_var escaping outward through prev or later outer-scope reads. The opposite direction is valid: set_local_variable before a var:X ?= {} / location scope switch, then use local_var:X inside the nested scope. |

The missing-icon check is a WARNING (baseline-trackable, does not block game load per the engine's own log severity), while missing NAME/DESC/price-name localization is an ERROR (a real player-facing 'TODO: Write a desc' defect), matching the severity split already established by check_modifier_names' issues-vs-warnings convention. |

| on_action | country | A monthly_country_pulse-registered callback (or any per-country on_monthly-style hook) starting straight into a global/expensive scope iteration (every_character, every_country, every_international_organizations_member_of, every_owned_location, nested every_key_in_variable_map, etc.) with no cheap has_variable/has_variable_list membership check first. Because the callback runs for EVERY country every month, an ungated O(k) scan inside it becomes O(countries x k) system-wide, and if k itself scales with world population/character count the callback alone is O(countries x world_size). | Before any nontrivial scope walk inside a per-country monthly callback, gate it behind the cheapest possible existence/membership check for the state the walk actually needs (has_variable_list on a country-scoped list variable is strictly better than a global every_character/every_country scan filtered by a limit, since the list is already bounded to what this country owns). Example fix: tv_leaderboard_monthly_pulse's touring-artist roll used an ungated every_character = { limit = { is_artist=yes has_variable=tv_on_exhibition } } scanning every living character in the world every country every month; replaced with if = { limit = { has_variable_list = tv_dispatched_artists_list } } every_in_list = { variable = tv_dispatched_artists_list ... } since dispatching countries already maintain that list. | Engineering Department, Governor's House, and Academy of Sciences monthly pulses already follow the correct has_variable-gate-first pattern and are good reference examples. This rule is needs_parser (not lint) because detecting it requires knowing whether a cheap gate trigger precedes the expensive iterator within the same callback body, which needs brace-aware scope tracking; a future scripts/validate.py checker could walk pulse_registry.yaml callbacks and flag every_character/every_country/every_international_organizations_member_of/every_owned_location appearing before any has_variable/has_variable_list check in the same effect body.
Second technique, used for tv_io_establishment_mark_basic_requirements_effect's conquest branch: an existence/membership gate does not help when the country IS a legitimate participant and the value must still progress toward completion (e.g. tv_update_conquest_score_effect's every_subject_or_below scan feeds a threshold every not-yet-established country needs to keep evaluating). There, split by is_ai instead of gating on existence: keep the scan on the existing monthly_country_pulse callback for is_ai = no, and add a sibling callback registered under yearly_country_pulse (see pulse_registry.yaml) that runs the same scan for is_ai = yes once a year. This mirrors the already-accepted human-monthly/AI-yearly split in towards_victory_situations.txt's tv_victory_situation.on_monthly. The scan itself is bounded by the ticking country's own subject count (not world size), so this is a lower-severity instance of the pattern than the world-character scan in tv_leaderboard_monthly_pulse -- worth fixing for consistency and to stop it running forever for AI, but not an O(n^2) hazard on its own.
Third technique, used for tv_trade_league_country_monthly_pulse_effect and tv_diplomatic_alliance_country_monthly_pulse_effect: both scanned every_international_organizations_member_of across ALL instances of the IO type every country every month, relying on an inner limit (leader_country ?= scope:<pulse_country>) to discard every entry except the one where the ticking country happens to be that instance's leader -- meaning only actual IO leaders ever did real work, but every non-leader country still paid the full scan cost walking every league/alliance's membership list. A plain has_variable_list membership gate is too coarse here (it would still admit every non-leader member, not just leaders). Use is_leader_of_international_organization_of_type = { type = tv_<io> } instead -- a single O(1) trigger check, already used elsewhere in this codebase (data/io_establishment.yaml ai_creation_requirement_body, src/in_game/common/generic_actions/tv_io_leader_actions.txt) -- to skip the scan entirely for every country that isn't currently leading any instance of that IO type.
Fourth case, tv_academy_world_debate_monthly_effect (scripts/philosophy_debate_codegen.py): this one already had the right gate (if = { limit = { has_variable = tv_academy_world_debate_active } ... }) but the tv_academy_world_debate_mirror_all_countries_effect = yes call (an every_country walk over every academy-owning country) was emitted at the WRONG indent level, one level shallower than the if body, so it ran every month unconditionally regardless of whether a debate was active. Not a missing gate, a misplaced statement outside an otherwise-correct one -- when a codegen helper builds nested blocks by explicit indent level (emit(lines, level, text) style, as opposed to raw multi-line string templates), double-check the indent level of every appended line against the block it is meant to close into, especially the last statement before a block's closing brace. The mirror is still safe to run on the exact month the debate resolves, since resolve_effect only clears active partway through the same if body and EU5 effect blocks execute sequentially rather than re-checking the outer limit -- so moving the call to be the last line inside the if (not after it) preserves the one clearing pass on resolution while skipping all the months no debate is running. |

Fixed 2026-07 by adding `scope:winner = { this = root }` to both on_actions' triggers. |
| script | country | A variable meant to gate "has this construction/ritual cycle already completed" is set once on completion and cleared only on full project abandonment, while a separate resume/expansion code path re-enters the SAME stage machinery for a NEW cycle on the SAME object without ever clearing that gate. | When a mechanic supports repeated cycles on one persistent object (a generic Engineering Department wonder's expand-then-hold-ceremony-again loop, `tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2`), any one-shot "this cycle is done" variable must be explicitly cleared at the start of each new cycle, not just on abandonment. `tv_wonder_ceremony_ready_trigger` (`tv_engineering_department_triggers.txt:418-423`) gates the ceremony conditions/rewards/hold-button display on `stage==4 AND NOT has_variable = tv_wonder_finalized`. `tv_wonder_finalized` is set once by `tv_wonder_finalize_effect` and was previously cleared ONLY by the full-abandonment cleanup (`tv_wonder_clear_project_state_core_effect`). The expansion-mode branch of `tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect` (added by commit afd14d65, 2026-06-11, well before `tv_wonder_finalized` even existed as a concept the branch needed to interact with) resets `tv_wonder_stage = 3` and other construction counters to let the wonder build past its previous completed level and re-reach stage 4, but never cleared `tv_wonder_finalized` — so after a wonder's FIRST ceremony completion, every later expansion cycle reached stage 4 again with `tv_wonder_finalized` still set from the prior cycle, permanently failing `tv_wonder_ceremony_ready_trigger`. The GUI's downstream cache-populate effect then took its else-branch (`tv_wonder_index_clear_selected_ritual_cache_effect`) every time, wiping the cached ritual id/cost-type the conditions/rewards/hold-button widgets key off — while a SEPARATE, unrelated "ceremony style locked" static text (gated only on `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked.IsSet`, which is correctly meant to persist across expansions per design so the player cannot re-pick a style) kept rendering, making the wonder look permanently stuck with no way to ever hold the ceremony again. Fix: clear ONLY the one-shot completion gate (`remove_variable = tv_wonder_finalized`) inside the expansion branch (`if = { limit = { var:tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2 } ... }`) — do NOT clear `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked`/`tv_wonder_ceremony_style`, since design intent is that an expansion reuses the previously-chosen style and only needs the player to click the hold/confirm button again, not re-pick. When two variables gate overlapping-looking UI (one meant to persist across cycles, one meant to reset per cycle), verify each one's clear points independently against the actual multi-cycle design intent instead of assuming a single "locked" flag's persistence also implies the completion gate should persist. | 2026-07-20: user reported that for a generic wonder, after choosing ceremony style 1, every EXPANSION of that wonder shows a static "style 1 locked" text with no conditions/rewards/hold-button, and the ceremony can never be held again. Traced via two background research passes plus direct git archaeology: the gap predates the 2026-07 Engineering Department mod split (c371a9af) entirely — `git log -S` on the pre-split file path shows `tv_wonder_finalized` was introduced by d394ac0a and the `project_mode ?= 2` expansion-resume branch was added later by afd14d65, which never accounted for the pre-existing completion gate. Not a regression from a single later change that broke previously-correct behavior; a design gap present since the expansion-resume feature's inception. User confirmed design intent (no re-pick allowed on expansion, but the hold-button must reappear) and confirmed the correct minimal fix is removing only `tv_wonder_finalized`, not the lock/style variables. Follow-up in the same session: fixing the gate alone was not sufficient — the GUI reads a SEPARATE cached layer (`tv_wonder_selected_ritual_id` etc.), populated only when something explicitly calls `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect`. That effect is not reactive; nothing recomputes it just because a raw variable changed. So the added `remove_variable = tv_wonder_finalized` fix (in the load-repair effect) was paired with an explicit `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect = yes` call right after it, and a THIRD, related but distinct gap was found and fixed the same way: `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (`:1998-2016`) transitions a wonder to `stage = 4` and calls `tv_wonder_initialize_ceremony_runtime_state_effect`, but that callee entire body — including its own cache-refresh call — is gated on `tv_wonder_unique_locked_trigger = yes`, a no-op for generic wonders. A FRESH (not-yet-locked) generic wonder self-heals anyway, because its choose-a-style GUI buttons are gated on a live `NOT tv_wonder_ceremony_locked.IsSet` check (no cache needed), and the player click itself calls `tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_effect`, which does refresh the cache. But an EXPANSION whose style is already locked (design intent: no re-pick allowed) never shows those buttons and has no equivalent player click, so nothing would refresh the cache until the next `tv_engineering_department_wonder_auto_cache_monthly_pulse` (up to about a month of the ceremony UI looking stuck, self-resolving, not permanent like the primary bug). Fixed by adding an unconditional `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect = yes` to `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` itself — harmless no-op for unique wonders and for fresh generic wonders (which already self-heal), and closes the gap specifically for locked-style expansions. General lesson: when a cache/derived-display layer is populated only by specific named call sites rather than being recomputed reactively, enumerate every state-transition point that could make the underlying condition true and verify each one calls the refresh — a transition that happens to also be reachable via a player click that already refreshes elsewhere can mask a transition that has no such click. |
| script | any | ordered_in_global_list limit block that references situation:X.var:Y without a prior has_variable = Y guard when Y might not be set | Wrap the entire ordered_in_global_list block in: if = { limit = { has_variable = Y } ordered_in_global_list = { ... } }. The outer if is evaluated once on the situation scope, so it is cheap. The inner limit still includes the var: reference but now only runs when Y is known to be set. | ordered_in_global_list evaluates its limit block for EVERY entry in the pool. If the referenced situation variable (e.g. tv_rank_1_country) was never set (because the pool was empty at situation on_start), every pool entry triggers a script error, producing N errors on first load. Guard with has_variable on the outer if to skip the whole pass when the prerequisite rank is absent. |
| script | any | Cascading ordered_in_global_list ranks that never clear old rank variables and gate later ranks only with has_variable = previous_rank | Treat ranking output as a fresh snapshot: remove all rank_N variables before rebuilding, ensure every pool entry has numeric order_by variables, and guard rank N with global_variable_list_size value > N-1 before running ordered_in_global_list. | A stale previous rank can survive from an earlier rebuild. If the current pool has fewer eligible entries, the later ordered_in_global_list pass can run with no selected item; prev is none, set_variable value = prev logs 'Value of wrong type ... none', and order_by/var reads may log failed variable fetches. Clearing outputs and checking current pool size prevents the pass from running when no current candidate exists. |
| scope | variable_map | Using an inflated ordered_key_in_variable_map max value such as 10000 to process every current key | Use every_key_in_variable_map with a found/stop flag when all keys may need inspection, or set ordered_key_in_variable_map max only to an exact known upper bound that cannot exceed the current map size. | EU5 logs a Script system error when ordered_key_in_variable_map max is larger than the current key list, even though it reports that it capped max at the list size. Do not use a huge max as a harmless 'iterate all' shortcut. |
| scope | any | Omitting max from an ordered_* scope while expecting it to iterate every matching object | Set an explicit sufficient max for full ordered traversal, or a verified dynamic bound. Reserve max = 1 or the default single result for deliberate best-candidate selection. | In runtime testing, ordered_owned_location without max scored 189 valid locations in the first pass but assigned only the first location in the ordered pass. An explicit max = 100000 makes the classification pass traverse all practical country location counts. |
| gui | any | Setting parentanchor on a widget that is a direct child of an hbox or vbox (e.g. inside hbox = { widget = { parentanchor = vcenter ... } }) | Remove parentanchor from layout-managed hbox/vbox children. Explicit ignore_layout = yes overlays may position themselves, as in the vanilla 1.4 construction queue. parentanchor remains valid on children of plain widget/window/container parents. | Brace-aware validator checks direct hbox/vbox child blocks for direct parentanchor properties, excluding direct ignore_layout = yes overlays. The 1.4 construction_queue_card in reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/location_window.gui uses this exception. Children of progressbar/widget/window are unaffected. |
| gui | any | Setting size with a percentage component on a widget that is a direct child of an hbox or vbox, e.g. size = { 97% 72 } inside organization_custom_content | Remove the percentage size and rely on layout policies, or use non-percent fixed/min/max sizing when a fixed dimension is actually needed. For io_character_card in organization_custom_content, omit size/parentanchor and rely on the card type's built-in layoutpolicy_expanding. | Pattern is empty because static detection requires parsing GUI parent structure. Percent sizes are valid in some plain widget/fixedgrid contexts, but hbox/vbox children are arranged by the box layout and should be driven by layoutpolicy_* or stretch factors. |
| scripted_values | any | Writing a leading + sign on positive float literals, e.g. value = +0.025 or change_variable = { ... value = +0.003 } | Omit the + sign entirely: value = 0.025. The EU5/Jomini parser does not accept a leading + on numeric literals and will misread the value. | Triggered by Python f-string format spec +:  e.g. f'{delta:+.5f}' emits +0.00357 for positive deltas. Fix: use f'{delta:.5f}' so no leading sign is emitted for positive values. |
| script | any | A raw hard comparison between two variables (`var:X <= var:Y`, `var:X > var:Y`, etc.) placed after a `has_variable = X` / `has_variable = Y` guard earlier in the SAME `AND` block, instead of checking whether the comparison's dynamism is even needed on that code path before patching its RHS syntax. | `has_variable` guards preceding a raw two-variable comparison inside the same `AND` do NOT prevent the engine's tooltip/effect-preview pre-evaluation pass from independently evaluating that comparison line and logging 'Event target link var returned an unset scope' / 'Invalid left side during comparison var' / 'Invalid right side during comparison var' / 'Failed to fetch variable ... due to not being set' — that preview pass evaluates each condition line independently rather than truly short-circuiting the AND at the first false guard. Two syntax-only fixes were tried on `tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger`'s `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style <= var:tv_wonder_locked_style_count` line and both failed: wrapping as `var:X ?= { this <= var:Y }` (still invalid — inside the `?=` block, scope has changed to the *value* of X, so a bare `var:Y` RHS can't resolve there), then `var:X ?= { this <= root.var:Y }` (syntactically valid per `reference_game_files/game/in_game/events/disaster/castilian_civil_war.txt:2446`, but STILL failed in practice). The real root cause was structural, not syntactic, per risk_cards/wonders.md rule 10: `tv_wonder_locked_style_count` is a *derived cache variable* written by `tv_wonder_index_cache_locked_wonder_attributes_effect` and read back by this same trigger later in the *same pre-evaluated chain* (`tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_effect`'s non-hidden `effect=` -> `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect` -> writes the cache -> calls this trigger to read it) — during tooltip/allow preview the write hasn't actually committed yet when the read happens, so no RHS syntax fixes it. The actual resolution was simpler still: the comparison was solving a non-problem. `tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger` (via `tv_wonder_can_choose_ceremony_style_trigger`'s `NOT = { tv_wonder_unique_locked_trigger = yes }` guard) is reachable only for *generic* wonders, which always have exactly 3 fixed ceremony styles (`tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_1/2/3` are the only actions that ever set this variable) — unique wonders use a completely separate ritual system (`tv_wonder_selected_ritual_*` variables) and never reach this trigger. The dynamic `tv_wonder_locked_style_count` (driven by `ceremony_styles()`/`final_buildings` in `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py`, genuinely variable per *unique* wonder) was introduced into this *generic*-wonder-only trigger by mistake in commit 66b5edf5 ('implement wonder global variable map', 2026-06-04), replacing the original literal `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style ?= { this <= 3 }` (from the trigger's introducing commit d394ac0a) that had never had this bug. Restored the literal `<= 3` form and dropped the now-unneeded `has_variable = tv_wonder_ceremony_style` / `has_variable = tv_wonder_locked_style_count` guards. General lesson: two consecutive failed syntax-only fixes on the same two-variable comparison is a strong signal to check the git history / design intent for whether the dynamism is even reachable on that path, rather than trying a third RHS variant. | 2026-07-16: user reported errors 'kept happening' (持续报错) while constructing a generic wonder, then after a first `?=`-wrap syntax fix, reported it errored again with a new message ('Invalid right side during comparison var'), then after a second `root.var:` syntax fix, said the fix was still ineffective ("修复无效，我已回退") and asked to look at how the trigger was originally written instead of continuing to patch the comparison syntax. `git log -S"tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger"`/`-S"tv_wonder_locked_style_count"` found the trigger's original form (commit d394ac0a) used a literal `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style ?= { this <= 3 }` with zero history of this error, and pinpointed commit 66b5edf5 as the exact change that introduced the two-variable comparison and thus the bug. The user then clarified the actual design: all generic wonders have exactly 3 ceremony styles; unique wonders never reach this trigger (separate ritual system) — so the 'dynamic style count' the two prior fix attempts were trying to make safe was never a real requirement on this path, it was a copy-paste-era mistake from the global-variable-map refactor. Restored the literal `<= 3` original form (deleting the now-pointless `has_variable` guards) and validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report passed. Key process lesson for future sessions: two consecutive failed syntax-only fixes on the same comparison line is a strong signal to check the git history / design intent for whether the dynamism is even needed, rather than trying a third RHS variant. |
| script | country | Referencing a conditional/dynamic script_value (one with an `if =`, `min =`, `max =`, or any block reading `global_var:`/`var:`) as the RHS of `gold =` (or any other amount field) inside a `common/prices/` price definition. | Keep the price's `gold =` (or equivalent) field a compile-time-static number, or a reference to a simple flat named value (vanilla `build_price_age_of_tradition = 50` style — no braces, no conditional logic). To make the effective cost track a runtime value (a CMM setting, a country variable, ...), keep the base price static and apply the adjustment through the `<price_name>_cost_modifier` tag instead: register an `auto_modifiers` (or `static_modifiers`) entry with `scales_with = { value = global_var:X multiply = ... divide = ... subtract = 1 }` computing the desired percentage delta from the base price, and set `<price_name>_cost_modifier = 1` in that same block so the final applied modifier equals `scales_with`'s value directly. `scales_with` blocks ARE full script_value contexts (proven by vanilla `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/auto_modifiers/country.txt`, e.g. `scales_with = { value = num_colonial_charters subtract = 1 min = 0 max = 1 }`) and evaluate at runtime in the owning scope, unlike `common/prices/` fields. IMPORTANT: `<price_name>_cost_modifier` is NOT engine-auto-derived — every price's cost_modifier tag, vanilla and TV alike, needs its own EXPLICIT `modifier_type_definitions` entry (`color = bad  percent = yes  game_data = { category = country }`; see `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` at `reference_game_files/game/main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions/00_modifier_types.txt:802` and every sibling TV price in `src_engineering_department/main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions/tv_engineering_department_modifier_types.txt`). Skipping that registration produces a SEPARATE, later error: `pdx_persistent_reader.cpp: Unknown modifier type: <name>. It is either invalid, or a potential dynamic modifier type definition missing from the database`, and `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price. <name>` even once the price's own `gold =` field is fixed. | 2026-08-02: a CMM-driven direct-build price feature defined `tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price = { gold = tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_value }` where the referenced script_value had `if = { limit = { has_global_variable = ... } ... }` branching to read a CMM global_var. In-game this produced `pdx_persistent_reader.cpp:289: Malformed token` plus `jomini_named_values.h: Matching scriptvalue [...], but of wrong type` for every affected price, plus a `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price. <name>_cost_modifier` warning that read, at the time, like a cascading symptom of the malformed price. Fixed round 1 by reverting the price to a flat default and moving the CMM-driven adjustment to a new `auto_modifiers` file with `scales_with` feeding a `<price>_cost_modifier` tag — but that tag was never explicitly registered, on the WRONG assumption (based on `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` appearing usable in advances/estate-privilege files with no obvious nearby registration) that the engine auto-derives `<price>_cost_modifier` from the price definition itself. In-game round 2 then produced a DIFFERENT, unrelated-looking error: `Unknown modifier type: tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_cost_modifier ... a potential dynamic modifier type definition missing from the database`, plus the SAME `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price` line recurring even though the price itself now parsed fine — proving the missing-modifier-type warning was never just a symptom of the malformed token; it was reporting a second, independent, still-unfixed gap. Re-grepping `00_modifier_types.txt` directly (rather than trusting the earlier failed reference_mods-wide search) found `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` explicitly defined there at line 802, and every sibling TV price (`tv_wonder_labor_camp_price`, `tv_wonder_ritual_annex_small_price`, ...) already had its own explicit entry in `tv_engineering_department_modifier_types.txt` all along — the auto-derivation theory was simply wrong. Fixed round 2 by adding the three missing entries to that same file. General lesson: when an identifier 'just works' somewhere without an obvious nearby registration, grep the SPECIFIC registration file directly for that exact identifier before concluding the mechanism is implicit/auto-derived — a quick reference_mods-wide grep that returns zero hits is not proof of absence if the search pattern or directory scope was too narrow. |
| modifier | country | Defining a mod price key without a matching <price_key>_cost_modifier modifier type | For every mod-defined price key, add <price_key>_cost_modifier in main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions with category = country, and localize the price key plus MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME/DESC entries. | EU5 automatically looks for a cost modifier type named <price_key>_cost_modifier for each price. If it is absent, the engine logs 'Missing modifier type for price'. |
| script | any | Defining a price in common/prices/ without a corresponding modifier_type in common/modifier_type_definitions/ | For every price named {name} in common/prices/, a modifier type named {name}_cost_modifier MUST exist in common/modifier_type_definitions/. EU5's price_database.cpp validates this at startup and logs 'Missing modifier type for price. {name}_cost_modifier'. The modifier type allows future modifiers/events to discount the price. | This is an engine-enforced convention, not optional. Even if no modifier currently applies the discount, the modifier_type definition must be present. The naming is exact: price 'tv_academy_1_price' requires modifier type 'tv_academy_1_price_cost_modifier'. These appear unused but are implicitly consumed by the price loading system. |
| compatibility | price | Treating a repeated price resource field such as gold as an additive delta, or injecting a negative value to cancel an earlier mod's price injection | Repeated scalar fields injected into a price object replace the earlier field value. When two mods use the same operation type, use filename order to select the owner; use a verified full replacement only when filename ordering cannot express the intended final object. Never emit a negative cleanup injection. | In the SOL-PP stack, A_SOL_economy_prices.txt loads before pp_road_gold_adjustments.txt within TRY_INJECT, so PP's road gold fields naturally replace SOL's. A later compatibility injection of -10/-25/-100/-400 became authoritative and road costs were clamped to zero. This price behavior differs from additive numeric static modifiers. |
| script | any | Production method base input/output values produce profit above 30% | Compute recipe value from goods default_market_price and keep base profit below 30%, usually by slightly increasing inputs or reducing output/default output price. | EU5 logs a startup error when a production_method's base profit exceeds 30%, e.g. tv_wonder_material_dispatch_point_production at 33.33%. | script | any | Using display/alias-like goods names such as candles or luxury_cloth in production method upkeep | Use real goods database IDs: beeswax for candle upkeep, fine_cloth for luxury cloth upkeep. | Production method input keys must be exact goods IDs. | gui | any | progressbar with dynamic GetVariable value binding and no explicit max | Always set max = 100 on the progressbar widget. Pattern left empty — regex cannot reliably detect the missing-max + variable-value combination. |
| script | location | Treating location prosperity reads as 0..100 percent points, such as dividing an average prosperity value by 100 before using it as a weight | Use the raw signed fractional prosperity value. | script | any | Python-style boolean token True/False emitted into an EU5 common script | Render script booleans as yes or no. | script | any | Filtering random_list branches with modifier = { factor = 0 ... } | Use trigger = { ... } inside each weighted random_list branch. Vanilla random_list branch eligibility uses trigger blocks; use NOT = { var:current ?= N } there when a reroll must exclude the current result. | Engineering Department estate-demand rerolls used modifier factor zero in random_list branches. A player could bribe a demand into the artwork-loss branch and repeated same-day rerolls kept resolving to that same visible demand. Replacing branch modifiers with trigger filters and excluding the current demand makes the random pool behave like vanilla and prevents visible no-op rerolls. |
| script | any | random = { chance = X random_list = { ... } } — nesting random_list as a child of random | random_list = { (100-X) = {} X = { random_list = { ... } } }. Both levels of random_list are confirmed valid EU5 syntax. | random_list as a direct child of random = { chance = X } is silently dropped by the EU5 engine — the 8% roll fires but the random_list produces no outcome. All intended events are skipped with no error in the log. Fix: restructure to nested random_list where the high-weight option is the no-event case. |
| gui | country | Assuming a built-in country trigger (e.g. `num_of_advances_researched >= 50`) will show the player's current progress '(37/50)' in its custom_tooltip. Vanilla's own default tooltip loc for these triggers (see `NUM_ADVANCES_TRIGGER` in triggers_l_english.yml: "Has $COMPARATOR$ $NUM|V0$ [advances|E] researched") only substitutes the comparator and threshold — never the live value — and GetVariable-style GUI live-value display only works on country *variables*, not raw engine triggers/counts. | To show a live '(current/target)' readout for a raw trigger-only count, mirror it into a country variable via `set_variable = { name = tv_x_mirror value = <trigger_or_count> }` on the same monthly cadence that already checks the trigger (verify the count is usable as a `value =` expression first — e.g. `value = num_of_advances_researched` is confirmed vanilla usage in `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/script_values/research_values.txt` and `estates/00_default.txt`). Then embed `[Country.MakeScope.GetVariable('tv_x_mirror').GetValue|0]` in the GUI-facing loc key only (e.g. an `_ESTABLISHMENT_STEP1_TEXT` card string), matching the existing `TV_CONQUEST_PROGRESS_DISPLAY`-style pattern. Do NOT also add this GUI `Country.MakeScope` macro to a sibling loc key that is rendered as a `custom_tooltip.text` inside a scripted_trigger — that renders in script-tooltip context (ROOT/THIS scopes), not GUI data-binding context, so the GUI-only accessor is unverified there and should be kept out. | Discovered while investigating a user report that the Academy of Sciences 'research at least 50 advances' requirement 'seemed unsatisfiable' after the EU5 1.3 patch. First fix attempt added a tv_science_advances_researched_mirror variable (refreshed monthly inside tv_io_establishment_mark_basic_requirements_effect) and appended the live value only to TV_SCIENCE_ESTABLISHMENT_STEP1_TEXT. The player then confirmed in-game that the mirrored value itself never moved — num_of_advances_researched is apparently stuck/broken under this EU5 1.3 build, not just missing a tooltip — so the mirror was reverted (see [[num_of_advances_researched_broken_eu5_1_3]] for the replacement stopgap). The GUI-vs-script-tooltip loc-key split documented above is still valid general guidance for any OTHER numeric establishment route that wants a live readout — conquest/prosperity/trade/diplomatic already track a working `var:tv_x_score` for their basic requirement, so the same live-display pattern could be extended to them the same way if requested. |

In the hidden = yes country event's immediate block, re-check both the feature prerequisite and sentinel before doing work and before rescheduling itself. Clear the sentinel on teardown so already queued events stop naturally. | Delayed silent hidden events can implement daily immediate work outside monthly pulses and tooltip-rendered option chains, but every seed starts an independent loop. | script | any | Using REPLACE:<id> in a standalone-loadable mod (e.g. src_engineering_department/, src_court_positions/) for a key that only a DIFFERENT, non-guaranteed mod (e.g. the main src/ mod) defines. | REPLACE:<id> requires <id> to already exist in the database at load time. A base-game vanilla identifier is always safe to REPLACE: regardless of mod combination. A key owned by another TV mod is safe to REPLACE: only if that mod is a guaranteed hard dependency. If the current mod must also work standalone (or with any subset of the other TV mods) and the key is not guaranteed present, give this mod its OWN uniquely-namespaced key instead of trying to share/override the other mod's key — the same 'narrow to your own namespace' resolution already used for Court Positions' message types (see PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md). | 2026-08-02: src_engineering_department/in_game/common/trigger_localization/tv_engineering_department_triggers.txt declared REPLACE:TV_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT, assuming the main src/ mod's bare TV_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT definition (src/in_game/common/trigger_localization/towards_victory_triggers.txt) had already loaded. This is true only when both mods run together — CLAUDE.md's own Project Identity section states Engineering Department must be fully playable standalone, and in that configuration the main mod's copy never loads, so REPLACE: has nothing to replace and errors. This was the ORIGINAL motivation for anti_patterns rule same_id_database_override_missing_replace_prefix's TV_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT example (added 2026-07-28, presumably tested only in the combined-mods configuration where REPLACE: happened to work), so that rule's guidance was itself incomplete for standalone-capable mods. Fixed by renaming the Engineering Department mod's copy to TV_ENGINEERING_DEPARTMENT_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT (bare declaration, no REPLACE: needed, all 3 internal custom_tooltip references and the EN/ZH game_concepts loc key renamed to match) rather than trying to share the main mod's key at all — eliminates the collision instead of resolving it via load-order-dependent REPLACE:. The main src/ mod's own TV_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT (used by ~50 unrelated references across src/) was left untouched. |
| building | any | Copying a vanilla building's `unique_production_methods` child ID unchanged into a `REPLACE:<building>` override | Keep the complete production-method definition, but rename each copied child to a unique `tv_`-prefixed ID (for example `university_maintenance` to `tv_university_maintenance`). | REPLACE swaps the building definition but does not replace a nested unique production method's global registration; retaining the vanilla child ID logs a duplicate-production-method error and prevents the override from loading cleanly. |
| scope | any | Using ai_will_select in a resolution to disable AI proposals and votes | Use the documented resolution script values ai_will_propose and ai_will_do; set both to value = 0 when AI must not initiate or vote for the resolution. | The 1.4 resolution schema does not document ai_will_select, and no vanilla resolution uses it. The two fields control proposal and vote scoring separately. |
| scope | any | every_X = { root = { change_variable = { name = Y ... } } } where Y was set_variable'd on `this` before the loop, called from a situation's on_monthly | Use set_local_variable / change_local_variable for the accumulator. local_variable persists across all scope switches. Inside every_X, access the current-scope value directly (no prev. needed): change_local_variable = { name = acc add = some_value }. Then use local_var:acc after the loop. | In a situation's on_monthly, root = the situation scope, not the iterating country. set_variable stores onto `this` (country); root = { change_variable } then targets the situation — the accumulation goes to the wrong scope and the variable on the country stays 0. fix: local_variable is scope-independent and thread-local to the effect call. |
| script | any | Gating a button/option's own definite-completion effect through a shared "maybe complete" helper whose guard trigger re-reads a variable that same option's effect chain just wrote a few lines earlier, when the caller already knows the condition is true and does not need to re-derive it. | EU5's option/button tooltip pre-evaluation does not reliably thread a `set_variable` write across several nested `scripted_effect`/`scripted_trigger` call layers within the SAME previewed chain: even though the write and the read are both inside the one call being previewed, and both run correctly (in write-then-read order) at real execution time, the preview pass can still evaluate the nested trigger against the pre-write value, producing a false result with no error (a false `limit`, not a script fault). The Unique Wonder Ceremony's stage-8 button (`tv_wonder_ceremony_advance_to_stage_8_effect`) writes `set_variable = { name = tv_wonder_ceremony_stage value = 8 }` then calls `tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect = yes`, which routes through `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect`'s `tv_wonder_selected_ritual_completion_requirements_met_trigger` guard — for the ceremony framework this trigger literally checks `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_stage ?= 8`, the SAME variable just written two lines above. The tooltip preview read back the still-7 value, so the whole `if` block containing the completion reward (`add_country_modifier`) rendered as unreachable and was silently omitted — cost showed, reward did not, with a clean error.log. Making the completion call inline (undoing the [[deferred_reward_via_trigger_event_silently_invisible_to_tooltip_preview]] deferral) was necessary but not sufficient; the real fix was to stop re-deriving "is this complete" via the shared trigger-gated helper when the caller already knows it is. Added `tv_wonder_mechanics_force_complete_active_ritual_effect`, a duplicate of `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect`'s body without the `completion_requirements_met_trigger` gate, and had the stage-8 button call it directly. General lesson: when a button/option's effect chain establishes a fact about itself (by writing a variable) and then needs to act on that fact, do not route back through a shared "maybe/check-then-act" helper that re-derives the fact via a nested trigger read of the same variable — call the unconditional "definite" version of that helper directly. Reserve the trigger-gated shared helper for callers (monthly pulses, other ritual modes) that genuinely do not know in advance whether the condition holds. | 2026-07-16: after fixing the deferred-reward tooltip gap (see [[deferred_reward_via_trigger_event_silently_invisible_to_tooltip_preview]]), the user redeployed via build.bat and re-tested in game — the stage-8 tooltip still showed only cost, error.log clean. Traced the full guard chain for wonder 103 (ritual id 10301): tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect -> tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect -> tv_wonder_selected_ritual_completion_requirements_met_trigger -> tv_wonder_selected_ritual_custom_completion_requirements_met_trigger's 10301 branch checks var:tv_wonder_ceremony_stage ?= 8, which the SAME advance_to_stage_8_effect had just set two lines earlier. Confirmed via git history and code reading that tv_wonder_ritual_in_progress, var:tv_wonder_stage (the separate overall build-stage variable, stays 4 throughout since tv_wonder_finalize_effect only sets it to 5 AFTER the static-modifier call), and the ritual-id/has_custom_completion_trigger cache variables (set once at construction-finalization time, never clobbered by other cache-refresh call sites which all key off the acting country's own tv_wonder_locked/tv_wonder_ceremony_style) were all genuinely fine — the only remaining suspect was the just-written tv_wonder_ceremony_stage read-back. First attempt at the effect edit accidentally landed in the wrong function in gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.py (this module exports several independent generate_*() entry points — generate() for tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.txt, generate_ritual_effects() via append_ritual_effects() for tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt — and the new effect needed to go inside append_ritual_effects, not before it); running the wrong module's own main() regenerated the wrong output file and silently left the real target unchanged, confirmed by grep showing the new effect name absent from tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt after the first regen. Relocated the edit, ran gen_tv_wonder_ritual_effects.py specifically, and confirmed the new tv_wonder_mechanics_force_complete_active_ritual_effect appears in the generated file and the ceremony stage-8 effect calls it. |
| script | any | Copying an existing vanilla trigger-localization key, scripted effect, or building type into an override file with the original bare top-level key | Prefix the patch with the narrowest correct directive: use `INJECT:<id>` when only adding a field that the vanilla definition does not contain (for example `INJECT:university = { on_construction_ended = { ... } }`); use `REPLACE:<id>` only when altering or replacing existing fields, preserving the complete original block plus the intended modification. This applies only to base-game vanilla identifiers guaranteed to exist regardless of load order — for a TV-namespaced key that only a non-guaranteed mod (e.g. the main `src/` mod) defines, see replace_prefix_requires_guaranteed_base_entry instead; REPLACE:-sharing that kind of key breaks whenever the defining mod isn't loaded. | EU5 does not infer a patch merely because a mod repeats an existing non-event database key. A bare later block is rejected as a duplicate; use INJECT for a new child field and REPLACE only when an existing definition must change. 2026-08-02: removed TV_HAS_VARIABLE_NOT_SET_TT from this pattern's example list after discovering it was the wrong fix for that specific key — see replace_prefix_requires_guaranteed_base_entry. |
| scope | generic_actions | Using `save_scope_as` inside a select_trigger `visible`/`enabled` block, or inside an `effect`'s `if`/`limit` trigger body, to stash a scope for a later comparison in that same trigger context | Use `save_temporary_scope_as` for any scope save performed inside a trigger-evaluated block (select_trigger visible/enabled, allow, potential, or any `limit = { ... }` body). Reserve `save_scope_as` for saves performed directly inside an effect body (siblings of `limit`, not inside it), where the scope needs to persist to sibling/later effect lines. | The engine's trigger parser does not register `save_scope_as` as a valid trigger type at all; only `save_temporary_scope_as` is valid inside trigger evaluation. `save_scope_as` is effect-only. The Trade Chain generator (`scripts/in_game/common/generic_actions/gen_tv_trade_league_actions.py`) used `save_scope_as` inside `chain_previous_save`/`chain_nearest_previous_limits` (limit-block distance comparisons) and `chain_selector_filter` (select_trigger visible/enabled), producing hundreds of duplicate 'Unknown trigger type: save_scope_as' errors on load. Fixed by switching those three helpers to `save_temporary_scope_as`; the one genuine effect-body save (`scope:target = { save_scope_as = tv_trade_chain_candidate_market }` in the action's `effect` block) was left unchanged. |
| scope | any | Reading `scope:<name>` where `<name>` does not exactly match the string passed to any `save_scope_as = <name>` earlier in the same effect chain (e.g. reading `scope:tv_trade_candidate_market_location` when only `save_scope_as = tv_trade_candidate_market` was ever executed) | Grep the generator for every `save_scope_as = <name>` it emits and use that exact name in every later `scope:<name>` reference, including references made from a nested scope such as `scope:tv_trade_monopoly_leader = { ... }`. Saved scopes are exact-string keyed; a plausible near-miss name (an extra `_location` suffix, a singular/plural mismatch) silently resolves to nothing rather than erroring at generation time. | assign_candidate_to_io_rank runs inside scope:tv_trade_monopoly_leader = { ... }, itself nested inside the every_market_in_world iterator that saved the candidate as tv_trade_candidate_market. The `_location` suffix was never saved anywhere (no STATIC_MARKET_FIELDS entry and no other save_scope_as call produces it), so the representative-market assignment for the Trade League detail page silently wrote an unresolved scope into tv_trade_{prefix}_market_{rank}_{good} instead of the real candidate market. Fixed by correcting the reference to scope:tv_trade_candidate_market and regenerating tv_trade_league_effects.txt. |
| encoding | any | multiply = { value = X  multiply = local_var  divide = local_var  add = local_var } inside a script_value | Flatten to top-level sequence: multiply = local_var  (no braces); each op on its own line | Inline sub-expression example in .info only shows scalar literals; variable refs (local_*, named sv) inside multiply={} blocks are unconfirmed and likely silently broken |
| compatibility | scripted_effect | Defining an existing scripted effect again in a later or zz_-prefixed file without a REPLACE operation | Emit REPLACE:<effect_name> for each scripted effect whose body must change. The engine keeps the original effect and rejects the duplicate, so compatibility-only branches never run. |
| script | international_organization | Changing an IO type variable inside a scripted_effect with a bare change_variable, expecting callers to show the variable gain or loss | Wrap the IO-scope change_variable in custom_description = { text = <effect_loc_key> value = $value$ ... } and register <effect_loc_key> in common/effect_localization with global/first/third/past/negative perspectives. Add matching main_menu localization strings for those perspective keys. | Scripted effect calls can otherwise produce empty option/action tooltips even though the IO variable changes. Vanilla uses custom_description plus effect_localization for imperial, celestial, and papal authority so the tooltip shows the signed value while executing the real effect. |
| scope | any | Calling a scripted trigger from a scope it does not support because one of its internal scope links happens to reach the desired object | Verify the scripted trigger's entry scope from its complete body. Calling it directly from country makes its owner link invalid even though the nested privilege checks are country-scoped. |
| script | scripted_effects | Defining a top-level scripted_trigger-style block inside common/scripted_effects | Generate or move the `_trigger = { ... }` block to common/scripted_triggers, and keep common/scripted_effects for real scripted effects only. | EU5 parses every top-level block in common/scripted_effects as a scripted effect. If a generated trigger body is placed there, trigger clauses such as has_variable, religious_unity, or any_international_organizations_member_of are parsed as effect commands and log `Unknown effect ...`. |
| script | building | Passing a typed building_type:<id> value into location_and_owner_can_build | Pass the bare building id, e.g. location_and_owner_can_build = { building_type = tv_conquest_headquarters }.
