Supplying building_type:<id> as the parameter produces building_type:building_type:<id> and logs 'More than one colon in event target link'. |
| scope | any | target_flag = selected_artist (or any custom name) in select_trigger for 3-step generic actions | Use the vanilla naming convention: target_flag = target for step 1, target_flag = target_1 for step 2, target_flag = target_2 for step 3. Always wrap effect block in if = { limit = { exists = scope:target } ... } guard. | Custom target_flag names (e.g. selected_artist) in multi-step generic actions cause 'Undefined event target' / 'scope returned unset scope' errors at runtime. Engine appears to only reliably resolve target, target_1, target_2 for scope: lookups in effects. Missing exists guard causes THIRD_MOVE_CHARACTER_EFFECT loc failures when AI path skips selections. |
| scope | any | A later select_trigger uses source = world while visible/enabled/effect-preview logic reads scope:target or another earlier target_flag | Do not use source = world on a selector that depends on earlier selections. Omit source, use an inherited/actor source, or build an interaction_source_list so the chooser retains the previous target flags. | Trade League embargo selected a market, then opened a country selector with source = world and filtered countries by any_owned_location = { market = scope:target }. The selector showed no countries and logged 'Asking for a flag that's not in the interaction target chooser specified'. Removing source = world kept the later selector in the same interaction-target chooser so scope:target remained available. |
| gui | any | A `vbox` with `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` (the standard auto-height chain pattern, paired with `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking`) contains a bare `text_multi` leaf with only `max_width`/`autoresize = yes`/`layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` and no `minimumsize` of its own -- e.g. `tv_engineering_department.gui`'s `TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_ACTION_ONGOING`/`TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_FINALIZED_TEXT` text_multi blocks, direct children of a vbox at `:8132`/`:8224` with `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes`. | `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` computes the vbox's own size from the MINIMUM size each child reports -- not from any `layoutpolicy_horizontal`/`size` set on the vbox itself or its parent `widget` (those were tried first, confirmed to have zero effect, and reverted). A bare `text_multi` with no `minimumsize` reports 0 intrinsic width, so it silently zeroes out the whole chain: the vbox (and any ancestor `widget` that in turn auto-fits to the vbox) collapses to margin-only width, and the text renders unselectable at effectively 0 width, with no error in the log. Give the leaf `text_multi` an explicit `minimumsize = { W -1 }` matching its `max_width` -- matching the vanilla `minimumsize = { W -1 }` + `autoresize` idiom (e.g. `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/advances_lateralview.gui:331-332`). The already-working Pharos/Hagia text rows in this same file never hit this because they are fully self-contained fixed-size boxes (`size = { W H }`, both dimensions literal) that never depend on `set_parent_size_to_minimum` in the first place. | Two prior guesses were tested live by the user and confirmed to NOT fix this: (1) adding `ignoreinvisible = yes` to a sibling hbox -- irrelevant, wrong container; (2) adding/removing `layoutpolicy_horizontal`/`layoutpolicy_vertical` and explicit `size` on the ancestor `widget`/`vbox` (:8128, :8220) -- the user's own live measurements showed the vbox width stuck at exactly its own doubled horizontal margin (8 = 4+4) regardless of those properties, and removing the widget's `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking` entirely broke the widget's OWN width too (470 -> 8). Those measurements were the key evidence that pointed to `set_parent_size_to_minimum` computing from child minimums rather than from ancestor policy. `docs/knowledge/risk_cards/gui.md` rule 14 and the tooltip-row paragraph in `docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md` already documented giving the ROW/CONTAINER a `minimumsize` under `set_parent_size_to_minimum`, but neither stated that the LEAF content widget itself also needs one -- that gap is why this took three attempts to diagnose instead of one doc lookup. A follow-up project-wide regex/brace scan for other instances of this pattern produced far more false positives than real hits, because it cannot detect that an intervening fully-literal-sized `widget` (explicit numeric width AND height) breaks the dependency chain and makes everything inside it safe regardless of ancestors further up (e.g. `tv_engineering_department.gui:8263`/`:8298`-style ritual-requirement rows, and `tv_trade_league.gui:593`, both false-flagged). Only 2 additional real instances were confirmed by hand (`:8140`, `:8211`, same vbox as the original two). A reliable project-wide audit needs a `needs_parser` `validate.py` check that tracks literal-size-wrapper breaks, not a naive ancestor-substring scan. |
| gui | any | Copy-pasting the same ~30-line header button+tooltip block (icon, action_tooltip, tooltipwidget shell) into every instance of a shared card type instead of baking it into the type's default, and/or leaving a fully-overridden shared-type instance (every visible block already blockoverride'd) instantiating the type anyway. | When a shared `type X = hbox { block "A" {...} block "B" {...} }` gets the same non-trivial content repeated across ≥3 instances (this project's own ≥3-repeated-structural-unit threshold), move the repeated content into the type's own default for that block and expose ONE narrow placeholder block at the point per-instance content differs -- but that placeholder MUST sit directly in the outer type's own body, not nested inside a further `blockoverride` of a DIFFERENT type's instantiation (e.g. a `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = { blockoverride "tooltip_content" { ... } } }` embedded in the outer type). A per-instance `blockoverride` on the OUTER type could not reach a custom block declared two levels deep inside that nested type's own blockoverride -- confirmed broken in-game even though it validated and parsed fine (validate.py is a text linter, not the real block-resolution engine). Every OTHER working block-override in this codebase is either declared directly within the same type's body (no nested-type crossing), or is a block belonging to the nested type itself, overridden directly by the caller at the instantiation site (e.g. `ConceptTooltipType`'s own `title_icon`/`title_text`/`tooltip_content`, per `SimpleConceptTooltipType`/right_panel.gui precedent) -- not a custom block invented inside that nested override. Fix: expose one placeholder that wraps the ENTIRE nested type instantiation (e.g. `block "header_help_tooltip" { tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = {...default...} } }`), so each instance's override supplies the whole `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = { blockoverride ... } }` block directly -- more duplication than the two-placeholder version, but every override target is a proven-safe pattern. Conversely, before instantiating a shared type for a new generated/repeated component, check whether every block the type would provide is already going to be blockoverride'd (header fully blanked, background fully replaced, bottom content fully replaced) -- if so, the type contributes nothing but its outer layout skeleton and one non-overridden default property (here `card_common_bottom_margin`'s `margin = { 15 10 }`), which is cheap to reproduce directly without the type indirection. | Applied 2026-07-13: a Europedia-opening header help button (icon + action_tooltip + ConceptTooltipType tooltip) was first added by hand to 2 of 11 named tv_engineering_department_card_common instances, then baked into the base type's common_header_extra_left/right defaults once the plan was to apply it to all 11. First attempt used two custom placeholder blocks (`header_help_title_text`, `header_help_tooltip_rows`) nested inside the base type's own `blockoverride "title_text"`/`blockoverride "tooltip_content"` of a `ConceptTooltipType` instantiation -- this parsed cleanly and passed validate.py, but the user reported the Construction and Organization&Logistics cards' tooltip content had actually disappeared in-game. Re-architected to a single `block "header_help_tooltip" {}` wrapping the entire `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = {...} }` as a direct child of `button_regular` (same-type nesting, no crossing), with each of the 6 named cards now overriding that one block with its full tooltipwidget content -- verified via brace-balance + validate.py after the restructure; still pending an actual in-game confirmation. Separately, the panel's 8 auto-generated Unique Wonder Ceremony stage cards already overrode every block the type renders (header_size/header_decor_templates/common_header all blanked, card_bg fully replaced, common_bottom_content fully replaced), so they were converted to a hand-built vbox in the generator, reproducing only the type's real remaining contribution -- the card_common_bottom_margin default `margin = { 15 10 }` that the cards' hardcoded 432px content width (`462 - 15*2`) already depended on. |
| gui | any | Rendering per-entity dynamic inline text-icons (`@icon_name!`) at one shared `fontsize` on a `text_single`, and assuming every icon name renders at the same visible pixel size because the call site's fontsize is identical. | `reference_game_files/game/main_menu/gui/shared/font_icons.gui` defines every `texticon` block's `iconsize.size`/`iconsize.fontsize` independently per icon name, with no shared convention: e.g. `construction` is 14x14 native at fontsize 14, `wealth` is 20x20 at fontsize 18, `topography`/`army_tradition`/`prestige` range 24x24-26x26 at fontsize 16. The engine scales the glyph as `native_size * (call_fontsize / native_fontsize)`, so calling every icon at one shared fontsize (here, 17, in the Unique Wonder Ceremony's 8 status cards, one inline icon per wonder per stage picked from `data/unique_wonders.yaml`'s `ceremony.stages[i].icon`) produced visibly different pixel sizes per wonder (a ~14px icon next to a ~26px icon in the same 52x52 piechart slot), plus inconsistent vertical centering from each icon's own baked-in `offset`. Verified (Step 3, `reference_official_defines` + `reference_game_files`) that there is no per-instance size-override syntax for inline icon markup (no `@icon|size!` form), and no vanilla or existing-codebase precedent for resolving a GUI `texture` field via `Localize()`/`Custom()` to fetch a per-entity dynamic icon path (that path is text-only). The correct fix is NOT a new icon widget with a dynamically-resolved texture (would need ~650 dispatch widgets with long `Or`-chained `visible` expressions across 121 wonders x 8 stages x 2 piechart states — real GUI bloat and per-frame eval cost) and NOT curating icon choices to already-similar sizes (data-only triage, not pixel-perfect). Instead, define a PARALLEL set of `texticon` aliases — one per distinct icon name actually used — each reusing the same vanilla texture but with a uniform `size`/`offset`/`fontsize` matching the call site's own fontsize (so the render-time scale factor collapses to exactly 1 for every icon). Mods CAN declare their own `texticon` blocks reusing a vanilla texture with custom sizing (precedent: `reference_mods/3601047146/main_menu/gui/shared/glorpui_font_icons.gui`, `reference_mods/3599735023/in_game/gui/IV2_texticons.gui`). Implemented via `scripts_engineering_department/main_menu/gui/shared/gen_tv_ceremony_font_icons_gui.py`, which derives the distinct icon-name list from the ceremony data itself (not hardcoded) and looks up each name's real texture path from a new `FONT_ICON_TEXTURES` map in `wonder_mechanics/_core.py` (a texture-capturing sibling of the existing `FONT_ICON_DECL_RE`/`_load_font_icon_names()`/`SUPPORTED_CEREMONY_STAGE_ICONS` name validator). The two ceremony localization generators then emit `@tv_ceremony_<name>!` instead of `@<name>!` via a new `ceremony_icon_alias()` helper in `wonder_ceremony_lib.py` — zero changes to the `.gui` call sites, zero new widgets, zero runtime dispatch changes. | 2026-07-17: user reported the 8-step Unique Wonder Ceremony's left-side piechart center icon sizes were inconsistent and generally too small, asking whether inline text icons could be replaced with a normal icon component. Investigated the GUI icon display tier order first (a real `icon` widget needs a dynamic texture, which has no engine precedent via Localize/Custom — verification failed), then a grouped-conditional-icon-widget alternative (rejected for ~650-widget bloat given 121 wonders), before landing on the texticon-alias approach, confirmed by mod precedent. Icon size was set to 22x22 initially, then increased to 32x32 per user follow-up request (still fits inside the 52x52 piechart slot with margin). `test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py` and `scripts/validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report` both passed after the fix (0 errors/warnings). |

Boolean negation belongs in a NOT block; != makes the scripted effect fail to compile. |

| gui | any | Custom situation_panel leaves the shared default situation_subheader_content in place when no subheader content is needed | Add blockoverride "situation_subheader_content" {} near the top of the situation_panel block to suppress the default 45px empty row. | The shared situation_panel template includes a default subheader widget. | compatibility | any | Copying an entire vanilla static modifier into TRY_REPLACE when the intended change is only an additive numeric adjustment | Use TRY_INJECT with only the difference between the desired final modifier value and the verified vanilla value; omit game_data and unchanged fields | Static modifier values accumulate across injected entries. Delta-only injection preserves unrelated vanilla fields and allows other mods to contribute their own modifier deltas. Fields that cannot be neutralized additively still require replacement. |
| localization | modifier | Emitting a common/static_modifiers id without matching STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> localization in every supported main-menu language | Generate or add STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> for every static modifier id, including generated display-only families used by ShowModifierEffect. | Static modifiers load as modifier database entries and the localization system requests their STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME keys during startup or GUI display. scripts/validate.py checks static modifiers in both in_game and main_menu common/static_modifiers against all supported main-menu localization languages. |
| modifier | country | Writing subject_loyalty as a fractional percent-like value such as 0.10 | Write subject_loyalty as loyalty points, e.g. 10 for +10 loyalty. Vanilla advances use values such as subject_loyalty = 5 even though the modifier type displays decimals. | The modifier definition has decimals=2 but no percent=yes, and vanilla advances grant whole loyalty points. A 0.x value is effectively a tiny fraction of a loyalty point, not a 10% or +10 loyalty bonus. |
| scope | country | Writing a subjugation-style effect (or peace/interaction/IO effect) that unconditionally calls `make_subject_of = { type = subject_type:vassal ... }` on a player-chosen overlord | `subject_type:vassal` is invisible/uncreatable for any overlord with `has_advance = samanta_advance` (Indian-subcontinent feudalism); its vanilla substitute is `subject_type:samanta`. Branch on `scope:<overlord> = { has_advance = samanta_advance }` and call `make_subject_of` with `subject_type:vassal` when absent, `subject_type:samanta` when present. See docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md section 5.11. | Found fixing tv_alliance_sublime_empire_subjugate_member (src/in_game/common/generic_actions/tv_diplomatic_alliance_actions.txt), which called make_subject_of with a hardcoded subject_type:vassal — silently failing to subjugate alliance members whose leader country had researched samanta_advance. Any future TV mechanic that lets a player-chosen country become an overlord (alliance/IO subjugation, victory-path rewards, custom peace options) needs the same vassal/samanta branch instead of assuming vassal always works. |
| scope | any | Leaving set_local_variable/change_local_variable scratch values alive after the scripted effect has finished using them | At the matching evaluation scope, add remove_local_variable = <name> after the last read of every temporary local variable. In generated loops, generate the cleanup list together with the declarations so added goods or pop types cannot bypass it. | Local variables used as calculation scratch space are not durable caches. Explicit cleanup prevents repeated market, country, or UI calculations from retaining temporary state and growing memory use. |
| scope | any | Using `save_temporary_scope_as = <name>` inside a `limit` block (or other trigger context) when the same effect/file also does a real `save_scope_as = <name>` with the identical name for the loop's payload, e.g. `every_in_list = { limit = { save_temporary_scope_as = tv_display_region ... } save_scope_as = tv_display_region ... }` | Give the trigger-context temporary save a distinct name (e.g. `tv_display_region_check`) and keep the real `save_scope_as = tv_display_region` for the effect body outside `limit`. This mod's convention (see `tv_govhouse_refresh_governor_display_effect`) is `<name>_check` for the limit-only temporary. | Engine logs `Trying to add the temporary target '<name>' which has the same name as a permanent target` and the effect/action silently fails at runtime (reproduced by appointing a regional governor). Temporary and permanent scope target names share one namespace per game-object scope; a `save_temporary_scope_as` registered inside a `limit`/trigger context conflicts with a later `save_scope_as` of the same name in the same effect chain, even though `save_temporary_scope_as` is otherwise the mandatory form inside trigger contexts (see EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md 'save_scope_as is an effect only'). |
| gui | any | A `TooltipRequirementsList = { ... maximumsize = { N -1 } ... }` block with N narrower than the template's default minimumsize (400px, from `@tooltip_inner_wide = @tooltip_title_wide(420) - @tooltip_inner_margin(10)*2`) and no `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize"` to match -- e.g. tv_engineering_department.gui's three wonder-debate demand-row TooltipRequirementsList instances (nobles/burghers/clergy), each declaring only `maximumsize = { 230 -1 }`. | Whenever `maximumsize` on a `TooltipRequirementsList` is set narrower than the inherited 400px default, also add `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize" { minimumsize = { N -1 } }` with the same N. Without it, the wider inherited minimumsize wins over the narrower maximumsize and the widget renders near its ~400px minimum regardless, which -- inside a non-clipping `layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` ancestor chain -- bubbles up through every expanding container and stretches an outer fixed-`maximumsize` card wider than declared. Match the working precedent already used by every other TooltipRequirementsList instance in the same file (e.g. the 'suitability location conditions' blocks pairing `maximumsize = { 218 -1 }` with `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize" { minimumsize = { 218 -1 } }`). | Reported by the user as the Engineering Department wonder-debate Card (declared maximumsize = { 500 -1 }) visibly widening only while the debate stage (tv_wonder_stage == 1) was active, then reverting to 500px once the debate ended -- exactly matching the demand rows' visibility gate. Fixed by adding the missing tooltip_minimumsize override to all three TooltipRequirementsList instances. |
| gui | any | Placing a `TooltipStringPairList` inside a fixed-width column (`layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed`, `maximumsize = { W -1 }`) to show `ShowModifierEffect(...)`/`ShowModifier(...)` breakdown rows, without also bounding the row text itself. | Vanilla `TooltipStringPairList` (`reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/cooltip_types.gui`) renders each row through `TooltipStringPairListContent` -> two `TooltipTableColumn` children (label expanding, value fixed), each of which blockoverrides the shared `type TooltipTableColumn`'s `field_text` (`main_menu_cooltip_types.gui` ~L1771: `autoresize = yes` with no `max_width`) down to a `field_text_format` splice block that by default has zero width constraint. A parent's `maximumsize`/`layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` only bounds the StringPairList's own declared box; it does not clip or wrap the child `text_single`'s natural single-line width, so long English modifier-effect text (short in Chinese, long in English) renders past the column's right edge with no error in the log. Fix by extending the existing `blockoverride "field_text_format" { fontsize = ... }` (already present at every StringPairList call site in this codebase for font sizing) to also add `autoresize = yes`, `max_width = ...`, and `elide = right` — verified as a valid vanilla `text_single` combination at `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/shared/io_tooltips.gui:459-466` (`text_single = { autoresize = yes elide = right using = tooltip_max_width_title_wide text = ... }`). This truncates with an ellipsis instead of overflowing, and does not require touching the vanilla `.gui` template at all, since `field_text_format` is already the caller-facing splice point. IMPORTANT: the label and value columns are two SIDE-BY-SIDE `TooltipTableColumn`/`TooltipTableField` instances sharing the ONE declared column width, and the caller-level `field_text_format` override reaches BOTH columns' identically-named nested block simultaneously (confirmed by the pre-existing shared `fontsize` override applying uniformly). The first fix attempt set `max_width = column_width - 25`, accounting only for the label column's OWN `margin={10,0}` + `spacing=5` chrome (~30px) and ignoring that the adjacent value column reserves its own ~30px chrome plus its own (short but nonzero) rendered text width out of the SAME total — that left almost no headroom for the value column and the overflow persisted. The correct budget is `column_width - (label_chrome ~30) - (value_chrome ~30) - (value_text_reserve ~45)` = `column_width - 105`, not `column_width - 25`. | 2026-07-17: user reported the Engineering Department wonder construction card and the location-page wonder tooltip both call two-column ShowModifierEffect breakdowns inside a fixed ~223px/218px column, and under English localization the modifier text (short enough in Chinese to fit) overflowed past the column's right edge instead of being capped around the expected ~200px content width. Traced the render chain from the mod's own `TooltipStringPairList` call site down through vanilla `cooltip_types.gui`/`main_menu_cooltip_types.gui` to confirm the shared `TooltipTableColumn` type's `field_text` has no built-in width cap, then found `field_text_format` is the existing splice point already used in this codebase for `fontsize` overrides — extended it in both generator scripts (`preview_modifier_column`, `render_modifier_column`) with `autoresize = yes` / `max_width = column_width - 25` / `elide = right`, regenerated via each script plus their merge/compat steps (`merge_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py`, `scripts/compat/gen_tv_meiou_and_taxes_location_window.py`), and confirmed `scripts/validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report` passed clean. User then pointed out the chosen width was still too generous and asked for a more careful pass: `column_width - 25` only subtracted the label column's own margin/spacing chrome and ignored that the value column sitting next to it in the same row draws from the SAME declared column width — corrected to `column_width - 105` (label chrome ~30 + value chrome ~30 + value text reserve ~45), regenerated again, `validate.py` re-confirmed clean. User then tested in-game and asked to widen the label back out to a flat `max_width = 140` (wider than the derived ~118/113 estimate, still narrower than the original overflowing 198/193) — set as a literal constant in both generators rather than a `column_width`-derived formula, since the user's in-game check is the authoritative signal here, not the chrome arithmetic estimate. |
| modifier | country | trade_efficiency = <value> | Use trade_land_efficiency and trade_sea_efficiency separately (both category=country, percent) | trade_efficiency does not exist in EU5; split into trade_land_efficiency + trade_sea_efficiency |
| script | international_organization | Trade League monopoly control computed from produced/traded/supply market proxies, old global-vs-IO goods totals, or representative-market-only samples | Compute monopoly control from a full scan of world markets using market every_export/every_import routes filtered by goods = goods:<good> and summed with trade_volume. Annual representative markets are display/decision support only. | produced_in_market:<good> and goods_demand_in_market(goods:<good>) are valid for weighting origin and consumer control and for choosing representative markets; traded_in_market and goods_supply_in_market are not route volume. Export/transit control uses market every_export trade_volume; consumer control uses market every_import trade_volume. |
| performance | international_organization | Generating one market every_export/every_import route scan per good when the mechanic needs all goods from matching routes | Scan each route once, save the route good with `traded_goods = { save_scope_as = ... }`, and use that goods scope as a variable_map key or list item. | script | any | trigger_event = { id = X } or trigger_event = X inside scripted_effects or on_action | Use trigger_event_non_silently = { id = X } for popup events shown to the player, or trigger_event_silently = X for background events. Both accept simple form (= event_id) and object form (= { id = X days = Y }). | EU5 does not have a bare trigger_event effect. In scripted_effects and on_action files, the engine reports 'Unknown effect trigger_event'. The two valid variants are trigger_event_non_silently (shows popup) and trigger_event_silently (hidden/background). This distinction does not apply inside event option = { } blocks, which may use a different syntax. |
| localization | any | MY_KEY = { global = MY_KEY } when the trigger using MY_KEY has custom_tooltip nested inside custom_description | Declare all four perspectives: MY_KEY = { none = MY_KEY  global = MY_KEY  first = MY_KEY  third = MY_KEY }. Use this form for every trigger_localization entry as a safe default. | EU5 engine requires the 'first' perspective when custom_description wraps a nested custom_tooltip. A global-only entry causes '(BUG: KEY missing perspective. Could not find parameters for PROMOUN first Negative required false)' at runtime. Adding all four perspectives is zero-cost and prevents this for any trigger regardless of nesting. |
| modifier | location | Assuming every new location-scoped static modifier definition must be prefixed `TRY_REPLACE:`, per CLAUDE.md's/Towards_Victory_Design.md's general wording about location-scoped statics. | `TRY_REPLACE:<name> = { ... }` is a merge-conflict-resolution prefix used specifically to override an ALREADY-EXISTING modifier name (vanilla or another mod's) — confirmed against reference-mod usage (e.g. `TRY_REPLACE:province_starving`, a real vanilla modifier id). A brand-new `tv_`-namespaced modifier name has no such collision and needs no wrapper — every existing wonder-domain location-modifier generator in this codebase (e.g. gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_ritual_auxiliary_location_modifiers.py, gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cost_local_modifiers.py) already omits `TRY_REPLACE` and works correctly. Only reach for `TRY_REPLACE` when the name you are defining already exists elsewhere. | Found 2026-07 while adding the Unique Wonder Ceremony's location-scoped temporary cost modifiers. CLAUDE.md's 'Critical EU5 Gotchas' section and docs/design/Towards_Victory_Design.md both state TRY_REPLACE as a blanket rule for location-scoped static modifiers, which overstates the actual, narrower requirement — flagged for a future wording pass rather than silently worked around. See docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 26. |
| event | country | Writing `value = leader_country.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)` (or any other parenthesis-argument event target link, e.g. `estate_power(...)`, chained after a scope and used directly as the RHS of `value =`, a comparison, `add =`, `subtract =`, etc.) without wrapping the whole scope+call expression in double quotes | Wrap the entire chained expression in double quotes: `value = "leader_country.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)"`. Every vanilla usage of a function-call-style event target link (estate_power(...), estate(...), etc.) used as a script value or trigger operand is quoted, e.g. reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/disasters/coup_attempt.txt:13 `power > "root.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)"` and reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/attribute_columns/57_parliament_issues.txt:40 `value = "scope:actor.estate_power(root.estate_type)"`. | Runtime symptom is a cluster of 3 errors per occurrence: jomini_eventtarget.cpp 'No data specified for an event target link that requires data. Link estate_power', jomini_scriptvalue.h 'Cannot read [leader_country.estate_power] as a script value', and pdx_persistent_reader.cpp 'Unexpected token: (' immediately followed by 'Unexpected token: )' and 'Unexpected token: =' on the next two lines -- the parser splits on the bare `(` because unquoted script-value RHS syntax does not support inline function-call arguments; quoting makes the whole expression parse as one compound math-value string instead. Found across 47 identical occurrences in a single generated file (tv_academy_of_sciences.txt), all from the same 3 codegen template lines. |
| encoding | any | Saving common/ scripts (static_modifiers, situations, scripted_effects, on_action, etc.) as plain UTF-8 without a byte-order mark | Save with UTF-8 BOM (first 3 bytes EF BB BF). Verify with `head -c 3 <file> | xxd` -> expect ef bb bf. | Engine retries without BOM but emits a warning per file per load. Localization YAMLs already require BOM; this rule extends the same requirement to common/ .txt scripts. Auto-generated files via scripts/gen_scaffold.py should always emit the BOM. |
| modifier | any | Using a v1.2-era `_cost`/`_cost_modifier` static modifier name (e.g. `global_build_buildings_cost`, `stability_cost`, `court_spending_cost_modifier`, `global_bureaucracy_maintenance_cost_modifier`) — these were renamed to `_efficiency` in EU5 v1.3. | Rename to the v1.3 `_efficiency` identifier (see reference_game_files/game/main_menu/common/static_modifiers/00_modifier_types.txt) and negate the value: `color=bad->good` flips at the same time, so a value that reduced cost (e.g. -0.10) becomes a positive efficiency value (e.g. 0.10). Exception: `stability_cost` -> `stability_cost_efficiency` and `court_spending_cost_modifier` -> `court_spending_efficiency` (name doesn't just gain the suffix). Confirmed exact `x-1` negation via matched vanilla `inflation_impact`/`construction_wages` country_modifier blocks; for fields without a matched-block confirmation, this project's convention (see reverted scripts/archive/revert_1_3_modifier_efficiency_to_1_2_cost.ps1) is a uniform x-1 sign flip, treated as a placeholder pending a real balance pass. | v1.3 renamed ~29 `_cost`-suffixed static modifiers to `_efficiency`. Also lost the `min=-0.95` floor clamp on the three `global_bureaucracy_*_cost_modifier` -> `_efficiency` fields. Discovered during the v1.3 migration pass (2026-07-10); ~114 usage sites found across hand-written src files and ~10 generator data YAMLs (victory_paths.yaml, trade_league_laws.yaml, govhouse_laws.yaml, arts_exhibition_laws.yaml, academy_laws.yaml, wonder_final_buildings.yaml, wonder_generic_rituals.yaml, unique_wonders.yaml). Unrelated to the separate v1.3 building_type.cpp validator capping local_manpower/employment_size <= 0.0125 (undocumented, not a rename). |
| event | any | `outcome = good` / `outcome = bad` at event top level (the `EAudioEventOutcome` audio-direction field) | Use `outcome = positive` / `outcome = negative` (keep `outcome = neutral` unchanged). Confirmed valid enum set from `reference_game_files/game/in_game/events/readme.txt` line 72: `outcome = positive/neutral/negative`, identical in both the pre-v1.3 and v1.3 reference commits — v1.3 only made the `pdx_persistent_reader.cpp` enum parser strict about rejecting values outside that set (error.log: `Failed to read enum! Type: "EAudioEventOutcome", Val "bad"/"good"`). | Found 2026-07-10 in error.log across ~841 sites in 17 TV event files after the v1.3 reference bump. Fix at the generator/data source, never the generated .txt, then regenerate. |
| workflow | any | scripts/validate.py's check_modifier_names treated every top-level-looking `word = number` line as a modifier-name assertion, including script_value operator keywords (value/add/subtract/multiply/divide/min/max/...) nested inside an auto_modifiers/static_modifiers entry's `scales_with = { ... }` or `limit = { ... }` sub-block. | check_modifier_names now tracks a per-line brace-depth stack and only applies the modifier-name whitelist check to lines outside any `scales_with`/`limit` nested block. (A separate attempt to also auto-whitelist every `common/prices/*.txt` entry's `<name>_cost_modifier` was reverted — that tag is NOT engine-auto-derived, it requires its own explicit `modifier_type_definitions` entry like any other custom modifier, so auto-whitelisting it would have hidden real missing-registration bugs from validate.py instead of catching them. See price_gold_field_rejects_dynamic_scriptvalue.) | Discovered while fixing the direct-build price bug: a correct, vanilla-proven scales_with block (`scales_with = { value = global_var:X multiply = 1000 divide = 10000 subtract = 1 }`) plus a legitimate `tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_cost_modifier = 1` line both false-positived as unknown modifiers. Verified the fix against the full repo (`scripts/validate.py --ai-report`, no --changed filter): 288 files checked, 0 errors, 0 warnings — no other auto_modifiers/static_modifiers file relied on the old (incorrect) flat-line behavior. |
| scope | any | Using max = 0 when a value expression must be clamped to a nonnegative lower bound | Use min = 0 for a lower bound and max = <positive limit> for an upper bound; verify both sides when writing a bounded value expression. | EU5 value expressions apply min as the lower clamp and max as the upper clamp. | event | any | Using REPLACE: or INJECT: for an individual vanilla event, or copying that event into a mod event file which loads after its base-game source file | Create a new event file whose filename sorts before the relevant base file (for example 0000_modded_events.txt), repeat the vanilla namespace, and copy only each modified event under its bare ID. Preserve the complete event block. If it calls a helper defined in the base event file, inline that helper's contents in the event or copy the helper under a new mod-specific name. The resulting duplicate-event-ID log entry is expected and harmless. | The event manager applies the earlier duplicate definition; event-specific REPLACE: and INJECT: are not a valid replacement mechanism. |
| script | any | limit = { var:some_var >= var:other_var } when some_var may not be set on the scope | Add has_variable = some_var to the limit block before the var: comparison: limit = { has_variable = some_var  var:some_var >= var:other_var } | EU5 does NOT default unset variables to 0 in trigger comparisons; var:X on the left side of >= throws a script error and aborts the if-block. set_variable = { value = var:X } with an unset var appears to be silent (no error), so only limit comparisons need the has_variable guard. |
| scope | any | var:X ?= { trigger_event_non_silently = ... } or var:X ?= { change_variable = ... } inside a random_list weight branch that is itself inside another random_list or every_character | Navigate to the scope ONCE before the random_list using save_scope_as: var:tv_exhibition_home_country ?= { save_scope_as = touring_home }. Then use scope:touring_home = { ... } inside every branch. Guard the outer random_list with if = { limit = { exists = scope:touring_home } }. | var:X ?= {} is the optional-navigation operator: it silently skips the block if the variable is unset or the target doesn't exist. Inside deeply nested random_list branches (every_character → random_list { 8 = { random_list { weight = { ... } } } }) the optional scope switch silently fails — no engine error is logged, the block is skipped, and no event fires. Pre-saving the target as a named scope before the random_list and using scope:name = {} inside branches is the reliable pattern. |
| scope | any | scope:actor = { has_variable = X } followed by scope:actor = { var:X = { ... } } in a generic action allow/custom_tooltip/trigger, expecting has_variable to suppress the var:X read | Use the optional variable link for variable-dependent reads: var:X ?= { ... }, var:X ?= 1, or var:X ?= { this >= N }. For generic actions, move a prerequisite to potential only when the action should disappear entirely. Do not rely on has_variable, custom_tooltip order, or separate sibling scope blocks to prevent a direct var:X read. | EU5 generic action UI evaluation may fetch every trigger line needed for tooltips, including sibling scope blocks inside allow. A direct var:X = { ... } fetches X before the logical result of has_variable can protect it, producing 'Failed to fetch variable' and 'Event target link var returned an unset scope'. Optional ?= returns false when the variable/scope is absent and avoids the error. |
| script | any | remove_from_variable_list = { name = X target = Y } for persistent variable lists | remove_list_variable = { name = X target = Y } to remove a specific entry. add_to_variable_list = { name = X target = Y } is still correct for adding. Temporary lists (add_to_list) use a different API: every_in_list = { list = X } and remove_from_list = X. | EU5 uses remove_list_variable (not remove_from_variable_list) to remove a scope reference from a persistent variable list. | script | any | every_in_variable_list = { name = X ... } or remove_from_variable_list = { name = X target = Y } for persistent variable lists | every_in_list = { variable = X ... } to iterate; remove_list_variable = { name = X target = Y } to remove a specific entry. add_to_variable_list = { name = X target = Y } is still correct for adding. Temporary lists (add_to_list) use a different API: every_in_list = { list = X } and remove_from_list = X. | EU5 has two distinct list types: (1) Temporary lists created with add_to_list — iterated with every_in_list { list = X }, removed with remove_from_list = X. (2) Persistent variable lists created with add_to_variable_list — iterated with every_in_list { variable = X }, removed with remove_list_variable { name = X target = Y }. every_in_variable_list and remove_from_variable_list do not exist in EU5; the engine reports 'Unknown effect' for both. |
| scope | variable_map | Using root inside a variable-map key callback called from a generic-action effect to write back to the action actor/current country | Save the current owner scope before random_key_in_variable_map/every_key_in_variable_map, then write back through scope:<saved_owner>; preserve the variable-map key stream instead of replacing it with generated per-id branches. | Generic action selector hover can pre-evaluate action effects without a usable root event target. | scope | variable_map | Calling is_key_in_variable_map on the current every_key_in_variable_map/ordered_key_in_variable_map callback scope, especially with target = this | Save the map-owning scope before the key iterator, copy this into a local variable, and run is_key_in_variable_map inside scope:<saved_owner> with target = local_var:<key>. | In a key iterator callback the current scope may be the numeric key itself (for example 1.00), which does not support scope-owned variables. | scope | variable_map | Calling is_key_in_variable_map or variable_map(...) for a country-scoped map directly from an every_in_list/random_in_list/any_in_list item scope such as a region, location, or character | Save the country/map owner before the item iterator, copy the dynamic key to a local variable, and run both is_key_in_variable_map and the quoted variable_map scope link inside scope:<saved_owner>. | Governor's House display refresh iterated tv_govhouse_region_list, making the current scope a region. | event | variable_map | A non-silent confirmation event's hidden follow-up re-reads a variable_map/global_variable_map entry for the event's key and identity-matches it against the scope captured when the event was queued, instead of validating the captured scope directly | When an event can stay open for arbitrary game time before the player answers, do not treat a variable_map lookup at confirm-time as the source of truth for what the player is confirming. Validate the scope saved at queue time/immediate (ownership, building state, etc.) directly, and only write it back into the map after that direct validation succeeds. Make any trailing unconditional cleanup effect conditional on the confirmation having actually committed, so a failed confirm leaves the pending state for the real recovery/re-request path instead of silently discarding it. | tv_govhouse_confirm_local_administration_event_scope_effect re-derived scope:tv_govhouse_selected_admin_location from tv_govhouse_region_to_admin_location_map and required it to `this =` match scope:tv_govhouse_pending_local_admin_scope. Because tv_govhouse.5 is trigger_event_non_silently and can sit open while on_location_changed_owner / admin-location-loss handling legitimately reselects the map's candidate for the region, the map value could drift away from the location the player actually built on. The identity match then silently failed, tv_governor_appoint_effect (and therefore the tv_govhouse_region_display_list rebuild) was skipped, and the unconditional trailing cleanup discarded the pending request anyway -- making the region vanish from the Governor's House list. Fixed by validating scope:tv_govhouse_pending_local_admin_scope directly (owner ?= only -- NOT has_building_with_at_least_one_level, since the same instant construct_building in this option's visible effect does not reliably update that read yet) and gating the cleanup on a committed flag. |

| scope | country | Reading a country-solver class coefficient in the tens or hundreds (e.g. Class 1 = 105.98) as a data error or numerical blow-up | Expect it. Capacity targets are baseline_total * capacity_floor + baseline_total * negative_pool * pressure_share, and pressure is clipped at zero, so a stratum whose baseline is BELOW target (needs scaling up) contributes zero pressure and its class receives only the 1% floor. That class column then carries ~1-3% of the mass of the dominant class, and ||W^-1 row|| reaches 96-404, so a normal 0.01 monthly move in a compression ratio swings that coefficient by 1-4 units. Switching capacity to abs(baseline - target) cuts median amplification from 120.6 to 55.9 but does NOT improve feasibility and shrinks the surviving Class 4 margin from 0.164 to 0.033. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Amplification and feasibility are independent problems: amplification comes from unequal column mass (the 1% anchor), feasibility comes from cone containment. Raising capacity_floor trades jitter for margin in both directions, so it is a tuning decision rather than a fix. HUN is especially exposed because nobles are 68% of national spending yet carry zero pressure, leaving the class that represents them with almost no matrix mass. |
| modifier | country | Reading global commodity demand while the previous SOL substitution modifier is still included, or writing M-1 despite an external additive offset | Remove the own named country modifier, read modifier:global_<good>_pop_demand, and write (1+e)(M-1). Apply the same nonnegative effective factor once in internal spending. Read live modifier values rather than copying age definitions. | Modifier read syntax is source-verified. Same-type addition and cross-type multiplication were measured on 2026-10-10; removal-to-read timing, engine refresh and fixed-point stability of this generated implementation still require in-game acceptance. |
| scope | country | Copying vanilla or framework entries into monthly_country_pulse/yearly_country_pulse just to preserve existing on_actions | Use the compact yearly_country_pulse style for both monthly_country_pulse and yearly_country_pulse: define only this mod's on_actions entries. The engine merges on_actions from duplicate pulse definitions automatically. | Manual payload copies are noisy and drift-prone. Keep events/random_events only when this mod itself adds them; do not duplicate vanilla or CMF on_action dispatchers. |
| scope | country | Diagnosing a country-solver negative_factor or a class-wide -100% local_pop_demand by inspecting condition number, classifier churn, coefficient magnitude, or by assuming a near-zero coefficient was a clamped floating-point artifact | Run tools/eu5_save_parser/cone_feasibility.py FIRST. Exact-solve feasibility means target in cone(class columns); because every class column sums nonnegative location vectors, cone(any 4-way partition) is contained in cone(all owned locations). If the all-location residual exceeds ~0.01 the target is unreachable and NO classifier change, class count, capacity rule, or conditioning fix can help - only relaxing the target or accepting an approximation. See docs/technical/SOL_Country_Level_Strata_Demand_Model.md section 3.9. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Linear independence is NOT the feasibility criterion: det(M) != 0 only gives uniqueness, and measured HUN condition numbers were a healthy 55.6-96.0 while the exact solve still failed. A zero coefficient returned by a nonnegative approximation is a legitimate boundary optimum, not a clamped artifact, so tightening the gate to reject delta < 0 does not stop -100% (it cannot reject = 0). Four wrong attributions were ruled out by data: silent zero-clamping, ill-conditioning, the yearly unit-spending refresh as switch trigger (1337.10/11/12 had frozen unit spending yet flipped infeasible->feasible), and classifier churn as an independent cause (1338 data under 1337 classes still gave Class 4 = -1.089). The real conflict is structural: nobles/clergy need scaling up while lower needs scaling down ~41%, but their per-location spending is coupled (nobles-vs-lower share correlation 0.29-0.47), so no nonnegative factor field separates them. |
| scope | country | Repeating market/pop basket expansion for every location and replacing unchanged country goods modifiers every month | Aggregate owned population by market/pop type, evaluate each basket once, and cache seven country-adjusted market spending totals. Apply permanent goods modifiers only on real size changes or missing modifiers; subtract the actually applied size when reading external offsets and retain an observed-total snapshot for clean-month checks. Keep the separate monthly location modifier lifetime policy. | Offline 180-location/3-market fixture cuts refresh variable operations from 571285 to 12023. This is not measured EU5 wall time; intermediate fixed-point rounding and engine cache invalidation require in-game acceptance. |
| scope | country | Seeing every sol_location_* column empty in an exported save and concluding the parser broke, the export is corrupt, or the save is unusable for analysis | Check metadata.json's countries_with_sol_variables field first. A value of 0 means the save itself carries no SOL runtime state -- the mod was absent or disabled when it was written -- so no amount of re-parsing will produce those columns. Confirm against the raw save with 'grep -c sol_location_demand_class <save>.eu5': a working save returns tens of thousands of hits, an unusable one returns 0. Crucially, such a save is NOT useless: every engine-native column still exports (population per stratum, shares, owner, market, rank, development, control), which is enough for population-structure and heterogeneity analysis. Only SOL-derived quantities are lost -- per-stratum base spending B_s(l), net income I_s(l), and the raw coefficient m_raw(l) -- which means feasibility and improvement analysis is impossible because the target vector t cannot be reconstructed. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). The earlier cone_survey note recorded this save as '623 no-valid-location countries and 0 scored, likely a data issue', which reads as a defect to investigate rather than a save that simply predates or excludes the mod. The distinction matters because the two situations call for opposite responses: a parser defect needs fixing, whereas a SOL-less save should be routed to the analyses it can still support. Verified usable for the heterogeneity measurement in tools/eu5_save_parser/heterogeneity_survey.py: it yields 295 countries with 5+ locations and the widest noble-share spread of any save examined (median 7.1x max/min, versus 3.8x in 1337). It is not in that tool's default save list -- the write-up settled on three timepoints -- but pass it explicitly with --saves 1536_09_21 to include it. The point stands regardless: a SOL-less save is a restricted dataset, not a broken one. |

Mocks now carry raw weights and report missing script values (2026-10-13). |
| scope | any | Using change_local_variable min=0 as a nonnegative lower-bound clamp | Use set_local_variable with value={ value=... min=0 } to express the lower bound. Do not copy value-block clamp semantics into the change-local effect or its offline interpreter. | Every recorded sample has new_total=actual_own, hence external baseline must be1, but the old local-effect min produced0. The offline fixture incorrectly modeled this as max(value,0), concealing mass monthly AI re-solving. Only this local-effect behavior is established by saves; do not reverse persistent-variable or script-value clamp rules globally. |
| scope | country | Calling gls_compute_savings_pressure unconditionally for every country (including AI) from the global situation's on_monthly, and again calling gls_compute_panel_display (which itself just re-runs gls_compute_savings_pressure) from gls_full_refresh_country right after sol_update_local_pop_demand_modifiers already refreshed it | Gate gls_compute_savings_pressure in the situation on_monthly behind is_human = yes (AI never reads gls_savings_pressure_adjustment for panel display; the value that actually affects gameplay is recomputed monthly per-country by sol_update_local_pop_demand_modifiers via monthly_country_pulse). Drop the gls_compute_panel_display call from gls_full_refresh_country since sol_update_local_pop_demand_modifiers in the same effect chain already refreshed the identical cache. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). gls_compute_savings_pressure only writes owner.var:gls_savings_pressure_adjustment, which sol_compute_location_pop_demand reads for the applied SOL modifier. For AI countries that cache is overwritten every month anyway by their own monthly_country_pulse tick before it can be consumed for display, so the situation's every-country every-month call was pure O(N_countries) waste. Same redundancy pattern as yearly_january_pulse_spike but on the monthly cadence; found during a 2026-07-28 review of monthly-tick computation cost. |
| goods | pop | Calibrating pop demand while leaving either NPop.DEVELOPMENT_SCALE_ON_DEMAND or per-good development_threshold values active | Treat them as two independent controls. Set NPop.DEVELOPMENT_SCALE_ON_DEMAND = 0 to remove the engine-wide multiplier, and fully negate every goods development_threshold with a late INJECT so vanilla + inject = 0. Compatibility goods such as PP victuals need their own negation. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). DEVELOPMENT_SCALE_ON_DEMAND = 0 removes the universal (1 + development * scale) multiplier but does not clear goods gates. Conversely, negating every development_threshold does not remove the universal multiplier. Both layers must be disabled for development-independent demand calibration. |
| goods | pop | Taking over a compatibility good's pop demand while leaving loaded continent_demand_modifier or sub_continent_demand_modifier blocks active | Audit every source goods block for continent_demand_modifier and sub_continent_demand_modifier. When the compatibility patch owns final demand, emit late negative INJECT blocks that exactly cancel each recorded key, just like wealth/development gate negations. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). These goods-level regional modifiers change pop demand by continent or sub-continent after the base demand values are loaded. A compatibility target that recalibrates demand_add but leaves them active gets regionally divergent final demand. The Just Goods compatibility generator now audits and negates them for aromatics, palmoil, soap, and whaleoil. |
| goods | pop | Calibrating strata income and pop demand while leaving NPop.POP_NEEDS_INCOME_SCALE at the vanilla value of 1.33 | Set NPop.POP_NEEDS_INCOME_SCALE = 1 in every base SOL deploy target so pop-needs expenditure uses the nominal cost of purchased goods | The vanilla 1.33 value makes pops pay 33% above nominal goods cost. Leaving it active breaks SOL's income-closed strata balance independently of demand quantities and development controls. |
| scope | country | Implementing a 'reduce calculation frequency' toggle by moving both the solve AND the add_location_modifier call to yearly_country_pulse with years = 1, so the modifier's lifetime is what carries the value between solves | Split the effect: keep applying the modifier every month from the cached per-location size variable (months = 1, mode = replace) and move only the expensive solve to yearly_country_pulse. A stale coefficient re-applied monthly is acceptable; a lapsed modifier is not. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). Relying on a years = 1 modifier to bridge yearly solves looks self-sustaining in steady state but breaks at every entry point that is not the yearly pulse: game start, save load, player-to-AI handover, a newly formed country, and switching the toggle on mid-game all write (or leave) a months = 1 modifier that expires long before the country's own anniversary date arrives. The country then runs on the bare demand_add baseline for up to 11 months with no error and no log line. Applying monthly from the cache also makes the toggle safe to flip at any time, and costs only the every_owned_location write the default configuration was already paying. |
| scope | country | Subtracting a cached requested modifier size from a live aggregate getter and interpreting any difference as an external modifier change | When the named modifier exists, read country_modifier_strength(<modifier key>) and subtract that actual strength. Keep the last requested size for change-only writes and a separate live-strength snapshot for own drift detection/repair. Account internal spending with the actual applied strength. | Controlled offline tests cover corrupt request caches, actual own drift, real 0.00001 external changes and stale diagnostic payload. They do not prove engine precision/refresh timing or establish the cause of the user's tenfold performance regression. Ordinary clean guards do not add strength getter calls. |
| gui | any | Generating the obsolete full-copy main_menu/gui/messagetypes.txt for SOL after the EU5 1.4.3 directory migration | Run scripts/gen_sol_messages.py to generate only the SOL recalculation message in main_menu/gui/messagetypes/SOL_messages.txt for stable and sol_standalone. Remove the obsolete generated file; compatibility submods inherit the base target's registration and must not duplicate it. | The id is retained for continuity; its fixed-path correction applied to older game versions. In 1.4.3 all files in gui/messagetypes/ are read and duplicate ids log both file paths. The shared rule owns engine behavior; this entry records SOL's generator and target ownership. Native registration still needs an in-game check. |
| scope | country | Describing or reasoning about the country demand solver's approximation strategy from BRIEF.md / PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md prose (e.g. 'player four-strategy L2 path', 'AI proportional_fast', 'minimax vertices', 'active-set candidates') without grepping the generated solver first | Grep the generated file before describing runtime behaviour: 'grep -c <effect_name> src/stable/in_game/common/scripted_effects/B_SOL_country_demand_solver.txt'. A count of 1 means definition-only (dead code, never called); 0 means the effect is not rendered at all and exists only in the generator's _SOLVER_EFFECTS name list used for file routing. As of 2026-08-13 only two strategies are live: id 7 exact-direct and id 8 the 209-case vertex sweep, with id 0 for raw_fallback. The objective is scaled L1 (mean of the four per-row relative residuals) with scaled L-infinity as a 1% tie-break, under a hard no-worsen constraint against raw -- NOT an L2 least-squares fit. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). BRIEF.md was self-contradictory: its Core Features paragraph correctly stated the L2 variants and minimax_ratio were retired, while the SOL System Architecture and runtime-cost paragraphs further down still described the retired L2/proportional_fast path and strategy ids 1-6. Reading the latter produced a technical write-up describing three algorithms that do not exist in the shipped mod. Two further traps: (a) the offline tool tools/eu5_save_parser/demand_analysis.py DOES still implement balanced_l2/improvement_l2/target_l2/absolute_l2/minimax_ratio/proportional_fast as comparison baselines, so finding them there is not evidence they run in-game; (b) A_SOL_economy_effects.txt still carries a CMF log branch for strategy id 6 (proportional fast) that no code path can now produce. Prefer the generated .txt as ground truth over any prose, including this file. |
| gui | any | Using _show/_hide plus global SetMapMode/SetDefaultMapMode to manage a mapmode for a dynamically instantiated situation panel | Use trigger_on_create with PdxGuiWidget.PushMapModeOverride on a zero-size child of the situation-specific panel. This fires when the child is created already visible and ties cleanup to that widget's destruction. | _show only runs after an existing widget changes from hidden to shown. It is not a construction callback, so it misses a situation child created after its parent is already shown. A global SetDefaultMapMode cleanup can also discard the player's prior mapmode instead of restoring the overridden mode. |
| modifier | country | Writing size = M - 1 for a per-good country demand modifier when the same good already has other global_<good>_pop_demand sources, or changing SOL accounting for a good without changing its engine demand | Sources of the same modifier type add inside one factor (1 + sum); different types (per-good, global_pop_demand, local_pop_demand, vanilla percent rows) multiply. For target relative factor M against external same-good offset e, write size = (1+e)*(M-1). A factor below zero clamps demand to 0. Freeze unsupported goods at M=1 in both transfers and accounting. | Measured on HUN nobles: legumes +0.5/+0.5 -> x2.0; sugar +0.25/-0.75 -> x0.5; fish -1.5 -> removed; wheat per-good 0.5, global 0.5, local 1.0 -> x4.5. Per-good M therefore multiplies cleanly with SOL local_pop_demand. Age 1 goods_demand offsets did not appear in the breakdown in this test; read e from the actual breakdown rather than age files. |
| modifier | location | Deleting or bypassing the calibrated SOL demand_add baseline when moving runtime demand to local_pop_demand | Keep data/target_demand.csv -> src/stable/in_game/common/goods/z_SOL_pop_goods.txt as the corrected demand_add baseline; apply local_pop_demand only as the monthly location-level multiplier. | The SOL multiplier is calibrated against corrected demand_add values. Removing that baseline changes absolute pop demand before the multiplier is applied. |
| scope | global | Using a market center location variable as a SOL market-keyed cache | Use global_variable_map keyed by the market scope instead. Script: remove_from_global_variable_map = { name = sol_market_* key = scope:sol_market_cache } then add_to_global_variable_map = { name = sol_market_* key = scope:sol_market_cache value = ... }. GUI: GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap('sol_market_*', Location.GetMarket.MakeScope). | SOL market data belongs to the market key, not to an arbitrary center location. Center-location caches are brittle and obscure the actual key. |
| modifier | pop | Restoring v1.1 substitute groups as a direct sum of group demands or calling normalized weights a conserved quantity or expenditure budget | Read the final demand blocks at commit 9670e141: all 11 overlapping goods add two group multipliers and divide by 2 before the engine applies the per-pop-type goods baseline. Preserve this distinction from any new budget-conserving design. | v1.1 has 20 groups, 67 memberships and 56 goods including optional victuals. The current 56-good core replaces victuals with camels. Legacy UI grouping comments and formula headers omit parts of the final runtime calculation. |
| scope | country | Describing the country demand solver's control flow by reading only the solver file (B_SOL_country_demand_solver.txt) or only the effect that performs the work, and presenting the raw-error prefilter as if it were the first gate | Read the dispatching effect sol_solve_country_pop_demand in A_SOL_economy_effects.txt top to bottom before describing when the solver runs. Its if/else_if/else chain applies three separate gates in order, and two of them live in the aggregation file rather than the solver: (1) num_locations < 5 routes straight to sol_solve_country_pop_demand_raw_only, skipping classification entirely; (2) the CMF per-side toggle check requires at least one of that side's exact/approx toggles to be on, else raw_only; (3) only then does the solver body run, where sol_country_demand_gate_possible checks whether any raw row exceeds tolerance. Grep for 'num_locations' in the aggregation file to find size gates -- they will never appear in the solver file. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). The size prefilter is deliberately placed before everything else because num_locations is known up front while the non-empty class count only exists after the confidence-ordered classification pass has already been paid for. It is easy to miss when tracing the solver because it sits in the caller, not in the solver file, and because the solver's own gate_possible variable looks like the entry condition. Anything describing solver cost, coverage, or the size-vs-feasibility relationship is wrong without it: it is also the direct engineering consequence of the 1-9 location bucket's 10.1% feasibility rate, so a write-up citing that bucket while omitting the gate leaves the reader without the reason it exists. |
| scope | country | Attributing default monthly AI cost to the entire substitution aggregate, or assuming solver/substitution toggles skip every newly added runtime cost | Trace monthly and yearly callers separately. Humans and normal-cadence AI solve monthly; reduced-frequency AI solves on its yearly anniversary with no monthly snapshot polling. Country substitution refresh precedes size/solver gates, and substitution-off still computes external spending. Auto modifiers only read cached deltas. Count scheduled refreshes and economic reads before claiming engine-time attribution. | The 2026-10-14 snapshot/static-modifier audit described an earlier implementation. The 2026-10-23 cache-only auto refactor removes that polling and grant path. Offline operation counts do not measure native demand refresh cost. |
| goods | pop | Inferring each pop's substitute groups or members from positive calibrated quantities, producing partial groups and singleton responses | Use data/pop_group_baskets.csv subscriptions. Every subscribed member must have positive net demand of at least 0.00001, and core consumption must equal the subscribed group union. Validate before codegen; average overlaps only over subscribed groups. Remove PP lumber uniformly at target level and preserve M&T positive minima after price scaling. | CAS commoners previously consumed only olives in the sugar/salt/olives group, forcing their olive response to 1 and diluting substitution. Offline commoner-only and mixed-stratum regressions with no sugar now give olives 4/3 and salt 2/3 for weights 2 and 1. Migration preserves allocated group budgets before rounding; overlapping groups share final quantities and must be rounded together. |
| scope | global | Gating heavy per-country / per-location work behind 'limit = { current_month = 1 }' inside a situation's on_monthly block — fires on every January 1 for every country at once | Use vanilla 'yearly_country_pulse' on_action instead — it fires once per country per year on each country's own anniversary date, naturally distributing load across the whole year. For globally-scoped yearly work (e.g. every_market_in_world scans), keep the situation-month-1 gate but split the work into market-hub-cached or quarterly passes. Anything that iterates every_owned_location per country per year MUST go through yearly_country_pulse, not the global situation. | Documentation-only (no regex lint). The situation's on_monthly with current_month=1 gate causes ALL countries to recompute simultaneously on Jan 1, producing a yearly frame-time spike. Vanilla yearly_country_pulse staggers per-country work because each country's pulse fires on its own creation-date anniversary, not all on Jan 1. The performance impact is O(N_countries × per_country_work) compressed into a single tick. Detected when SOL January lag report (2026-05-07) traced to 6.7M conditional checks on Jan 1 from 5-strata × 336-checks per location across 4000+ owned locations. |
| scope | country | Reusing legacy income samples as denominators after replacing HUD income with living standard. | Use sol_ls_sol_sample_<month>_* for SOL history; never read or rewrite legacy sol_ls_sample_* income samples. Show no prior-year data until a complete matching SOL year exists, and leave absent estates unsampled. | Project migration contract verified by offline scope execution. Population weighting belongs to the SOL accumulator; location net income remains a purchasing-power input. |
| gui | global | Shipping a map-mode / situation / modifier-type icon as an opaque circular medallion (glowing background, circle crop), or giving related map modes identical icons. | Vanilla map_modes/, situations/ and modifier_types/ icons are one painted object on a transparent 128x128 (64x64 for modifier_types) canvas with a ~5-8px margin; related modes reuse the main object and add a small lower-right badge (peasants -> peasants_percentage, control -> missing_control). Prefer a hand-drawn SVG rendered to transparent PNG (assets/icons/sol_living_standard.svg -> scripts/render_sol_icon.py); image models rarely hit the vanilla style. Install the set with scripts/compose_sol_mapmode_icons.py. | Vanilla stability.dds is already a balance scale, so a scale also collides semantically. Plain RGB-distance keying leaves a magenta halo on soft dark shadows; estimate alpha from key-ness min(R,B)-G instead. |

## Valid Enum Values

| Enum | Valid Values | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| location_rank | rural_settlement, town, city, megalopolis | Current EU5 1.x values. |
| government_type | monarchy, republic, theocracy, tribe, steppe_horde | Exact government type IDs. |

## Quick Reference Rules

- **3-Step Rule**: Direct edit only if 100% certain → `reference_official_defines/` → `reference_game_files/` + `reference_mods/`
- **Mandatory Step 2/3** for: `blockoverride` names, `custom_tooltip` keys, `situation_card_common` structure, `location_rank:*`, modifier names, scripted triggers/effects, localization YAML encoding, GUI expressions
- **Verification format**: output `**Verification** — Step [2/3], Reference: file:line, Quote: "exact text"` before any code
- **Bug fix rule**: Replace syntax with verified form; do NOT remove the feature
- **Pre-test**: Run `$env:PYTHONUTF8='1'; & $env:EU5_PYTHON scripts/validate.py --changed` before launching game
- **Float precision**: EU5 truncates all floats at 5 decimal places; never emit 6+ dp in mod files

## Symbol Quick-Reference

> Read `data/index/<name>.txt` for the full list (one entry per line).  
> For icon availability, check `data/index/icons.txt` before using `@icon_name!` inline syntax.

| Category | Count | Index file | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GUI Icons (`@xxx!`) | 381 | `data/index/icons.txt` | Use `@name!` in raw_text/text fields and YAML values |
| Scripted Triggers | 567 | `data/index/scripted_triggers.txt` | Verify name before using in `trigger = { ... }` |
| Scripted Effects | 852 | `data/index/scripted_effects.txt` | Verify name before using in `effect = { ... }` |
| Static Modifiers | 2659 | `data/index/static_modifiers.txt` | Modifier name whitelist; validate.py checks these |
| English Loc Keys | 788 | `data/index/loc_keys_en.txt` | All defined EN keys; cross-check before adding duplicates |

| Script | Input(s) | Output(s) | When to run |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `scripts/gen_sol_lshud.py` | SOL four-section source + SOL history/ranking/map emitters + vendored Glorp UI types | stable/standalone HUD, Glorp offset shim, four map modes, scoped map tooltips, font icon registration/asset, bilingual localization and ranking caches | Run by gen_sol_chain; supports --check; stable must load after Glorp UI; verify with test_sol_lshud_income/ranking/ui.py and test_sol_map_modes.py |
| `scripts/gen_sol_substitution.py` | goods_weights.csv + pop_group_baskets.csv + substitute_groups.json + calibrated demand | country pulse effects, guarded cache/display values, triggers and 56 auto modifiers | Run by gen_sol_chain; edit sol_substitution.py, regenerate, then run test_sol_substitution.py |
| `scripts/gen_whole_group_baskets.py` | pop_group_baskets.csv + goods_weights.csv + calibrated demand | whole-group and emittable-net checks; one-time budget-preserving target_demand.csv migration | --check runs first in gen_sol_chain; --fill migrates incomplete baskets once |
| `scripts/update_mod_version.py` | local date or explicit --date | selected src/<target>/.metadata/metadata.json version fields | Run automatically by build.bat before deployment; supports --target and --check |
| `scripts/gen_sol_chain.py` | SOL demand data + vanilla/PP/M&T/JTG goods + Glorp UI | active SOL generated files for all deploy targets | Preferred one-shot SOL generation entry; build.bat runs it automatically |
| `scripts/gen_sol_messages.py` | SOL resolution message definition | stable/standalone main_menu/gui/messagetypes/SOL_messages.txt (SOL entries only) | Run by gen_sol_chain; supports --check; removes its obsolete full-copy database for EU5 1.4.3 |
| `scripts/gen_sol_pp_compat.py` | stable SOL outputs + PP definitions | SOL-PP compact feature, demand, effects, and GUI overrides; validates PP road-price filename ownership | After changing SOL balance/demand/effects/UI or updating the PP reference; run by gen_sol_chain |
| `scripts/gen_sol_mnt_compat.py` | data/target_demand.csv + stable SOL + M&T definitions | SOL-M&T goods, accounting, EPBM, CMM residual cleanup, actions, and location GUI | After changing SOL demand/effects/UI or updating the M&T reference; run by gen_sol_chain |
| `scripts/gen_sol_jtg_compat.py` | data/jtg_pop_demand.csv + stable SOL outputs | SOL-JTG demand, values, effects, and 82-good GUI overrides | After changing JTG demand data or updating the Just Goods Workshop reference; run by gen_sol_chain |
| `scripts/sync_location_window.py` | Glorp UI location window + extracted vanilla types | Current Glorp UI-based stable window with optional Construction Manager references, one SOL tooltip button, synchronized type support, and backward-compatible 1.4 API migration | After updating the Glorp UI reference; run by gen_sol_chain for stable/all |
| `scripts/gen_sol_economy_effects.py` | scripts/sol_economy_effects_source.py + scripts/sol_country_demand_solver_source.py + scripts/sol_living_standard_source.py + data/demand_price_table.csv | economy/solver effects, annual SOL values, flat/percent modifier types, icons, 17 building injections, migration/growth static modifiers and bilingual names | After changing SOL economy emitters, country solver emitters, or demand data; run by gen_sol_chain for stable|sol_standalone|all |
| `scripts/gen_pop_goods.py` | data/target_demand.csv | src/<target>/.../z_SOL_pop_goods.txt | After editing calibrated demand_add baseline; supports --target stable|sol_standalone|all |
| `scripts/gen_demand_csv.py` | z_SOL_pop_goods.txt + vanilla goods | data/demand_price_table.csv | After gen_pop_goods.py |
| `scripts/gen_market_unit_consumption.py` | data/demand_price_table.csv | src/<target>/.../SOL_market_unit_consumption_values.txt | After gen_demand_csv.py or SOL market base-spending logic changes; supports --target stable|sol_standalone|all; market refresh block is embedded by gen_sol_economy_effects |
| `scripts/generate_sol_location_window.py` | vanilla location_window.gui | src/sol_standalone/in_game/gui/location_window.gui | After vanilla location_window.gui changes; run by gen_sol_chain for sol_standalone/all |
| `scripts/gen_brief.py` | anti_patterns.yaml + valid_enums.yaml + PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md | docs/knowledge/BRIEF.md | After editing any YAML knowledge file |
| `scripts/gen_index.py` | reference_game_files + src/ | data/index/*.txt symbol indexes | Run by gen_brief.py automatically |
| `scripts/gen_scaffold.py` | --type argument | Scaffold .txt/.yml file in specified directory | When creating a new EU5 file |
| `scripts/validate.py` | src/ mod files + knowledge YAML | Console report (exit 0/1) | Before launching game; --changed for quick check |
